D ​ E DE -type little strings from glued brane webs
Sung-Soo Kim, Yuji Sugimoto, Xing-Yue Wei, Futoshi Yagi

Abstract
We propose brane web configurations for D D -type and E E -type 𝒩 = ( 1 , 0 ) \mathcal{N}=(1,0) little string theories based on a trivalent or quadrivalent gluing of 5-brane web diagrams. Tri-/quadri-valent gluing is a powerful way of computing 5d/6d partition functions for supersymmetric gauge theories based on the topological vertex. We generalize the gluing techniques to little string theories by introducing a new compact direction and compute their supersymmetric partition functions on Omega-deformed ℝ 4 × T 2 \mathbb{R}^{4}\times T^{2} . As concrete examples, we consider little string theories arising from Type IIB NS5-branes probing D 4 D_{4} or D 5 D_{5} singularity. Their effective gauge theory descriptions as the affine D 4 D_{4} or D 5 D_{5} quiver gauge theory can be realized with quadrivalent or trivalent gluing, respectively. Based on these gluings of 5-brane webs, we compute their refined partition functions and compare them with the known results. We extend the computation of the partition function to little string theory engineered from IIB NS5-branes probing E 6 E_{6} singularity based on a trivalent gluing. We also discuss the generalization to higher rank cases and the symmetries of the partition functions.



1
Introduction

The supersymmetric partition function provides a powerful way of understanding BPS spectra of higher dimensional field theories. Various computational tools for computing the supersymmetric partition functions on
\mathbb{R}^{4}\times S^{1}
for 5d theories or
\mathbb{R}^{4}\times T^{2}
for 6d theories have been developed, in particular, for theories of eight supercharges, such as 5d/6d superconformal theories (SCFTs) as well as little string theories (LSTs). These partition functions are the Witten indices on the so-called
\Omega
-deformed
\mathbb{R}^{4}
counting the BPS states which are BPS spectrum protected by supersymmetry and also weighted by electric charges and angular momenta.

The computational tools include the ADHM construction of the instanton moduli space
Atiyah:1978ri
;
Nekrasov:2002qd
;
Nekrasov:2003rj
;
Marino:2004cn
;
Nekrasov:2004vw
;
Fucito:2004gi
;
Hwang:2014uwa
. This leads to Nekrasov instanton partition functions for 5d
\mathcal{N}=1
gauge theories in the Omega background. The partition functions count the BPS bound states of instantons on the Coulomb branch of the moduli space. A systematic way of computation has been developed for gauge theories with the classical gauge groups, but there are some limitations for those theories with hypermultiplets of higher dimensional representations or with higher Chern-Simons levels,
or with exceptional gauge groups. The ADHM construction is also applicable for 6d SCFTs, computing the elliptic genera of the self-dual strings in 6d SCFTs and LSTs in the tensor branch
Haghighat:2013tka
;
Haghighat:2013gba
;
Kim:2014dza
;
Haghighat:2014vxa
;
Gadde:2015tra
;
Kim:2015gha
;
Kim:2016foj
;
Kim:2017xan
;
Haghighat:2017vch
;
Kim:2018gak
;
Kim:2018gjo
;
Haghighat:2018dwe
.

The blowup method
Nakajima:2003pg
;
Nakajima:2005fg
;
Gottsche:2006bm
is a systematic and very powerful tool for computing the partition function where the BPS partition function is obtained by solving the blowup equation which identifies the partition functions on
\Omega
-deformed
\mathbb{C}^{2}
and that on the blown-up
\mathbb{P}^{1}
in
\hat{\mathbb{C}}^{2}
that can be smoothly blown-down to
\mathbb{C}^{2}
. The blowup method was generalized and extended to any 5d/6d gauge theory, quiver gauge theory, and 6d theory on a circle with/without a twist
Keller:2012da
;
Kim:2019uqw
;
Kim:2020hhh
;
Kim:2021gyj
as well as extended
Gu:2017ccq
;
Huang:2017mis
;
Kim:2020hhh
to any local Calabi-Yau 3-fold associated with geometric constructions of
Jefferson:2018irk
;
Bhardwaj:2019fzv
.

Topological vertex method
Aganagic:2003db
;
Iqbal:2007ii
;
Awata:2008ed
is yet another systematic tool for computing the partition function. It uses Type IIB 5-brane web diagrams
Aharony:1997ju
;
Aharony:1997bh
and the partition functions are expressed as a sum of instanton contributions, that is a sum of Young diagrams associated with Kähler parameter for instanton fugacity. If a 5-brane web diagram for the supersymmetric field theory of interest is given, one can perform the topological vertex to obtain the corresponding partition function. Here, 5-brane webs used for topological vertex refer to not only toric web diagrams but also a particular class of non-toric ones which include generalized toric web diagrams and 5-brane webs with orientifolds (O5- or ON-planes)
Kim:2017jqn
;
Hayashi:2020hhb
;
Nawata:2021dlk
;
Kim:2022dbr
.

It is worthy of noting that new 5-brane configurations have been obtained, including webs for 5d
G_{2}
gauge theories
Hayashi:2018bkd
;
Hayashi:2018lyv
, 5d gauge theories with rank-3 antisymmetric hypermultiplets
Hayashi:2019yxj
, local
\mathbb{P}^{2}
with an adjoint hyper or similar non-Lagrangian theories with an O7
+
-plane
Kim:2020hhh
, and also periodic webs for 6d SCFTs of spiral shapes of E-string theory
Kim:2015jba
;
Hayashi:2015fsa
and two O5-planes for those in the Higgsing and twisting sequences of 6d
D_{N}
gauge theories
Kim:2019dqn
;
Kim:2021cua
. There are, however, still many theories whose 5-brane webs are unknown, for instance, 5d SU(3)
8
theory. For such theories, conventional topological vertex method is not applicable. On the other hand, recently, new 5-brane web configurations for 5d and 6d SCFTs were proposed based on gluings of 5-brane webs in a trivalent or quadrivalent way, which is not realized on a two-dimensional
(p,q)
-plane but is extended to an ambient space such that theories are realized in a way similar to Dynkin diagrams for gauge groups of 5d gauge theories or quiver gauge theories of the shape of an affine Dynkin diagram for 6d conformal matters, and more with Higgsing of these two. We refer to them as the tri-/quadri-valent gluings
Hayashi:2017jze
;
Hayashi:2021pcj
. These new 5-brane web configurations do not require orientifold planes but can realize much more 5d/6d SCFTs than conventional 5-brane webs on the
(p,q)
-plane can do, which includes 5d exceptional gauge theories or 6d conformal matter of exceptional symmetries. The computation of the partition functions based on these tri-/quadri-valent gluings also agrees with the known result. It is however an extension of such gluings to little string theories not yet developed.

Little string theories are non-gravitational string theories in six dimensions
Seiberg:1997zk
;
Berkooz:1997cq
;
Losev:1997hx
;
Dijkgraaf:1996cv
;
Dijkgraaf:1996hk
, which arise in the limit of the string coupling
g_{s}\to 0

Seiberg:1997zk
;
Berkooz:1997cq
;
Dijkgraaf:1996cv
;
Dijkgraaf:1996hk
;
Blum:1997mm
;
Intriligator:1997dh
;
Brunner:1997gf
;
Hanany:1997gh
and have a scale associated with
1/\sqrt{\alpha^{\prime}}
or the tensions of little strings. A large class of LSTs has been constructed from F-theory on an elliptically fibered non-compact Calabi-Yau threefolds
Bershadsky:1996nu
;
Aspinwall:1996vc
;
Aspinwall:1997ye
;
Bhardwaj:2015oru
, and the classification of LSTs is discussed in
Bhardwaj:2015oru
;
Bhardwaj:2019hhd
. LSTs can be regarded as an affine extension of 6d SCFTs. In other words, on tensor branch, one can realize a certain class of LSTs as 6d quiver gauge theories of the shape of affine Dynkin diagrams. For a necklace and a
D
-type affine quiver cases, one can depict them with a conventional
(p,q)
5-brane web. A necklace quiver is for
A_{N}
-type LSTs and the corresponding 5-brane webs have two periodic directions from which T-duality of LSTs can be realized an
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
symmetry of 5-brane web with two periodicities
Kim:2015gha
. 5-brane webs for
A
-type LSTs are studied from the perspective of triality and for computing the partition functions
Hohenegger:2015btj
;
Hohenegger:2016eqy
;
Hohenegger:2016yuv
;
Bastian:2017ing
;
Bastian:2017ary
;
Bastian:2018dfu
. For
D
-type LSTs, it is also possible to study them with 5-brane webs of
two ON-branes
Sen:1997kz
;
Sen:1998ii
;
Kapustin:1998fa
;
Hanany:1999sj
, which describes an affine
D
-type Dynkin quiver with a periodic direction with NS5-branes. T-duality is realized again as the S-dual relation between 5-brane webs with two ON-planes and with two O5-planes.
E
-type LSTs still remain as a challenge for conventional
(p,q)
5-brane webs.

In this paper, we explore a possible extension of tri-/quadri-valent gluing to 6d little string theories, which makes it possible to describe LSTs engineered from
k
IIB NS5-branes probing
D_{N},E_{N}
-type singularities as glued 5-brane webs. (For simplicity, we refer to them as rank-
k

D_{N},E_{N}
-type LSTs.) Tri-/quadri-valent gluings of 6d (
G
,
G
) conformal matters realize the 6d SCFTs as a quiver theory of the shape of affine Dynkin diagram associated with symmetry group
G
, where each node corresponds to a usual 5-brane web. Notice that the glued branes, therefore, have external 5-branes which are extended to infinity. So, it is not good for describing LSTs. To make the tri-/quadri-valent gluing to describe LSTs, we propose the following prescription. We further glue the external 5-branes such that two external 5-branes that are a part of a single strip diagram are glued together as if they are compactified on a circle. All the external 5-branes are glued in this bivalent way but are required to be compactified on the
same
circle. In this way, we can introduce another periodicity for the resultant glued webs. In other words, the periodicity, which is necessary for a 5d affine quiver to be LSTs, comes from compactifying all the external 5-branes from a tri-/quadri-valent glued configuration. The resulting 5-brane configurations are hence an affine
D
-, and
E
-type quiver equipped with a periodic direction, and therefore they are 5-brane configurations that can describe a certain class of LSTs associated with affine
D,E
-extensions of 6d SCFTs. We then apply the refined topological vertex method to our proposed webs to compute the supersymmetric partition functions for LSTs. As illustrative examples, we explicitly compute the quadrivalent gluing for
D_{4}
-type little string theory and the trivalent gluing for
D_{5}
-type little string theory. We also compute the partition function for
E_{6}
-type LST based on the trivalent gluing. We then discuss the symmetry of the theories on tensor branch in connection with Weyl reflection symmetry associated with external NS-charged 5-branes, and based on the Weyl symmetry we expand the partition functions of
D_{4}
-,
D_{5}
- and
E_{6}
-type LSTs. Our results for
D_{4}
-,
D_{5}
-type LSTs reproduce the known elliptic genus results
Kim:2017xan
for these two theories obtained based on the ADHM construction where T-duality was checked, implying our proposal is also of the T-duality in it. It is worth noting that our computation based on tri-/quadri-valent glugings is performed in a different expansion compared to
Kim:2017xan
, which manifests global symmetry of the theories.

The organization of the paper is as follows. In section
2
, we provide a short review of the topological vertex method and discuss our setup for conventions and notations.
In section
3
, we discuss our proposal for tri-/quadri-valent gluings for rank-
1

D
-type and
E
-type LSTs. We then propose a general expression for the partition function of a
D_{N}
- or
E_{N}
-type LST with general rank which can be constructed from tri-/quadri- valent gluings in section
4
. Detailed computations and symmetries are discussed in section
5
. We summarize our results and possible further direction to pursue in section
6
. In appendices, we discuss some of the computational details, and we also include known results of
A
-type little string theories from the literature.

2
Topological vertex and gluings 5-brane webs

We start with a short review on topological vertex formalism
Aganagic:2003db
;
Iqbal:2007ii
and then discuss tri-/quadri-valent gluings
Hayashi:2017jze
;
Hayashi:2021pcj
. We then discuss an interesting relation between trivalent gluing and 5-brane webs with ON-planes.

2.1
Refined topological vertex

The topological vertex is our main tool for computing the partition function of 5d
\mathcal{N}=1
supersymmetric gauge theories on a circle or 6d
\mathcal{N}=(1,0)
theories on a torus based on Type IIB 5-brane webs. The partition function captures the BPS spectrum of these theories on
\mathbb{R}^{4}\times S^{1}
or
\mathbb{R}^{4}\times T^{2}
, which is the Witten index on the
\Omega
-deformed
\mathbb{R}^{4}
, counting the number of the BPS states weighted by their electric charges and angular momenta. Here, we denote the
\Omega
-background parameters
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}
and define their fugacities,
\mathfrak{q}=e^{-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\epsilon_{1}}
and
\mathfrak{t}=e^{\boldsymbol{\beta}\epsilon_{2}}
, where
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
is the circumference of
S^{1}
.

To perform the computation of the partition function using the topological vertex, one needs to identify the edge factors and the vertex factors for a given 5-brane web.
First, an edge factor is given to each edge in the 5-brane web, and likewise a vertex factor is associated with each vertex in the 5-brane web. To give the edge factor, one needs to distinguish the framing factors along either with the preferred direction
f_{\mu}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})
or with the non-preferred directions
\tilde{f}_{\mu}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})
. The preferred direction here is the direction associated with an expansion parameter such as the instanton fugacity, while the non-preferred directions are other directions that are not a preferred direction. With a Young diagram denoted by a Greek letter
1
1

1

In this paper we use Greek letters to define the Young diagrams.

\mu
, these two framing factors take the following forms,

\displaystyle f_{\mu}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|\mu|}\mathfrak{t}^{\frac{||\mu^{t}||^{2}}{2}}\mathfrak{q}^{-\frac{||\mu||^{2}}{2}}=f_{\mu^{t}}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{t})^{-1},

(1)

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{\mu}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|\mu|}\mathfrak{t}^{\frac{||\mu^{t}||^{2}+|\mu|}{2}}\mathfrak{q}^{-\frac{||\mu||^{2}+|\mu|}{2}}=\tilde{f}_{\mu^{t}}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{t})^{-1}\ ,

(2)

where
\mu^{t}
is the transpose of the Young diagram
\mu
,
|\mu|=\sum^{\ell(\mu)}_{i=1}\mu_{i}
, and
||\mu||^{2}=\sum^{\ell(\mu)}_{i=1}\mu_{i}^{2}
. The edge factor along the edge associated with the Young diagram
\mu
is then defined as

\displaystyle

\displaystyle(-Q)^{|\mu|}f^{\mathfrak{n}}_{\mu}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\quad\text{for a preferred direction},

(3)

\displaystyle(-Q)^{|\mu|}\tilde{f}^{\mathfrak{n}}_{\mu}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\quad\text{for a non-preferred direction},

where
Q
is a Kähler parameter associated with the edge, the power
\mathfrak{n}
is given as
\mathfrak{n}=p_{\text{in}}q_{\text{out}}-p_{\text{out}}q_{\text{in}}
for a pair of incoming and outgoing charges of 5-branes,
(p_{\text{in}},q_{\text{in}})
, and
(p_{\text{out}},q_{\text{out}})
, which are connected to the edge of interest (see also figure
1
).

Figure 1:
A convention of the charge of external legs. Here we define a Kähler parameter
Q
along an internal line.

The vertex factor is defined as

\displaystyle C_{\lambda\mu\nu}(\mathfrak{t},\!\mathfrak{q})=\mathfrak{q}^{\frac{||\mu||^{2}+||\nu||^{2}}{2}}\mathfrak{t}^{-\frac{||\mu^{t}||^{2}}{2}}\tilde{Z}_{\nu}(\mathfrak{t},\!\mathfrak{q})\!\sum_{\eta}\!\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{t}}\Big)^{\!\!\frac{|\eta|+|\lambda|-|\mu|}{2}}\!\!\!s_{\lambda^{t}/\eta}(\mathfrak{t}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{q}^{-\nu})s_{\mu/\eta}(\mathfrak{q}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{t}^{-\nu^{t}}),

(4)

where the Young diagrams are chosen such that the associated arrows are all out-going.
The transpose of a Young diagram will be assigned when the arrow is opposite, and the last Young diagram
\nu
in (
4
) is the Young diagram along the preferred direction. The factor
\tilde{Z}_{\nu}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})
is defined as

\displaystyle\tilde{Z}_{\nu}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})=\prod_{i=1}^{\ell(\nu)}\prod_{j=1}^{\nu_{i}}\big(1-\mathfrak{t}^{\nu^{t}_{j}-i+1}\mathfrak{q}^{\nu_{i}-j}\big)^{-1},

(5)

and
s_{\lambda/\eta}(\bf{x})
are the skew-Schur functions of a vector
\bf{x}
with infinite components. For example,

\mathfrak{t}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{q}^{-\nu}=(\mathfrak{t}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}^{-\nu_{1}},\mathfrak{t}^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathfrak{q}^{-\nu_{2}},\cdots)
.

From them, the partition function can be obtained by summing over all the edge and the vertex factors of the 5-brane web of interest,

\displaystyle Z=\sum_{\lambda,\mu,\nu,\cdots}\Big(\prod\text{Edge factor}\Big)\Big(\prod\text{Vertex factor}\Big)\ .

(6)

Note that in usual toric diagrams, we can evaluate the sum of Young diagrams along the non-preferred direction and get closed forms which may be interpreted as contributions coming from vector multiplets and hypermultiplets. Eventually, the partition function can be expressed as a sum of the Young diagrams along the preferred direction.

It is convenient to introduce the following functions:

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\lambda\mu}(Q;\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle\equiv\!\prod_{i,j=1}^{\infty}\!\!\Big(\!1\!-\!Q\mathfrak{t}^{i-\frac{1}{2}-\lambda_{j}}\mathfrak{q}^{j-\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{i}}\!\Big)\!=\!\mathcal{M}\big(Q\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q}\big)^{-1}\mathcal{N}_{\lambda^{t}\mu}\big(Q\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q}\big),

(7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(Q;\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle\equiv\!\prod_{i,j=1}^{\infty}\big(1-Q\mathfrak{t}^{i-1}\mathfrak{q}^{j}\big)^{-1},

(8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\lambda\mu}(Q;\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle\equiv\!\prod_{i,j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1-Q\mathfrak{t}^{i-1-\lambda_{j}^{t}}\mathfrak{q}^{j-\mu_{i}}}{1-Q\mathfrak{t}^{i-1}\mathfrak{q}^{j}}

(9)

\displaystyle=\!\prod_{(i,j)\in\lambda}(1-Q\mathfrak{t}^{\mu_{j}^{t}-i}\mathfrak{q}^{\lambda_{i}-j+1})\prod_{(i,j)\in\mu}(1-Q\mathfrak{t}^{-\lambda_{j}^{t}+i-1}\mathfrak{q}^{-\mu_{i}+j})\ .

With these functions, one can simplify or evaluate the Young diagram sums by applying the Cauchy identities,

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda}Q^{|\lambda|}s_{\lambda/\eta_{1}}(\mathfrak{t}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{q}^{-\nu_{1}})s_{\lambda/\eta_{2}}(\mathfrak{q}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{t}^{-\nu_{2}})

\displaystyle\quad=\mathcal{R}_{\nu_{2}\nu_{1}}(Q;\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})^{-1}\sum_{\lambda}Q^{|\eta_{1}|+|\eta_{2}|-|\lambda|}s_{\eta_{2}/\lambda}(\mathfrak{t}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{q}^{-\nu_{1}})s_{\eta_{1}/\lambda}(\mathfrak{q}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{t}^{-\nu_{2}})\ ,

(10)

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda}Q^{|\lambda|}s_{\lambda/\eta_{1}^{t}}(\mathfrak{t}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{q}^{-\nu_{1}})s_{\lambda^{t}/\eta_{2}}(\mathfrak{q}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{t}^{-\nu_{2}})

\displaystyle\quad=\mathcal{R}_{\nu_{2}\nu_{1}}(-Q;\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\sum_{\lambda}Q^{|\eta_{1}|+|\eta_{2}|-|\lambda|}s_{\eta_{2}^{t}/\lambda}(\mathfrak{t}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{q}^{-\nu_{1}})s_{\eta_{1}/\lambda^{t}}(\mathfrak{q}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{t}^{-\nu_{2}})\ .

(11)

It is also useful to implement an extended version of the Cauchy identities:

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda}Q^{|\lambda|}s_{\lambda/\mu_{1}}(Q_{1}\mathfrak{t}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{q}^{-\nu_{1}})s_{\lambda^{t}/\mu_{2}}(Q_{2}\mathfrak{q}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{t}^{-\nu_{2}})

\displaystyle~=\mathcal{R}_{\nu_{2}\nu_{1}}(-QQ_{1}Q_{2})\sum_{\lambda}Q^{|\lambda|}s_{\mu_{2}^{t}/\lambda}(QQ_{1}\mathfrak{t}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{q}^{-\nu_{1}})s_{\mu_{1}^{t}/\lambda^{t}}(QQ_{2}\mathfrak{q}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{t}^{-\nu_{2}})\ ,

(12)

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda}Q^{|\lambda|}s_{\lambda/\mu_{1}}(Q_{1}\mathfrak{t}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{q}^{-\nu_{1}})s_{\lambda/\mu_{2}}(Q_{2}\mathfrak{q}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{t}^{-\nu_{2}})

\displaystyle~=\mathcal{R}_{\nu_{2}\nu_{1}}(QQ_{1}Q_{2})^{-1}\sum_{\lambda}Q^{|\lambda|}s_{\mu_{2}/\lambda}(QQ_{1}\mathfrak{t}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{q}^{-\nu_{1}})s_{\mu_{1}/\lambda}(QQ_{2}\mathfrak{q}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{t}^{-\nu_{2}})\ .

(13)

\mathfrak{t}
and the one below is
\mathfrak{q}

2.2
Tri-/quadri-valent gluing of 5-brane webs

A trivalent or quadrivalent gluing is a 5-brane web-like construction realizing a geometric description of a given theory
Hayashi:2017jze
. Its building block is 5-brane webs of parallel external edges, such as SU(2)
2π
or a 5-brane web for pure SU(
N
)
κ
theory at the Chern-Simons level
\kappa=N
, denoted by SU(
N
)
N
, or those of fundamental hypermultiplets. Based on the topological vertex formalism, one can compute the partition function by gluing 5-brane webs. Here, the gluing means to take the Young diagram sums along the parallel external edges, which makes a gauging of the external edges and hence increase the number of Coulomb branch. We will explain the tri-/quadri-valent gluing diagrammatically, and the detailed computation will be explained in Section
3
as applications of LSTs.

The simplest gluing would be bivalent gluing which is to glue a pair of 5-brane webs with parallel external edges. It is, in fact, a commonly used technique in the topological vertex formalism to compute partition functions from 5-brane webs. For instance, consider a 5-brane web for the 5d
{\rm SU}(2)_{\pi}\times{\rm SU}(2)\times{\rm SU}(2)_{\pi}
theory. The partition function can be computed from two 5-brane webs for SU(2) theory with two flavors, by gluing two sets of parallel edges which are two flavors of each SU(2) theory. We call such gluing involving two sets of parallel edges the bivalent gluing.

As a generalization, three (and four) sets of parallel edges can be glued together. This gluing is called trivalent (and quadrivalent) gluing. Unlike two sets of parallel edges, gluing of more than three sets of parallel edges may not be depicted on a 5-brane plane. Many 5d/6d examples of the tri-/quadri-valent gluing technique have been discussed in
Hayashi:2017jze
;
Hayashi:2021pcj
, supporting that the gluing technique is another powerful way of computing the partition function especially for those theories which do not have a 5-brane realization yet, which includes 5d
F_{4},E_{6,7,8}
exceptional gauge theories and 6d
(E_{N},E_{N})
conformal matters. We note that multivalent gluings of more than four sets parallel edges do not lead to a physically sensible theory.

Figure 3:
A bivalent gluing.

We briefly demonstrate these gluing techniques with 5d SU(
2
) theory with an even number of hypermultiplets in the fundamental representation (flavors).
First, consider a 5-brane web with 4 flavors given in figure
3
, in which we can vertically cut the 5-brane web by half, leading to two pieces of 5-brane web representing a free hypermultiplet. One can obviously glue together these free hypermultiplets to reconstruct a 5-brane web for 5d SU(2) gauge theory with 4 flavors, which is, in fact, what we usually do. We here note that one also represents two flavors as an “SU(1)” theory, as shown in figure
4
.
This means that we have a quiver gauge theory SU(1)
\,-\,
SU(2)
\,-\,
SU(1) which is a
D
-type quiver.

\mathbf{F}
(left) and
{\rm SU}(1)-{\rm SU}(2)-{\rm SU}(1)
(right) by vertical Hanany-Witten moves, where the blue dots are (0,1) 7-branes.

.

\mathbf{F}

-

Figure 6:
SU(2)+6
\mathbf{F}
as a trivalent gluing configuration discussed in (
14
).

For a trivalent quiver with three SU(1)s, we can do a trivalent gluing of three SU(1)+2
\mathbf{F}
where parallel external 5-branes associated with two flavors are glued. In other words, three 2
\mathbf{F}
are gauged to become an SU(2) node in the middle of the trivalent quiver (
14
). One can show that the partition function based on this trivalent gluing correctly reproduces that of SU(2)
+6\mathbf{F}
.

Figure 7:
A quadrivalent gluing for SU(2)
+8\mathbf{F}
.

We can even consider a 5-brane web for SU(2) gauge theory with 8 flavors by introducing one more SU(1)
+2\mathbf{F}
,
which makes a quadrivalent quiver, as shown in figure
7
.
As discussed,
SU(1)-SU(2)-SU(1) of
D
-type quiver can be depicted as a 5-brane web with an ON-plane in figure
5
(a).
The quadrivalent quiver for SU(2)
+8\mathbf{F}
having four SU(1)’s can also be realized in a similar way, but in the quadrivalent case we need to put two ON-planes. It is worth noting that topological vertex computation based on a 5-brane web with ON-plane(s) was recently developed in
Kim:2022dbr
. We do not provide the computation detail, but we report that the partition function computed by using the refined topological vertex method based on this quadrivalent quiver for SU(2)
+8\mathbf{F}
perfectly agrees with that based on a 5-brane web with two ON-planes obtained in
Kim:2022dbr
.

We remark that the 5-brane web with two ON-planes has a periodic direction so that this corresponds to a 6d theory on a circle. In this case, it is the 6d E-string theory on a circle which is represented by an affine
D_{4}
quiver theory in 5d description and hence can be realized as a quadrivalent gluing.
In other words, a quadrivalent gluing gives rise to affine
D_{4}
quiver in the 5d description, representing the 6d theory on a circle.

We also note that the equivalence between tri-/quadri-valent gluing and 5-brane web with ON-plane(s) can be readily generalized to (affine)
D
-type quiver gauge theories with more than one Coulomb branch parameters. For instance, 6d
(D_{N},D_{N})
conformal matter can be realized on a 5-brane with two ON-planes as an affine
D_{N\geq 5}
quiver or as two trivalent gluings of the shape of affine
D_{N\geq 5}
quiver. One can compute the corresponding partition function based on either a 5-brane web with two ON-planes or trivalent gluings. The parameter map between these two cases can be easily read off as a simple generalization of the map given in figure
5
.

3
Little string theory from bivalent self-gluings

In this section, we propose a novel 5-brane configuration for a certain class of
\mathcal{N}=(1,0)
LSTs. In conventional Type IIB 5-brane construction, some LSTs are realized on 5-brane webs that have two periodic structures where parallel external edges are identified or two same kinds of orientifolds are presented
Brunner:1997gf
;
Hanany:1997gh
. As a result, 5-brane configurations describing LSTs have no external edges instead they make compact directions vertically and horizontally. T-duality of LSTs can be understood as an S-duality of such a 5-brane web. If one decompactifies one of these compact directions, one gets a 6d SCFT. Conversely, for a given 5-brane web for a 6d SCFT, one can identify parallel external edges to make a 5-brane configuration for 6d LSTs.

We extend this logic to tri-/quadri-valently glued 5-brane webs describing 6d SCFTs. A trivalent or quadrivalent gluing making a quiver of the shape of an affine Dynkin diagram (affine quiver) gives rise to a 5d realization of a 6d theory with a global symmetry associated with the Dynkin diagram of the affine quiver. For such 5-brane configuration to describe certain LSTs, we further generalize this construction to include bivalent “self-gluings,” such that all the external edges in the same quiver node are glued together. This makes an additional compact direction and leaves no external edges. In this section, based on the bivalent self-gluings, we discuss how to introduce these self-gluings to 5-brane configurations and compute the partition function for 6d
\mathcal{N}=(1,0)
little string theories engineered from an NS5-brane probing
D
-type and
E
-type singularities, which we refer to as the
D
-type and
E
-type LSTs. For
D
-type LSTs, we also discuss equivalence between Type IIB 5-brane webs for LSTs and tri-/quadri-valent gluings for LSTs by presenting the Kähler parameter maps between these two brane webs.

Figure 8:

A Type IIB 5-brane configuration for the
D_{4}
-type LST which is of the shape of a circular quiver horizontally and has two O5-planes (dashed lines) vertically. (a) The resulting configuration gives rise to a 6d SO(8)-Sp(0) circular quiver. (b) A deformed brane web by a generalized flop near two O5-planes.

3.1
LST on
D_{4}
singularity

We start with the
D_{4}
-type little string theory which is the simplest
D
-type little string theory. Recall first that the
D_{4}
-type LST can be realized by a Type IIB 5-brane web diagram as depicted in figure
8
(a), which is an SO(8)-“Sp(0)” circular quiver due to the identification of the left four D5-branes with the right four D5-branes making the horizontal periodic direction, and two dashed lines in the web diagram represent two O5-planes making the vertical periodic direction. We note that the 5-brane configuration in figure
8
(a) can be deformed into the one in figure
8
(b), where we perform a “generalized flop”
Hayashi:2017btw
near two O5-planes in transition from figure
8
(a) to figure
8
(b).
We can further deform the 5-brane web in figure
8
(b) by shifting the locations of SO(8) color branes labeled with
m_{1}
further down while the one with
m_{4}
further up. We do this deformation to take an S-duality which gives rise to two ON-planes, which is known as a Type IIB 5-brane web with ON-planes describing an (affine)
D
-type quiver as shown in figure
9
(b) where two ON-planes are depicted by two vertical dashed lines.

Figure 9:
(a) A 5-brane web with two ON-planes giving rise to an affine
D_{4}
quiver associated with (
D_{4}
,
D_{4}
) conformal matter or E-string theory. (b) A 5-brane web for 6d
D_{4}
-type LST which can be obtained from tuning of NS5-branes and identifying them so that, as a result, they provide an additional periodic (vertical) direction.

Notice that
the web diagram in figure
9
(b) can also be obtained from that in figure
9
(a) which is a 5d affine
D_{4}
quiver gauge theory by the following procedure: First, we shrink the distances between pairs of the edges associated with
\mu_{1,1}
and
\mu_{2,1}
that are near the right ON-plane and the edges associated with
\mu_{3,1}
and
\mu_{4,1}
that are near the left ON-plane in figure
9
(a). We then tune the horizontal coordinates of the two ends of each of the middle two vertical strips to be the same.
After that,
we identify vertical branes by gluing the ends of each vertical strip to have an vertical periodic direction.
Therefore, 5-brane configuration in figure
9
(b) describes 6d affine
D_{4}
quiver,

SU
(2)
SU
(1)
SU
(1)
SU
(1)
SU
(1)
.

This affine structure gives rise to a periodic direction of the
D_{4}
-type LST and the other periodic direction is realized by the vertical compactification as shown in figure
9
(b).

We remark here that in figure
9
(a), the color branes associated with
\mu_{1,1}
and
\mu_{2,1}
connect to the rightmost vertical strip which leads to a bifundamental contribution that mixes
\mu_{1,1}
and
\mu_{2,1}
to the partition function, while the color branes
\mu_{1,1}
and
\mu_{2,1}
also connect to the second right vertical strip which leads to a vector contribution that also mixes
\mu_{1,1}
and
\mu_{2,1}
to the partition function, and these two contributions, in fact, cancel each other so that there are no factors that mix
\mu_{1,1}
and
\mu_{2,1}

Kim:2022dbr
;
Bourgine:2017rik
, as expected from a
D
-type quiver theory.
The same analysis can be applied to the bifundamental and vector contributions from
\mu_{3,1}
and
\mu_{4,1}
. As a result, one can factorize the partition function into five parts that correspond to each of the five gauge nodes, which turns out to be equivalent to the computation procedure from the quadrivalent gluing method.

We expect that the cancellations of the bifundamental and vector contributions that mix
\mu_{1,1}
with
\mu_{2,1}
and
\mu_{3,1}
with
\mu_{4,1}
also happen for the LST in figure
9
(b) which can be understood as the following. Due to the identification of external edges the vertical strips are now periodic and we can use the method in
Haghighat:2013gba
to compute such periodic strips which we summarized in Appendix
B
. In particular, from (
B
) we know that there are infinitely many bifundamental and vectors contributions that mix
\mu_{1,1}
with
\mu_{2,1}
and
\mu_{3,1}
with
\mu_{4,1}
. But due to the brane web structure in figure
9
(a), the color brane associated with
\mu_{1,1}
always connects to the rightmost strip from the left side and the color brane associated with
\mu_{2,1}
always connects to the rightmost strip from the right side in figure
9
(b). Then from
Kim:2022dbr
,
\mu_{1,1}
and
\mu_{2,1}
belong to two SU gauge nodes respectively and we can see the cancellations of bifundamental and vector contributions that mix
\mu_{1,1}
and
\mu_{2,1}
by swapping infinitely many color branes(due to vertical periodicity) associated with
\mu_{1,1}
and
\mu_{2,1}
so that the two SU gauge nodes do not overlap. The same analysis goes for
\mu_{3,1}
and
\mu_{4,1}
. After the cancellations, there are no factors in the partition function that mix
\mu_{1,1}
with
\mu_{2,1}
and
\mu_{3,1}
with
\mu_{4,1}
which also leads to the factorization of the partition function into five parts with each part correspond to a gauge node.
This also means that the
D_{4}
-type LST, although ‘‘vertically’’ periodic
2
2

2

Here, by abuse of notation, we use the term, vertically periodic, to denote the periodicity arising from bivalent gluings of non-parallel edges and also to distinguish it from the other periodicity induced from affine quivers depicted horizontally.
compared to the E-string theory, can also be reconstructed by the quadrivalent gluing method.
Therefore, we propose that one can realize the
D_{4}
-type LST of figure
9
(b) by the quadrivalent gluing in figure
10
(a). There are several things that need to be explained about this quadrivalent gluing diagram before we explain how to realize the vertical periodicity using the bivalent self-gluing:

Figure 10:
(a) A quadrivalent gluing realization of the
D_{4}
-type LST. The four subdiagrams in the corners correspond to four SU(1) gauge nodes, the middle subdiagram corresponds to SU(2) gauge node. (b) Gluing for non-parallel edges for the diagram on the bottom right of (a). The configuration shown corresponds to a gluing associated with a double period
Q_{\tau}
.

Figure 11:
Another equivalent quadrivalent gluing realization of the
D_{4}
-type LST.

(1)

We did not explicitly show how to tri-/quadri-valently glue subdiagrams together in the previous section.
Here figure
10
(a) explicitly illustrates how to do this by also showing the subdiagram of the SU(2) gauge node in the middle of the figure, and this middle diagram looks exactly like the middle part of the ON-plane realization in figure
9
(b).
Now we have five subdiagrams in figure
10
(a).
Each subdiagram corresponds to a gauge node of the affine
D_{4}
quiver, and there are overlaps between two subdiagrams that correspond to two connected gauge nodes.

(2)

Each subdiagram in figure
10
(a) is equivalent to its
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
transformed diagram. For example, we can do an
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
transformation to make the shape of the bottom right subdiagram just like the shape of the top right subdiagram, but this should not affect the middle SU(2) subdiagram in which the top two NS-charged branes have to parallel to each other and also the bottom two NS-charged branes have to parallel to each other.

(3)

Although we draw two SU(1)
subdiagrams on the left and the other two on the right, the tri-/quadri-valent gluing are not sensitive from where subdiagrams are glued. For example, we can also draw one SU(1) subdiagram on the left and three on the right as shown in figure
11
in which the middle SU(2) subdiagram also accordingly changes.
However, by using the topological vertex formalism for the quadrivalent gluing diagrams in figure
10
(a) and figure
11
, it is easy to show that the two partition functions are the same. Here we omit the proof, but the reader can easily prove this by using the formula that we will obtain in section
4.3
.

Bivalent self-gluing.

We label the Kähler parameters and Young diagrams of figure
10
(a) in accord with figure
9
(b) and, in particular, the Young diagrams
\mu_{I,j}
label the
j
-th color brane of the
I
-th gauge node. It is obvious that figure
10
(a) has affine
D_{4}
quiver structure but it is less obvious to see the vertical periodicity from the figure. The way that one realizes the vertical periodicity is simply gluing the two end edges of each vertical strip for each subdiagram in figure
10
(a), which we already referred to as the bivalent self-gluing.

Gluing non-parallel edges are not a conventional gluing, though it was discussed for a 5-brane configuration with two O5-planes
Kim:2021cua
. So, we explain how to bivalently self-glue non-parallel lines. First, we pick the bottom right subdiagram in figure
10
(a), as an example. In figure
10
(b), we draw two adjacent periods of the bottom right subdiagram of figure
10
(a). The upper period is just like the original subdiagram in figure
10
(a), but because the two ends of each vertical strip in the bottom right subdiagram of figure
10
(a) are non-parallel, in order to draw the next lower period continuously, we have to tilt the original subdiagram by an
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
transformation which leads to the lower period shown in figure
10
(b). However, the
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
transformation does not change the Kähler parameters, so if the brane web in figure
10
(b) wants to be vertically periodic, the Kähler parameters should not change between different periods. In particular,
Q_{2}
should be equal to
Q_{2}^{\prime}
. In order to satisfy the periodicity constraint
Q_{2}=Q^{\prime}_{2}
,
Q_{2;5}
has to satisfy the condition

\displaystyle Q_{2;5}=Q_{5,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\ .

(15)

By the same reasoning, for the other three SU(1) subdiagrams, we also have

\displaystyle Q_{1;5}=Q_{3;5}=Q_{4;5}=Q_{5,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\ .

(16)

The middle SU(2) subdiagram in figure
10
(a) is automatically periodic once the periodicity conditions (
15
) and (
16
) are satisfied. We can see that the periodicity conditions of the quadrivalent gluing in figure
10
(a) are also consistent with the periodicity conditions coming from the brane web with two ON-planes in figure
9
(b). Then due to the vertical periodicity, in figure
10
(a) if we draw lines along each external NS-charged branes in each gauge node towards the center of Coulomb branch, the two intersection points will be just at the center of Coulomb branch, the distance between this two points is just the instanton factor of the gauge node, and it is just equal to the horizontal distance between the two parallel NS-charged branes of the gauge node.
So
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4},Q_{5}
are the instanton factors of the gauge node
1,2,3,4,5
respectively in the 5d theory perspective, and they are also the tensor branch parameters of the 6d
D_{4}
-type LST. The horizontal period
u
of the theory due to its affine
D_{4}
quiver structure is then given by

\displaystyle u=Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{4}Q_{5}^{2}\ .

(17)

Now we are ready to use the quadrivalent gluing diagram in figure
10
(a) to compute the partition function
Z^{D_{4}}
of
D_{4}
-type LST which takes the following summation and product form,

Z^{D_{4}}=\sum_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}Z_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}^{\text{SU(2)}}\sum_{\mu_{1,1}}Z_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{1,1}}(Q_{5,1},Q_{\tau},Q_{1})\sum_{\mu_{2,1}}Z_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{2,1}}(Q_{5,1},Q_{\tau},Q_{2})\\
\times\sum_{\mu_{3,1}}Z_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{3,1}}(Q_{5,1},Q_{\tau},Q_{3})\sum_{\mu_{4,1}}Z_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{4,1}}(Q_{5,1},Q_{\tau},Q_{4}),

(18)

in which

Z_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}^{\text{SU(2)}}\equiv(-Q_{5})^{|\mu_{5,1}|+|\mu_{5,2}|}f_{\mu_{5,1}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})f_{\mu_{5,2}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})^{-1}\times\hbox to86.05pt{\vbox to48.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 58.03452pt\lower-36.25096pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

(20)

The other summands,
Z_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{2,1}},Z_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{3,1}},Z_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{4,1}}
, are defined in the same form as
Z_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{1,1}}
with
\mu_{1,1},Q_{1}
replaced by
\mu_{2,1},Q_{2},\mu_{3,1},Q_{3},\mu_{4,1},Q_{4}
respectively as they correspond to the four SU(1) subdiagrams of figure
10
(a) which are related to each other by
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
or a mirror reflection.

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu_{1}\nu_{2}}C_{\nu_{1}\nu_{2}^{t}\mu_{5,1}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})C_{\nu_{2}\nu_{1}^{t}\mu_{5,2}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})(-Q_{\tau}Q_{5,1}^{-1})^{|\nu_{1}|}\tilde{f}_{\nu_{1}}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{t})^{-1}(-Q_{5,1})^{|\nu_{2}|}\tilde{f}_{\nu_{2}}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{t})^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\mathfrak{q}^{\frac{||\mu_{5,1}||^{2}+||\mu_{5,2}||^{2}}{2}}\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{5,2}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\sum_{\nu_{i}\eta_{i}}\left(Q_{\tau}Q_{5,1}^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}\right)^{|\nu_{1}|}\left(Q_{5,1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}\right)^{|\nu_{2}|}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{t}}\right)^{\frac{|\eta_{1}|+|\eta_{2}|}{2}}

\displaystyle\times s_{\nu_{1}^{t}/\eta_{1}}(\mathfrak{t}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{q}^{-\mu_{5,1}})s_{\nu_{2}^{t}/\eta_{1}}(\mathfrak{q}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{t}^{-\mu_{5,1}^{t}})s_{\nu_{2}^{t}/\eta_{2}}(\mathfrak{t}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{q}^{-\mu_{5,2}})s_{\nu_{1}^{t}/\eta_{2}}(\mathfrak{q}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{t}^{-\mu_{5,2}^{t}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\mathfrak{q}^{\frac{||\mu_{5,1}||^{2}+||\mu_{5,2}||^{2}}{2}}\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{5,2}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\prod_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-Q_{\tau}^{l}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\mathcal{R}_{\mu_{5,2}^{t}\mu_{5,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{5,1}^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{R}_{\mu_{5,1}^{t}\mu_{5,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{5,1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{R}_{\mu_{5,1}^{t}\mu_{5,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{R}_{\mu_{5,2}^{t}\mu_{5,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})},

(21)

where in the last equality, we used the Cauchy identities repeatedly
Haghighat:2013gba
.

3

3

See Appendix
B
for more details on how to compute the vertical periodic strip.

We can immediately obtain the other half SU(2) subdiagram based on (
21
) since they are mirror images to each other,

\mu_{5,1}
\mu_{5,2}
Q_{5,1}
Q_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{t}^{\frac{||\mu_{5,1}^{t}||^{2}+||\mu_{5,2}^{t}||^{2}}{2}}\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}^{t}}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{t})\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{5,2}^{t}}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{t})\prod_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-Q_{\tau}^{l}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\mathcal{R}_{\mu_{5,2}^{t}\mu_{5,1}}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{5,1}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{t}}})\mathcal{R}_{\mu_{5,1}^{t}\mu_{5,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{5,1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{t}}})\mathcal{R}_{\mu_{5,1}^{t}\mu_{5,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{t}}})\mathcal{R}_{\mu_{5,2}^{t}\mu_{5,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{t}}})}.

(22)

By substituting (
22
) and (
21
) into (
19
), we get

\displaystyle Z_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}^{\text{SU(2)}}=

\displaystyle\prod_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1-Q_{\tau}^{l})^{2}}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n})^{2}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})^{2}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{5,1}^{-1})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{5,1}^{-1}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{5,1})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{5,1}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\hat{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}^{\text{SU(2)}},

(23)

where

\displaystyle\hat{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}^{\text{SU(2)}}\equiv

\displaystyle~\mathfrak{t}^{||\mu_{5,1}^{t}||^{2}}\mathfrak{q}^{||\mu_{5,2}||^{2}}Q_{5}^{|\mu_{5,1}|+|\mu_{5,2}|}\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{5,2}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}^{t}}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{t})\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{5,2}^{t}}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{t})

\displaystyle\times\!\bigg(\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,2}\mu_{5,1}}\!(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{5,1}^{-1})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,2}\mu_{5,1}}\!(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{5,1}^{-1}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}\!(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{5,1})

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}\!(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{5,1}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,2}\mu_{5,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n})

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,2}\mu_{5,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\!\bigg)^{-1}\ .

(24)

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu_{4}}C_{\nu_{4}\nu_{4}^{t}\mu_{1,1}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})(-Q_{\tau})^{|\nu_{4}|}\tilde{f}_{\nu_{4}}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{t})^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}^{\frac{||\mu_{1,1}||^{2}}{2}}\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{1,1}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\prod_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-Q_{\tau}^{l}}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\mathcal{R}_{\mu_{1,1}^{t}\mu_{1,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})}\ .

(27)

By combining all results, we find

\displaystyle Z_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{1,1}}(Q_{5,1},Q_{\tau},Q_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-Q_{\tau}^{l}}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})}{\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{5,1}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})^{2}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{5,1}^{1/2}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})^{2}}\times\hat{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{1,1}}(Q_{5,1},Q_{\tau},Q_{1}),

(28)

where

\displaystyle\hat{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{1,1}}(Q_{5,1},Q_{\tau},Q_{1})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}^{\frac{||\mu_{1,1}||^{2}}{2}}\mathfrak{t}^{\frac{||\mu_{1,1}^{t}||^{2}}{2}}(-Q_{1}Q_{5,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}})^{|\mu_{1,1}|}\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{1,1}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{1,1}^{t}}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{t})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\bigg[\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{1,1}\mu_{5,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{5,1}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{1,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{5,1}^{1/2}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,2}\mu_{1,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{5,1}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{1,1}\mu_{5,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{5,1}^{1/2}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\bigg(\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{1,1}\mu_{1,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{1,1}\mu_{1,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\bigg)^{-1}\bigg]\ .

(29)

Z_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{2,1}}
,
Z_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{3,1}}
and
Z_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{4,1}}
can be easily obtained from
Z_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{1,1}}
according to their definitions.

\mathcal{N}
function to
\varTheta
function.

In order to simplify the expression of the partition function above, we replace the
\mathcal{N}
functions in (
24
) and (
29
) by Jacobi theta functions via the following formulas
4
4

4

The identities
\sum_{(i,j)\in\lambda}\lambda_{i}=||\lambda||^{2}
,
\sum_{(i,j)\in\lambda}j=\frac{1}{2}|\lambda|+\frac{1}{2}||\lambda||^{2}
,
\sum_{(i,j)\in\lambda}\mu_{j}^{t}=\sum_{(i,j)\in\mu}\lambda_{j}^{t}
are used to derive these formulas.
,
5
5

5

Similar formulas appear in
Hohenegger:2013ala
as well as
Haghighat:2013tka
.

\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\beta\alpha}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q^{-1}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{N}_{\alpha\beta}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q;\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})=\frac{(-1)^{|\beta|}(Q\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{t}}})^{\frac{|\beta|+|\alpha|}{2}}\mathfrak{t}^{\frac{||\beta^{t}||^{2}-||\alpha^{t}||^{2}}{4}}\mathfrak{q}^{\frac{-||\beta||^{2}+||\alpha||^{2}}{4}}}{\left(\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}(1-Q_{\tau}^{n})\right)^{|\beta|+|\alpha|}}\\
\times\varTheta_{\beta\alpha}(Q^{-1}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\alpha\beta}(Q|Q_{\tau})\ ,

(30)

\displaystyle\frac{\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\alpha\alpha}(Q_{\tau}^{n};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{N}_{\alpha\alpha}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})}{\tilde{Z}_{\alpha}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\tilde{Z}_{\alpha^{t}}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{t})}=\frac{\mathfrak{t}^{\frac{||\alpha^{t}||^{2}}{2}}\mathfrak{q}^{\frac{||\alpha||^{2}}{2}}}{\left(\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}(1-Q_{\tau}^{n})\right)^{2|\alpha|}}\varTheta_{\alpha\alpha}(\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\alpha\alpha}(1|Q_{\tau})\ ,

(31)

where we have defined the
\varTheta
function as follows:

\displaystyle\varTheta_{\alpha\beta}(Q|Q_{\tau})\equiv\prod_{(i,j)\in\alpha}\left((-iQ_{\tau}^{\frac{1}{8}})^{-1}\theta_{1}(Q^{-1}\mathfrak{t}^{-\beta_{j}^{t}+i}\mathfrak{q}^{-\alpha_{i}+j-1}|Q_{\tau})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{(i,j)\in\alpha}\left[\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{n}Q_{\tau}^{\frac{n(n+1)}{2}}\Big((Q^{-1}\mathfrak{t}^{-\beta_{j}^{t}+i}\mathfrak{q}^{-\alpha_{i}+j-1})^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-(Q^{-1}\mathfrak{t}^{-\beta_{j}^{t}+i}\mathfrak{q}^{-\alpha_{i}+j-1})^{-n-\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\right]\ ,

(32)

where
\theta_{1}
is the Jacobi theta function given as

\displaystyle\theta_{1}(y|Q_{\tau})=

\displaystyle i\sum_{n=-\infty}^{\infty}(-1)^{n+1}Q_{\tau}^{\frac{1}{2}(n+\frac{1}{2})^{2}}y^{n+\frac{1}{2}}=-iQ_{\tau}^{\frac{1}{8}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{n}Q_{\tau}^{\frac{n(n+1)}{2}}(y^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-y^{-n-\frac{1}{2}})\ .

(33)

Note that unlike the
\mathcal{N}_{\alpha\beta}
function in (
9
), the
\varTheta_{\alpha\beta}
function has only the product over
\alpha
in the definition (
32
).

In (
24
) and (
29
), we encounter the following kind of factor

\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\sigma\rho}\big(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}\frac{Q_{\tau}}{Q_{\Delta}}\frac{\mathfrak{t}/\mathfrak{q}}{X}\big)\,\mathcal{N}_{\rho\sigma}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{\Delta}X),

(34)

where the vertical position of the brane labeled by Young diagram
\rho
is higher than that of the brane labeled by Young diagram
\sigma
in the fundamental region
6
6

6

In figure
10
(a), the quadrivalent gluing subdiagrams are vertically periodic, we choose the particular one period shown in the figure as the fundamental region which has parallel NS-charged branes at the ends.

of the web diagram. Here,
Q_{\Delta}\equiv e^{-\boldsymbol{\beta}\,d}
is the vertical distance in Kähler parameter between the two branes in the fundamental region where
d
is the vertical distance, and
X
represents
\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}
,
\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}
, or 1.
When we apply (
30
) to (
34
), we have two choices:

\displaystyle Q_{\Delta}X\rightarrow Q\quad\text{and}\quad\rho,\sigma\rightarrow\alpha,\beta,

(35)

or

\displaystyle\frac{Q_{\tau}}{Q_{\Delta}}\frac{\mathfrak{t}/\mathfrak{q}}{X}\rightarrow Q\quad\text{and}\quad\sigma,\rho\rightarrow\alpha,\beta\ .

(36)

These two choices lead to two different expressions involving
\varTheta
functions, and we choose (
35
), which turns out to make the expression of the partition function simpler.

Expression of
Z^{D_{4}}
.

Before we write down the final expression of the partition function for the
D_{4}
-type LST, we define some new parameters and symbols so that the result can be expressed in a compact form.

In the fundamental region, let us consider the
k
-th color brane of the
I
-th gauge node whose corresponding Young diagram is denoted by
\mu_{I,k}
.
If the vertical position of the color brane is
a_{I,k}
, then we name the corresponding Kähler parameter as
y_{I,k}

y_{I,k}\equiv e^{-\boldsymbol{\beta}a_{I,k}}\ .

(37)

In terms of
y_{I,k}
, we express the periodicity conditions (
15
) and (
16
) as

\displaystyle y_{1,1}=1,\quad y_{2,1}=1,\quad y_{3,1}=1,\quad y_{4,1}=1,\quad y_{5,1}y_{5,2}=1,

(38)

where we have set the vertical position
a_{1,1}=0
.
Next we define the following symbol

{\textit{{n}}}_{I,k;J,\ell}\equiv\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}n-1,&\quad\text{if}\ a_{I,k}>a_{J,\ell},\\
n,&\quad\text{if}\ a_{I,k}<a_{J,\ell}\ \text{or}\ (I,k)=(J,\ell)\,.\end{array}\right.

(39)

By transforming
\mathcal{N}
functions to
\varTheta
functions, as well as substituting (
3.1
), (
24
), (
28
) and (
29
) into (
18
), the partition function for the
D_{4}
-type LST

Z^{D_{4}}
is expressed as

Z^{D_{4}}=Z^{D_{4}}_{\text{pert}}\cdot Z^{D_{4}}_{\text{inst}}\ ,

(40)

where

\displaystyle Z^{D_{4}}_{\text{pert}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\prod_{I=1}^{4}\frac{\mathcal{M}(\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{I,1;I,1}})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{I,1;I,1}})}{\prod_{l=1}^{2}\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{I,1}}{y_{5,\ell}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{I,1;5,l}})}\prod_{p=1}^{2}\frac{\prod_{r=1}^{2}\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{5,r}}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{5,p;5,r}})\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{5,r}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{5,p;5,r}})}{\prod_{J=1}^{4}\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{J,1}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{5,p;J,1}})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1-Q_{\tau}^{l})^{6}},

(41)

\displaystyle Z_{\text{inst}}^{D_{4}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\prod_{I=1}^{4}\bigg(Q_{I}^{|\mu_{I,1}|}\frac{\prod_{l=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{I,1}\mu_{5,l}}(\frac{y_{I,1}}{y_{5,l}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})}{\varTheta_{\mu_{I,1}\mu_{I,1}}(1|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\mu_{I,1}\mu_{I,1}}(\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{p=1}^{2}Q_{5}^{|\mu_{5,p}|}\frac{\prod_{J=1}^{4}\varTheta_{\mu_{5,p}\mu_{J,1}}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{J,1}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})}{\prod_{r=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{5,p}\mu_{5,r}}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{5,r}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\mu_{5,p}\mu_{5,r}}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{5,r}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})}\,.

(42)

We will deal with a general higher rank case of LSTs in section
4
,
where we will write the perturbative part of partition functions in terms of Plethystic exponential (PE).

3.2
LST on
D_{5}
singularity

Figure 12:
A Type IIB 5-brane web for
D_{5}
-type LST with two ON-planes.

The rank-1
D_{5}
-type LST can be realized by a web diagram with two ON-planes as depicted in figure
12
, which describes 6d affine
D_{5}
quiver,

SU
(2)
SU
(2)
SU
(1)
SU
(1)
SU
(1)
SU
(1)
.

We propose that the
D_{5}
-type LST can also be realized by trivalent gluings
7
7

7

The two SU(1) gauge nodes on the left and the right SU(2) gauge node are trivalently glued at the left SU(2) gauge node, the two SU(1) gauge nodes on the right and the left SU(2) gauge node are trivalently glued at the right SU(2) gauge node.
together with the bivalent self-gluings
as shown in figure
13
, where the
Young diagram
\mu_{I,j}
labels the
j
-th color brane of the
I
-th gauge node.

Figure 13:
A brane web for
D_{5}
-type LST as trivalent gluings. The four subdiagrams in the corners correspond to four SU(1) gauge nodes, the middle subdiagram corresponds to SU(2)-SU(2) gauge nodes.

Like the
D_{4}
-type LST case, in order to do the bivalent self-gluing consistently for each SU(1) subdiagram in figure
13
,
we impose the following conditions for vertical periodicity

\displaystyle Q_{1;5}=Q_{2;5}=Q_{5,1}^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad Q_{3;6}=Q_{4;6}=Q_{6,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\ .

(43)

For the middle SU(2)-SU(2) subdiagram in figure
13
to be vertically periodic, the following condition has to be satisfied,

\displaystyle Q_{6;5}=Q_{5,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{6,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ .

(44)

Then like in the
D_{4}
-type LST case, due to vertical periodicity
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4},Q_{5},Q_{6}
are the instanton factors of the gauge node
1,2,3,4,5,6
respectively in the 5d theory perspective, and they are also the tensor branch parameters of the 6d
D_{5}
-type LST. The horizontal period
u
of the theory due to its affine
D_{5}
quiver structure is then given by

\displaystyle u=Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{4}Q_{5}^{2}Q_{6}^{2}\ .

(45)

Using the trivalent gluing diagram in figure
13
, the partition function
Z^{D_{5}}
of
D_{5}
-type LST is given by

\displaystyle Z^{D_{5}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu_{5,1}\,\mu_{5,2}\,\mu_{6,1}\,\mu_{6,2}}Z^{\text{SU(2)-SU(2)}}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\mu_{1,1}}Z_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{1,1}}(Q_{5,1},Q_{\tau},Q_{1})\sum_{\mu_{2,1}}Z_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{2,1}}(Q_{5,1},Q_{\tau},Q_{2})

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\mu_{3,1}}Z_{\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}\mu_{3,1}}(Q_{6,1},Q_{\tau},Q_{3})\sum_{\mu_{4,1}}Z_{\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}\mu_{4,1}}(Q_{6,1},Q_{\tau},Q_{4})\ ,

(46)

in which the summands are defined as follows:

Z^{\text{SU(2)-SU(2)}}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}\equiv\left(-Q_{6}\sqrt{\frac{Q_{6,1}}{Q_{5,1}}}\right)^{|\mu_{6,1}|+|\mu_{6,2}|}(-Q_{5})^{|\mu_{5,1}|+|\mu_{5,2}|}f_{\mu_{5,1}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})f_{\mu_{5,2}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})^{-1}\\

(47)

where the edge factors of
\mu_{5,1},\mu_{5,2},\mu_{6,1},\mu_{6,2}
come from the middle subdiagram of figure
13
.
The
Z_{\mu_{\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\mu_{\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\mu_{\boldsymbol{\cdot}}}(\boldsymbol{\cdot},\boldsymbol{\cdot},\boldsymbol{\cdot})
functions in (
46
) which correspond to the four SU(1) subdiagrams of figure
13
are the same function defined in (
20
) with indices and arguments accordingly changed.

After computing the three subdiagrams in (
47
) and then substituting the results back into (
47
), we find

Z^{\text{SU(2)-SU(2)}}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}=\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n})^{4}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})^{4}\mathcal{M}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{5,1}})\mathcal{M}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{5,1}}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{5,1})\\
\times\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{5,1}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{M}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{6,1}})\mathcal{M}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{6,1}}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{6,1})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{6,1}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\\
\times\Big(\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{5,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}Q_{6,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})^{2}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{5,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{6,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})^{2}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{6,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{5,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})^{2}\\
\times\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{5,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{6,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})^{2}\Big)^{-1}\prod_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1-Q_{\tau}^{l})^{3}}\times\hat{Z}^{\text{SU(2)-SU(2)}}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}\ ,

(48)

where

\displaystyle\hat{Z}^{\text{SU(2)-SU(2)}}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}^{\frac{2||\mu_{5,2}||^{2}+||\mu_{6,1}||^{2}+||\mu_{6,2}||^{2}}{2}}\mathfrak{t}^{\frac{2||\mu_{5,1}^{t}||^{2}+||\mu_{6,1}^{t}||^{2}+||\mu_{6,2}^{t}||^{2}}{2}}Q_{5}^{|\mu_{5,1}|+|\mu_{5,2}|}\left(-\frac{Q_{6}\sqrt{Q_{6,1}}}{\sqrt{Q_{5,1}}}\right)^{|\mu_{6,1}|+|\mu_{6,2}|}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{I=5}^{6}\prod_{j=1}^{2}Z_{\mu_{I,j}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})Z_{\mu_{I,j}^{t}}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{t})\times\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{6,1}\mu_{5,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{6,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{5,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{6,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{5,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{6,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{6,2}\mu_{5,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{5,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}Q_{6,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{6,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{5,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{6,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,2}\mu_{6,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{5,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}Q_{6,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{6,1}\mu_{5,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{5,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{6,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,2}\mu_{6,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{6,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{5,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{6,2}\mu_{5,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{5,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{6,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})

\displaystyle\times\Big(\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{5,1})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{5,1}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,2}\mu_{5,1}}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{5,1}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,2}\mu_{5,1}}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{5,1}}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,2}\mu_{5,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{5,2}\mu_{5,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n})

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{6,1})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{6,1}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{6,2}\mu_{6,1}}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{6,1}})

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{6,2}\mu_{6,1}}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{6,1}}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{6,2}\mu_{6,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{6,2}\mu_{6,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\Big)^{-1}\ .

(49)

Expression of
Z^{D_{5}}
.

Using the
y
parameters defined in (
37
), one readily finds that the periodicity conditions in (
43
) and (
44
) for the
D_{5}
-type LST become

\displaystyle y_{1,1}=1,\quad y_{2,1}=1,\quad y_{3,1}=1,\quad y_{4,1}=1,\quad y_{5,1}y_{5,2}=1,\quad y_{6,1}y_{6,2}=1,

(50)

where we have set
a_{1,1}=0
.
Gathering all the results and rewriting
Z^{D_{5}}
suitably with the help of the transformation from
\mathcal{N}
to
\varTheta
, we find

Z^{D_{5}}=Z^{D_{5}}_{\text{pert}}\cdot Z^{D_{5}}_{\text{inst}}\ ,

(51)

where

\displaystyle Z^{D_{5}}_{\text{pert}}=

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\prod_{I=1}^{2}\frac{\mathcal{M}(\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{I,1;I,1}})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{I,1;I,1}})}{\prod_{l=1}^{2}\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{I,1}}{y_{5,l}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{I,1;5,l}})}\prod_{J=3}^{4}\frac{\mathcal{M}(\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{J,1;J,1}})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{J,1;J,1}})}{\prod_{k=1}^{2}\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{J,1}}{y_{6,k}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{J,1;6,k}})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{p=1}^{2}\frac{\prod_{c=1}^{2}\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{5,c}}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{5,p;5,c}})\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{5,c}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{5,p;5,c}})}{\prod_{r=1}^{2}\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{6,r}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{5,p;6,r}})\prod_{K=1}^{2}\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{K,1}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{5,p;K,1}})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{s=1}^{2}\frac{\prod_{e=1}^{2}\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{6,s}}{y_{6,e}}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{6,s;6,e}})\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{6,s}}{y_{6,e}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{6,s;6,e}})}{\prod_{d=1}^{2}\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{6,s}}{y_{5,d}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{6,s;5,d}})\prod_{L=3}^{4}\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{6,s}}{y_{L,1}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{6,s;L,1}})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1-Q_{\tau}^{l})^{7}},

(52)

\displaystyle Z_{\text{inst}}^{D_{5}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\prod_{I=1}^{2}\bigg(Q_{I}^{|\mu_{I,1}|}\frac{\prod_{l=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{I,1}\mu_{5,l}}(\frac{y_{I,1}}{y_{5,l}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})}{\varTheta_{\mu_{I,1}\mu_{I,1}}(\frac{y_{I,1}}{y_{I,1}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\mu_{I,1}\mu_{I,1}}(\frac{y_{I,1}}{y_{I,1}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{J=3}^{4}\bigg(Q_{J}^{|\mu_{J,1}|}\frac{\prod_{k=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{J,1}\mu_{6,k}}(\frac{y_{J,1}}{y_{6,k}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})}{\varTheta_{\mu_{J,1}\mu_{J,1}}(\frac{y_{J,1}}{y_{J,1}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\mu_{J,1}\mu_{J,1}}(\frac{y_{J,1}}{y_{J,1}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{p=1}^{2}\bigg(Q_{5}^{|\mu_{5,p}|}\frac{\prod_{r=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{5,p}\mu_{6,r}}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{6,r}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})\prod_{K=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{5,p}\mu_{K,1}}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{K,1}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})}{\prod_{c=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{5,p}\mu_{5,c}}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{5,c}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\mu_{5,p}\mu_{5,c}}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{5,c}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{2}\bigg(Q_{6}^{|\mu_{6,s}|}\frac{\prod_{d=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{6,s}\mu_{5,d}}(\frac{y_{6,s}}{y_{5,d}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})\prod_{L=3}^{4}\varTheta_{\mu_{6,s}\mu_{L,1}}(\frac{y_{6,s}}{y_{L,1}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})}{\prod_{e=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{6,s}\mu_{6,e}}(\frac{y_{6,s}}{y_{6,e}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\mu_{6,s}\mu_{6,e}}(\frac{\mu_{6,s}}{\mu_{6,e}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})}\bigg).

(53)

3.3
E
-type LSTs

Here we apply our trivalent gluing with the bivalent self-gluing to
E
-type LSTs which are engineered from a IIB NS5-brane probing
E_{6},E_{7},E_{8}
singularities. As the simplest example among the

(E_{6},E_{6})

DelZotto:2014hpa
:

SU(3)_{0}
SU(2)

SU(2)

.

(54)

Hayashi:2021pcj
of three

3.3.1
LST on
E_{6}
singularity

Figure 17:
The
E_{6}
-type LST by trivalent gluing together with the bivalent self-gluing. The three subdiagrams in the corners correspond to SU(2)-SU(1) gauge nodes, the middle subdiagram corresponds to SU(3) gauge node.

In this subsection, we compute the partition function of
E_{6}
-type LST from its trivalent gluing brane webs.
We propose that the
E_{6}
-type LST can be explicitly realized by the trivalent gluing together with the bivalent self-gluings as given in figure
17
, where the Young diagram
\mu_{I,j}
label the
j
-th color brane of the
I
-th gauge node. In figure
18
, we also draw two adjacent periods of the bottom right subdiagram of figure
17
. By the periodicity constraints,
Q_{4}=Q^{\prime}_{4}
and
Q_{3}=Q^{\prime}_{3}
, we find that
Q_{4;7}
and
Q_{3;4}
have to satisfy the following conditions,

\displaystyle Q_{4;7}=Q_{7,1}^{\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{\frac{2}{3}}Q_{4,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}},\quad Q_{3;4}=Q_{4,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\ .

(57)

Figure 18:
Subdiagram of
E_{6}
-type LST with two periods.

By the same reasoning, for the other two
SU(2)-SU(1) subdiagrams, we also have

\displaystyle Q_{2;7}=Q_{7,1}^{\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{\frac{2}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}},\qquad Q_{1;2}=Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\ ,

(58)

\displaystyle Q_{6;7}=Q_{7,1}^{\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{\frac{2}{3}}Q_{6,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}},\qquad Q_{5;6}=Q_{6,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\ .

(59)

These three periodicity conditions, (
57
), (
58
), and (
59
), also make the middle SU(3) subdiagram of figure
17
vertically periodic.
Then due to the vertical periodicity
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4},Q_{5},Q_{6},Q_{7}
are the instanton factors of the gauge node
1,2,3,4,5,6,7
respectively in the 5d theory perspective, and they are also the tensor branch parameters of the 6d
E_{6}
-type LST. The horizontal period
u
of the theory due to its affine
E_{6}
quiver structure is then given by

\displaystyle u=Q_{1}Q_{2}^{2}Q_{3}Q_{4}^{2}Q_{5}Q_{6}^{2}Q_{7}^{3}\ .

(60)

Now we are ready to use the trivalent gluing diagram in figure
17
to obtain the partition function of
E_{6}
-type LST which takes the following form,

\displaystyle Z^{E_{6}}=\sum_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}Z^{\text{SU(3)}}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}

\displaystyle\times Z_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,2}\mu_{1,1}}(Q_{7,1},Q_{7,2},Q_{2,1},Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{\tau})

\displaystyle\times Z_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}\mu_{4,1}\mu_{4,2}\mu_{3,1}}(Q_{7,1},Q_{7,2},Q_{4,1},Q_{3},Q_{4},Q_{\tau})

\displaystyle\times Z_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}\mu_{5,1}}(Q_{7,1},Q_{7,2},Q_{6,1},Q_{5},Q_{6},Q_{\tau}),

(61)

with

\displaystyle Z^{\text{SU(3)}}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}\equiv

\displaystyle(-Q_{7})^{|\mu_{7,1}|+|\mu_{7,3}|}f_{\mu_{7,1}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})f_{\mu_{7,3}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})^{-1}\left(-\frac{Q_{7,1}Q_{7,2}Q_{7}}{Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{4,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{6,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)^{|\mu_{7,2}|}
