(63)

The other two
Z
’s in (
61
) are also defined in the same way as the one in (
63
) with indices and arguments accordingly changed as they correspond to the three SU(2)-SU(1) subdiagrams of figure
17
which are related to each other by
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
or a mirror reflection.

We compute the two diagrams in (
62
), substitute the results back and obtain

\displaystyle Z^{\text{SU(3)}}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,2})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1}Q_{7,2})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n})^{3}\mathcal{M}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{7,1}})\mathcal{M}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{7,2}})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{7,1}Q_{7,2}})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,2}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1}Q_{7,2}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})^{3}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{7,1}}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{M}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{7,2}}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{M}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{7,1}Q_{7,2}}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\prod_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1-Q_{\tau}^{l})^{2}}\times\hat{Z}^{\text{SU(3)}}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}},

(64)

where

\displaystyle\hat{Z}^{\text{SU(3)}}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}^{\frac{1}{2}||\mu_{7,2}||^{2}+||\mu_{7,3}||^{2}}\mathfrak{t}^{||\mu_{7,1}^{t}||^{2}+\frac{1}{2}||\mu_{7,2}^{t}||^{2}}Q_{7}^{|\mu_{7,1}|+|\mu_{7,3}|}\left(-\frac{Q_{7,1}Q_{7,2}Q_{7}}{Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{4,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{6,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)^{|\mu_{7,2}|}\prod_{i=1}^{3}\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{7,i}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{7,i}^{t}}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{t})

\displaystyle\times\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,3}\mu_{7,3}}(Q_{\tau}^{n})

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,3}\mu_{7,3}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,1}}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{7,1}}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,1}}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{7,1}})

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,3}\mu_{7,1}}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{7,1}Q_{7,2}}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,3}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1}Q_{7,2})

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,3}\mu_{7,1}}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{7,1}Q_{7,2}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,3}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1}Q_{7,2}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,3}\mu_{7,2}}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{7,2}}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,2})

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,3}\mu_{7,2}}(\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{Q_{7,2}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,2}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}).

(65)

We then compute the two diagrams in (
63
) and substitute the results back which yields

\displaystyle Z_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,2}\mu_{1,1}}(Q_{7,1},Q_{7,2},Q_{2,1},Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{\tau})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1-Q_{\tau}^{l})^{2}}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{2,1})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n})^{3}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{2,1}^{-1})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{2,1}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})^{3}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{2,1}^{-1}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\times\Bigg(\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1}^{\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{-\frac{1}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1}^{\frac{2}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{\frac{1}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})^{2}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1}^{\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{\frac{2}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1}^{-\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{\frac{1}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1}^{\frac{2}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{\frac{1}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1}^{\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{\frac{2}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{7,1}^{-\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{\frac{1}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{7,1}^{-\frac{2}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{-\frac{1}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{7,1}^{-\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{-\frac{2}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{7,1}^{-\frac{2}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{-\frac{1}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{7,1}^{\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{-\frac{1}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{7,1}^{-\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{-\frac{2}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{2,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})^{2}\Bigg)^{-1}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\hat{Z}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,2}\mu_{1,1}}(Q_{7,1},Q_{7,2},Q_{2,1},Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{\tau}),

(66)

where

\displaystyle\hat{Z}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,2}\mu_{1,1}}(Q_{7,1},Q_{7,2},Q_{2,1},Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{\tau})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}^{\frac{||\mu_{2,1}||^{2}+||\mu_{2,2}||^{2}+||\mu_{1,1}||^{2}}{2}}\mathfrak{t}^{\frac{||\mu_{2,1}^{t}||^{2}+||\mu_{2,2}^{t}||^{2}+||\mu_{1,1}^{t}||^{2}}{2}}(-Q_{7,1}^{-\frac{2}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{-\frac{1}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}})^{|\mu_{2,1}|}(-Q_{2,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}Q_{1})^{|\mu_{1,1}|}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left(-Q_{7,1}^{-\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{-\frac{2}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{2}\right)^{|\mu_{2,2}|}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{2,i}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{2,i}^{t}}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{t})\times\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{1,1}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{1,1}^{t}}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{t})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{2,1}\mu_{7,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{7,1}^{-\frac{2}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{-\frac{1}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{2,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1}^{\frac{2}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{\frac{1}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{2,2}\mu_{7,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{7,1}^{-\frac{2}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{-\frac{1}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{2,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1}^{\frac{2}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{\frac{1}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,2}\mu_{2,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{7,1}^{-\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{\frac{1}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{2,1}\mu_{7,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1}^{\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{-\frac{1}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{2,2}\mu_{7,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{7,1}^{\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{-\frac{1}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,2}\mu_{2,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1}^{-\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{\frac{1}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,3}\mu_{2,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{7,1}^{-\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{-\frac{2}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{2,1}\mu_{7,3}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1}^{\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{\frac{2}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{7,3}\mu_{2,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{7,1}^{-\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{-\frac{2}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{2,2}\mu_{7,3}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{7,1}^{\frac{1}{3}}Q_{7,2}^{\frac{2}{3}}Q_{2,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{1,1}\mu_{2,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{2,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{2,1}\mu_{1,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{2,2}\mu_{1,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{2,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{1,1}\mu_{2,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{2,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\Bigg(\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{2,2}\mu_{2,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{2,2}\mu_{2,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{1,1}\mu_{1,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{1,1}\mu_{1,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{2,2}\mu_{2,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{2,1}^{-1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{2,1}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{2,2}\mu_{2,1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{2,1}^{-1}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}})\mathcal{N}_{\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{2,1})\Bigg)^{-1}\ .

(67)

Expression of
Z^{E_{6}}
.

Using the
y
parameters defined in (
37
), one finds that the periodicity condition in (
57
), (
58
), and (
59
) for the
E_{6}
-type LST become

\displaystyle y_{1,1}=1,\qquad y_{2,1}y_{2,2}=1,\qquad y_{3,1}=1,\qquad y_{4,1}y_{4,2}=1,\

\displaystyle y_{5,1}=1,\qquad y_{6,1}y_{6,2}=1,\qquad y_{7,1}y_{7,2}y_{7,3}=1,

(68)

where we have set
a_{1,1}=0
.
Substitute (
66
), (
67
), (
64
), and (
65
) into (
61
), and transform the
\mathcal{N}
functions into the
\varTheta
functions, we find

Z^{E_{6}}=Z^{E_{6}}_{\text{pert}}\cdot Z^{E_{6}}_{\text{inst}}\ ,

(69)

with

\displaystyle Z^{E_{6}}_{\text{pert}}=

\displaystyle\prod_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1-Q_{\tau}^{l})^{8}}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\prod_{I=1,3,5}\frac{\mathcal{M}(\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{I,1;I,1}})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{I,1;I,1}})}{\prod_{l=1}^{2}\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{I,1}}{y_{I+1,l}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{I,1;I+1,l}})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{J=2,4,6}\prod_{p=1}^{2}\frac{\prod_{s=1}^{2}\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{J,p}}{y_{J,s}}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{J,p;J,s}})\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{J,p}}{y_{J,s}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{J,p;J,s}})}{\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{J,p}}{y_{J-1,1}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{J,p;J-1,1}})\prod_{r=1}^{3}\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{J,p}}{y_{7,r}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{J,p;7,r}})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{c=1}^{3}\frac{\prod_{e=1}^{3}\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{7,c}}{y_{7,e}}\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{7,c;7,e}})\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{7,c}}{y_{7,e}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{7,c;7,e}})}{\prod_{L=2,4,6}\prod_{d=1}^{2}\mathcal{M}(\frac{y_{7,c}}{y_{L,d}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}Q_{\tau}^{{\textit{{n}}}_{7,c;L,d}})}\ ,

(70)

\displaystyle Z^{E_{6}}_{\text{inst}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\prod_{I=1,3,5}\bigg(Q_{I}^{|\mu_{I,1}|}\frac{\prod_{l=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{I,1}\mu_{I+1,l}}(\frac{y_{I,1}}{y_{I+1,l}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})}{\varTheta_{\mu_{I,1}\mu_{I,1}}(\frac{y_{I,1}}{y_{I,1}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\mu_{I,1}\mu_{I,1}}(\frac{y_{I,1}}{y_{I,1}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{J=2,4,6}\prod_{p=1}^{2}\bigg(Q_{J}^{|\mu_{J,p}|}\frac{\varTheta_{\mu_{J,p}\mu_{J-1,1}}(\frac{y_{J,p}}{y_{J-1,1}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})\prod_{r=1}^{3}\varTheta_{\mu_{J,p}\mu_{7,r}}(\frac{y_{J,p}}{y_{7,r}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})}{\prod_{s=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{J,p}\mu_{J,s}}(\frac{y_{J,p}}{y_{J,s}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\mu_{J,p}\mu_{J,s}}(\frac{y_{J,p}}{y_{J,s}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{c=1}^{3}\bigg(Q_{7}^{|\mu_{7,c}|}\frac{\prod_{L=2,4,6}\prod_{d=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{7,c}\mu_{L,d}}(\frac{y_{7,c}}{y_{L,d}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})}{\prod_{e=1}^{3}\varTheta_{\mu_{7,c}\mu_{7,e}}(\frac{y_{7,c}}{y_{7,e}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\mu_{7,c}\mu_{7,e}}(\frac{y_{7,c}}{y_{7,e}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})}\bigg)\ .

(71)

4
Higher ranks and general expression of partition functions

In the previous section, we discussed the partition functions for rank-1
D_{4}
,
D_{5}
, and
E_{6}
-type LSTs based on tri-/quadri-valent gluings
Hayashi:2017jze
;
Hayashi:2021pcj
by further introducing the bivalent self-gluings. In terms of the
\varTheta
functions defined in (
32
), the partition functions are expressed in a more compact form, which leads to an effective way of organizing or generalizing the partition functions. In this section, we discuss a generalization of the rank-1
D_{4},D_{5}
and
E_{6}
-type LSTs by increasing the ranks of each gauge node of affine quivers
k
times, which we refer to as rank-
k

D
/
E
-type LSTs.
We express the partition functions for these higher rank LSTs also in terms of the
\varTheta
functions.

To explain how to generalize, let us first recall the procedure of obtaining the partition function of
E_{6}
-type LST in the previous section. First, we draw the subdiagrams in the trivalent gluing method as shown in figure
17
. Then we compute the partition function of each subdiagram by the topological vertex formalism. At this step, further corrections to the partition function of each subdiagram are needed:

(1)

For each of the three SU(2)-SU(1) subdiagrams, we need to divide them by additional factors. For example, in (
63
) we remove the vector contributions of SU
(3)
from the upper right SU(2)-SU(1) subdiagram.

(2)

For the middle subdiagram whose computation is shown in (
62
), only the SU
(3)
vector contributions are counted, while the bifundamental contributions of SU
(3)
-SU
(2)
and the vector contributions of SU
(2)
are not counted as they are already taken into account in the partition functions of the three SU(2)-SU(1) subdiagrams.

There are no bifundamental contributions between two different SU
(2)
’s as they are not connected in the quiver, so there is no string connecting color branes of different SU
(2)
’s in the middle subdiagram of figure
17
. However, such unphysical bifundamental contributions would appear if the middle subdiagram was used directly for computation. So these further corrections to the partition function of each subdiagram ensure that no redundant factors and unphysical factors are counted when we multiply the partition functions of each subdiagram together to get the full partition function.

We emphasize that, as summarized above, picking up the proper vector and bifundamental contributions for each subdiagram is important for the computation. Roughly speaking, as the
\mathcal{R}
factors which are generated in the computation by topological vertex through the formula (
B
) represent all the vector and bifundamental contributions, picking up the proper vector and bifundamental contributions for each subdiagram corresponds to picking up the proper
\mathcal{R}
factors for each subdiagram. Once we understand this point, we can further simplify the method of obtaining the full partition function of the LST.

Figure 19:
Schematic picture of a decomposition of trivalent gluing subdiagrams of
E_{6}
-type LST into smaller subdiagrams, where each subdiagram corresponds to a single gauge node.

4.1
Introduction of general node

We further decompose the subdiagrams in figure
17
into smaller subdiagrams that correspond to each single node of the affine
E_{6}
quiver as is shown in figure
19
. Now each subdiagram of figure
19
has similar web structure and the subdiagrams differ in the number of color branes in the left, middle and right of the subdiagrams. For a general
D,E
-type LST with arbitrary rank, its tri-/quadri-valent gluing diagrams can also be further decomposed into smaller subdiagrams which correspond to single gauge nodes of the affine quiver. We can treat the subdiagrams of single gauge nodes as the building blocks to construct a general
D,E
-type LST with arbitrary rank. Specifically, we can compute the partition function of each single gauge node and then multiply these partition functions together to obtain the full partition function of the LST.

To this end, we first consider a general single gauge node whose subdiagram is illustrated in figure
20
. Our strategy for obtaining the partition function of this general node is to first compute the contribution to the partition function from one arbitrary color brane inside this node. As we choose this color brane to be arbitrary, we will obtain a formula for the contribution that is applicable for any color brane inside this node.
Then we multiply contributions from all the color branes inside this node to get the partition function of this general node.

In figure
20
we pick one arbitrary color brane inside the node and label it by Young diagram
\alpha
, and consider only the bifundamental and vector contributions that involve
\alpha
. For convenience and clarity, we use uppercase Greek letters to denote the name of a color brane whose Young diagram is denoted by the corresponding lowercase Greek letter. For example, we call the color brane labeled by Young diagram
\alpha
as
A
. In figure
20
, there are
r_{1}
color branes
M_{1},\cdots,M_{r_{1}}
above
A
inside the same node which we label by Young diagrams
\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{r_{1}}
. There are
l_{1}
color branes
N_{l_{1}},\cdots,N_{1}
below
A
inside the same node which we label by Young diagrams
\nu_{l_{1}},\cdots,\nu_{1}
. On the right side of the node, there are
r_{2}
color branes
P_{1},\cdots,P_{r_{2}}
above
A
which we label by Young diagrams
\rho_{1},\cdots,\rho_{r_{2}}
. There are
l_{2}
color branes
\varGamma_{l_{2}},\cdots,\varGamma_{1}
below
A
which we label by Young diagrams
\gamma_{l_{2}},\cdots,\gamma_{1}
. On the left side of the node, there are
r_{3}
color branes
\varLambda_{1},\cdots,\varLambda_{r_{3}}
above
A
which we label by Young diagrams
\lambda_{1},\cdots,\lambda_{r_{3}}
. There are
l_{3}
color branes
\varXi_{l_{3}},\cdots,\varXi_{1}
below
A
which we label by Young diagrams
\xi_{l_{3}},\cdots,\xi_{1}
. If this node is at the junction of the quiver, then there could be more than one adjacent gauge node on the left or right of this node, and correspondingly the color branes on the left or right of the node may belong to different gauge nodes.

Figure 20:
A general subdiagram corresponding to a single gauge node.

In figure
20
, in order to make this gauge node to be vertically periodic, the top two NS-charged branes have to be parallel to each other and the bottom two NS-charged branes also have to be parallel to each other. We label the corresponding brane charges as
(x_{1},1)
and
(x_{2},-1)
. Also, due to vertical periodicity the horizontal distance between the top two parallel NS-charged branes has to be equal to the horizontal distance between the bottom two parallel NS-charged branes, we denote the distance as
Q_{b}
which is also the instanton factor as well as the tensor branch parameter of this gauge node. Following the assignment of the charges of the top and bottom NS-charged branes, we can label all the charges of the remaining NS-charged branes. The charge conservation at the two vertices of color brane
A
gives the constraints:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}x_{1}+x_{2}=r_{3}-r_{1}+l_{3}-l_{1}-1\\
x_{1}+x_{2}=r_{1}-r_{2}+l_{1}-l_{2}+1\end{array}\right.\ ,

(72)

from which we find that

n_{2}=2n_{1}\ ,

(73)

with

\displaystyle n_{1}\equiv r_{1}+l_{1}+1,\qquad n_{2}\equiv r_{2}+l_{2}+r_{3}+l_{3}.

(74)

Note that
n_{1}
is the number of color branes inside the gauge node in figure
20
and
n_{2}
is the number of color branes on the left or right side of the gauge node, (
73
)
ensures the simultaneous parallelism of the top two and bottom two NS-charged branes.

From the charge distributions of the branes in figure
20
with the labelings of Kähler parameters, we can find the relations between the Kähler parameter
Q_{A}
of the brane
A
and the Kähler parameter
Q_{M_{1}}
of the brane
M_{1}
:

\displaystyle Q_{M_{1}}=

\displaystyle~Q_{A}Q_{1}^{x_{1}-1}\cdots Q_{r_{1}-1}^{x_{1}-(r_{1}-1)}Q_{r_{1}}^{x_{1}-r_{1}}Q_{r_{1}+1}^{x_{1}-r_{1}+1}\cdots Q_{r_{1}+r_{2}}^{x_{1}-r_{1}+r_{2}}

\displaystyle\times Q_{1}^{-x_{1}-1}\cdots Q_{r_{1}-1}^{-x_{1}-(r_{1}-1)}{Q^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{2}}}^{-x_{1}-r_{1}}{Q^{\prime}_{1}}^{-x_{1}-r_{1}+1}\cdots{Q^{\prime}_{r_{3}}}^{-x_{1}-r_{1}+r_{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~Q_{A}Q_{1}^{-1}\cdots Q_{r_{1}-1}^{-(r_{1}-1)}Q_{r_{1}}^{-r_{1}}Q_{r_{1}+1}^{-r_{1}+1}\cdots Q_{r_{1}+r_{2}}^{-r_{1}+r_{2}}

\displaystyle\times Q_{1}^{-1}\cdots Q_{r_{1}-1}^{-(r_{1}-1)}{Q^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{2}}}^{-r_{1}}{Q^{\prime}_{1}}^{-r_{1}+1}\cdots{Q^{\prime}_{r_{3}}}^{-r_{1}+r_{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~Q_{A}\frac{Q_{P_{r_{2}}A}Q_{P_{r_{2}-1}A}\cdots Q_{P_{1}A}Q_{\varLambda_{r_{3}}A}Q_{\varLambda_{r_{3}-1}A}\cdots Q_{\varLambda_{1}A}}{Q_{M_{r_{1}}A}^{2}Q_{M_{r_{1}-1}A}^{2}\cdots Q_{M_{1}A}^{2}},

(75)

where we have defined, for example,
Q_{\varLambda_{1}A}\equiv\frac{y_{\varLambda_{1}}}{y_{A}}
in which
y_{\varLambda_{1}},y_{A}
are exponentiated vertical positions of the brane
\varLambda_{1}
and
A
defined in (
37
).

By the same reasoning, for the Kähler parameter
Q_{N_{1}}
of the brane
N_{1}
,

\displaystyle Q_{N_{1}}=

\displaystyle~Q_{A}Q_{-l_{1}-l_{2}}^{x_{2}-l_{1}+l_{2}}\cdots Q_{-l_{1}-1}^{x_{2}-l_{1}+1}Q_{-l_{1}}^{x_{2}-l_{1}}Q_{-l_{1}+1}^{x_{2}-l_{1}+1}\cdots Q_{-1}^{x_{2}-1}

\displaystyle\times{Q^{\prime}_{-l_{3}}}^{l_{3}-x_{2}-l_{1}}\cdots{Q^{\prime}_{-1}}^{1-x_{2}-l_{1}}{Q^{\prime}_{-\frac{1}{2}}}^{-x_{2}-l_{1}}Q_{-l_{1}+1}^{-x_{2}-l_{1}+1}\cdots Q_{-1}^{-x_{2}-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~Q_{A}\frac{Q_{A\varGamma_{l_{2}}}Q_{A\varGamma_{l_{2}-1}}\cdots Q_{A\varGamma_{1}}Q_{A\varXi_{l_{3}}}Q_{A\varXi_{l_{3}-1}}\cdots Q_{A\varXi_{1}}}{Q_{AN_{l_{1}}}^{2}Q_{AN_{l_{1}-1}}^{2}\cdots Q_{AN_{1}}^{2}}.

(76)

In order to have the compactification along the vertical direction, we should have

Q_{M_{1}}=Q_{N_{1}}=Q_{b}\ ,

(77)

so

Q_{\textbf{m}}^{2(r_{1}+l_{1}+1)}=Q_{\textbf{r}}^{r_{2}+l_{2}}Q_{\textbf{l}}^{r_{3}+l_{3}}\ ,

(78)

where

\displaystyle Q_{\textbf{m}}\equiv

\displaystyle\left(Q_{M_{1}A}\cdots Q_{M_{r_{1}}A}Q_{N_{l_{1}}A}\cdots Q_{N_{1}A}\right)^{\frac{1}{r_{1}+l_{1}+1}},

(79)

\displaystyle Q_{\textbf{r}}\equiv

\displaystyle\left(Q_{P_{1}A}\cdots Q_{P_{r_{2}}A}Q_{\varGamma_{l_{2}}A}\cdots Q_{\varGamma_{1}A}\right)^{\frac{1}{r_{2}+l_{2}}},

(80)

\displaystyle Q_{\textbf{l}}\equiv

\displaystyle\left(Q_{\varLambda_{1}A}\cdots Q_{\varLambda_{r_{3}}A}Q_{\varXi_{l_{3}}A}\cdots Q_{\varXi_{1}A}\right)^{\frac{1}{r_{3}+l_{3}}}\ .

(81)

Q_{\textbf{m}}
,
Q_{\textbf{r}}
and
Q_{\textbf{l}}
are the relative vertical positions of the centers of color branes in the middle, right, and left of figure
20
to the color brane
A
.

For a gauge node at one end of an affine
D,E
-type quiver, its adjacent node can only appear on its one side, so the adjacent color branes can only appear either on its right side or its left side. For example,
r_{2}
and
l_{2}
of such gauge node will be both zero if there are no color branes on the right side of the gauge node. Then, from (
78
) and (
73
), for this gauge node we have

Q_{\textbf{m}}=Q_{\textbf{l}}\ .

(82)

So the positions of centers of color branes for this node and its adjacent node are the same. Consequently, we find that the vertical positions of the centers of color branes of all nodes in the affine
D,E
-type quiver are the same by using
eqs.

73
and
78
repeatedly for every node of the quiver, which means the bifundamental masses are all zero and we can choose a common center of Coulomb branch for all the gauge nodes. From now on, we will choose this common center as the zero point of the vertical direction.

4.2
Partition function of general node

In figure
20
, we have picked an arbitrary color brane
A
inside the gauge node.
Consider another arbitrary color brane
B
in figure
20
which may or may not lie inside the same gauge node. Let us denote the vertical positions of the color branes
A
and
B
by
a_{A}
and
a_{B}
, then their exponentiated vertical positions are given by
y_{A}\equiv e^{-\boldsymbol{\beta}\,a_{A}}
and
y_{B}\equiv e^{-\boldsymbol{\beta}\,a_{B}}
, respectively. By considering whether the color branes
A
and
B
are in the same gauge node or not as well as their relative vertical positions, we discuss and compute vector or bifundamental contribution arising from these color branes.

Bifundamental contributions.

When the color branes
B
is not inside but outside the gauge node, the fundamental string connecting brane
A
and
B
leads to the bifundamental contributions. Let us first consider the case that their relative vertical position is
a_{B}>a_{A}
. Following Appendix
B
, one readily finds that the corresponding
\mathcal{R}
factors are given by

\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{R}_{\alpha^{t}\beta}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}\frac{Q_{\tau}}{Q_{BA}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{R}_{\beta^{t}\alpha}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{BA};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q}).

(83)

These
\mathcal{R}
factors are further factorized into
\mathcal{M}
factors and
\mathcal{N}
factors by the definition in (
7
). The
\mathcal{M}
factors from (
83
) contribute to the perturbative part of the partition function,

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{\tau}Q_{AB}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{BA}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\text{PE}\left[-\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{\tau}Q_{AB}\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}\,\mathfrak{q}}}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\right]\text{PE}\left[-\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{BA}\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}\,\mathfrak{q}}}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\text{PE}\left[-\frac{(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}Q_{\tau}^{n-1})Q_{\tau}Q_{AB}\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}\,\mathfrak{q}}}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\right]\text{PE}\left[-\frac{(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}Q_{\tau}^{n-1})Q_{BA}\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}\,\mathfrak{q}}}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\text{PE}\left[-\frac{\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}\,\mathfrak{q}}}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\frac{1}{1-Q_{\tau}}(Q_{\tau}Q_{AB}+Q_{BA})\right],

(84)

where we have assumed
|Q_{\tau}|<1
and
|\mathfrak{t}|,|\mathfrak{q}|\ll 1
in order for convergence of the Plethystic exponential and we shall impose this assumption throughout the rest of this paper.

On the other hand, these
\mathcal{M}
factors can also be written as

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{\tau}Q_{AB}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})^{-1}\cdot\mathcal{M}(Q_{BA}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})^{-1}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{\tau}Q_{BA}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})^{-1}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{\tau}Q_{AB}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})^{-1}\cdot\mathcal{M}(Q_{AB}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})^{-1}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{\tau}Q_{BA}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{AB}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})^{-1}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{\tau}Q_{BA}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\text{PE}\left[-\frac{\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}\,\mathfrak{q}}}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\frac{1}{1-Q_{\tau}}(Q_{\tau}Q_{BA}+Q_{AB})\right],

(85)

where by the
\rightarrow
, we mean an analytic continuation due to the flop transition of topological string
Iqbal:2004ne
;
Konishi:2006ev
;
Taki:2008hb
that we made to the middle
\mathcal{M}
by the formula

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(Q;\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})=\text{PE}\left[\frac{Q\mathfrak{q}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}\right]\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(Q^{-1}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})=\text{PE}\left[\frac{Q^{-1}\mathfrak{t}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}\right].

(86)

It follows from (
4.2
) and (
4.2
) that the product of
\mathcal{M}
factors is equal to the following PE up to a flop:

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{\tau}Q_{AB}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{BA}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\text{PE}\left[-\frac{\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}\,\mathfrak{q}}}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\frac{1+Q_{\tau}}{1-Q_{\tau}}\frac{Q_{AB}}{2}\right]\text{PE}\left[-\frac{\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}\,\mathfrak{q}}}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\frac{1+Q_{\tau}}{1-Q_{\tau}}\frac{Q_{BA}}{2}\right]\ ,

(87)

where we have separated the result into two factors, the first PE factor is assigned to the brane
B
and the second PE factor is assigned to the brane
A
. Since (
87
) is true also for
a_{B}<a_{A}
, we can assign the factors in the same way. So, the factor that is assigned to
A
is

\displaystyle\text{PE}\left[-\frac{\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}\,\mathfrak{q}}}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\frac{1+Q_{\tau}}{1-Q_{\tau}}\frac{Q_{BA}}{2}\right]\quad\text{for both }a_{B}>a_{A}\text{ and }a_{B}<a_{A}.

(88)

The
\mathcal{N}
factors from (
83
) contribute to the instanton part of the partition function which are

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\alpha\beta}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}\frac{Q_{\tau}}{Q_{BA}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{N}_{\beta\alpha}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{BA}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})

(89)

\displaystyle=\frac{(Q_{BA})^{\frac{|\beta|}{2}}{\hat{f}_{\beta}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{W^{|\beta|}}\varTheta_{\beta\alpha}(\tfrac{y_{B}}{y_{A}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})\times\frac{(-1)^{|\alpha|}(Q_{BA})^{\frac{|\alpha|}{2}}{\hat{f}_{\alpha}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{W^{|\alpha|}}\varTheta_{\alpha\beta}(\tfrac{y_{A}}{y_{B}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})\ ,

where
\alpha,\beta
are the Young diagrams associated with the color branes
A,B
, respectively, and
\hat{f}_{\alpha}
and
W
are defined as

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{\alpha}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\equiv\mathfrak{t}^{\frac{||\alpha^{t}||^{2}}{2}}\mathfrak{q}^{-\frac{||\alpha||^{2}}{2}}\ ,

(90)

\displaystyle W\equiv\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}(1-Q_{\tau}^{n})\ .

(91)

In (
89
), we have separated the result into two parts: the first part is before the multiplication sign which is assigned to
\beta
, and the second part is after the multiplication sign which is assigned to
\alpha
. If
a_{B}<a_{A}
, then the factors that should be assigned to
\alpha
are just the first part with
\alpha,\beta
exchanged. So, the factors that should be assigned to
\alpha
are

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{(-1)^{|\alpha|}(Q_{BA})^{\frac{|\alpha|}{2}}{\hat{f}_{\alpha}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{W^{|\alpha|}}\varTheta_{\alpha\beta}(\frac{y_{A}}{y_{B}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau}),&\quad\text{if}\ a_{B}>a_{A}\ ,\\
\frac{(Q_{AB})^{\frac{|\alpha|}{2}}{\hat{f}_{\alpha}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{W^{|\alpha|}}\varTheta_{\alpha\beta}(\frac{y_{A}}{y_{B}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau}),&\quad\text{if}\ a_{B}<a_{A}\ .\end{array}\right.

(92)

Vector contribution I.

When the color brane
B
(in the case that
B
is not
A
) is inside the gauge node, the fundamental string connecting brane
A
and
B
leads to vector contributions.
If
a_{B}>a_{A}
, the corresponding
\mathcal{R}
factors based on Appendix
B
are

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\alpha^{t}\beta}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}\tfrac{Q_{\tau}}{Q_{BA}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{t}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{R}_{\beta^{t}\alpha}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{BA}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{t}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{R}_{\alpha^{t}\beta}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}\tfrac{Q_{\tau}}{Q_{BA}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{R}_{\beta^{t}\alpha}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{BA}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q}).

(93)

The
\mathcal{M}
factors from (
4.2
) contribute to the perturbative part of the partition function,

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}\tfrac{Q_{\tau}}{Q_{BA}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{BA};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}\tfrac{Q_{\tau}}{Q_{BA}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{BA}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle=\text{PE}\left[\frac{\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t}}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\frac{1}{1-Q_{\tau}}(Q_{\tau}Q_{AB}+Q_{BA})\right]\ .

(94)

These
\mathcal{M}
factors can also be written as

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{\tau}Q_{AB};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\cdot\mathcal{M}(Q_{BA};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{BA};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle\times\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{\tau}Q_{AB}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{M}(Q_{BA}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{BA}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{\tau}Q_{AB};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\cdot\mathcal{M}(Q_{AB}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{BA};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle\times\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{\tau}Q_{AB}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{M}(Q_{AB};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{BA}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{AB};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{BA};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{AB}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle\times\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{BA}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{PE}\left[\frac{\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t}}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\frac{1}{1-Q_{\tau}}(Q_{\tau}Q_{BA}+Q_{AB})\right]\ ,

(95)

where (
86
) is used when deriving the formula on the right side of the
\rightarrow
.
It follows from (
4.2
) and (
4.2
) that the product of
\mathcal{M}
factors is equal to the following PE up to a flop:

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}\tfrac{Q_{\tau}}{Q_{BA}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{BA};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}\tfrac{Q_{\tau}}{Q_{BA}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{BA}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\text{PE}\left[\frac{\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t}}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\frac{1+Q_{\tau}}{1-Q_{\tau}}\frac{Q_{AB}}{2}\right]\text{PE}\left[\frac{\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t}}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\frac{1+Q_{\tau}}{1-Q_{\tau}}\frac{Q_{BA}}{2}\right]\ ,

(96)

where we have separated the result into two factors, the first PE factor is assigned to the brane
B
and the second PE factor is assigned to the brane
A
. Since (
96
) is true also for
a_{B}<a_{A}
, we can assign the factors in the same way. So, the factor that is assigned to
A
is

\displaystyle\text{PE}\left[\frac{\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t}}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\frac{1+Q_{\tau}}{1-Q_{\tau}}\frac{Q_{BA}}{2}\right],\quad\text{for both }a_{B}>a_{A}\text{ and }a_{B}<a_{A}\ .

(97)

The
\mathcal{N}
factors from (
4.2
) contribute to the instanton part of the partition function,

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\alpha\beta}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}\tfrac{Q_{\tau}}{Q_{BA}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{N}_{\beta\alpha}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{BA};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{N}_{\alpha\beta}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}\tfrac{Q_{\tau}}{Q_{BA}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{N}_{\beta\alpha}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{BA}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\ .

(98)

After rewriting the above contributions with the help of (
89
), we get

\displaystyle\resizebox{21931650}{}{$\frac{(Q_{BA})^{|\beta|}{\hat{f}_{\beta}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})}^{-1}}{W^{2|\beta|}}\varTheta_{\beta\alpha}(\tfrac{y_{B}}{y_{A}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\beta\alpha}(\tfrac{y_{B}}{y_{A}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})\times\frac{(Q_{BA})^{|\alpha|}\hat{f}_{\alpha}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})}{W^{2|\alpha|}}\varTheta_{\alpha\beta}(\tfrac{y_{A}}{y_{B}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\alpha\beta}(\tfrac{y_{A}}{y_{B}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})$},

(99)

where we have separated the result into two parts, the first part is assigned to the brane
B
and the second part is assigned to the brane
A
.
If
a_{B}<a_{A}
, then we can also get the corresponding result by just exchanging
\alpha,\beta
(
A,B
) in the above equation. So, the factors that should be assigned to
\alpha
are

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\left(\frac{(Q_{BA})^{|\alpha|}\hat{f}_{\alpha}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})}{W^{2|\alpha|}}\varTheta_{\alpha\beta}(\tfrac{y_{A}}{y_{B}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\alpha\beta}(\tfrac{y_{A}}{y_{B}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})\right)^{-1}&\quad\text{if}\ a_{B}>a_{A}\,,\\
\left(\frac{(Q_{AB})^{|\alpha|}{\hat{f}_{\alpha}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})}^{-1}}{W^{2|\alpha|}}\varTheta_{\alpha\beta}(\tfrac{y_{A}}{y_{B}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\alpha\beta}(\tfrac{y_{A}}{y_{B}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})\right)^{-1}&\quad\text{if}\ a_{B}<a_{A}\,.\end{array}\right.

(100)

Vector contribution II.

When brane
B
is brane
A
,
the fundamental string connecting brane
A
and

B
also provides vector contributions which are the following
\mathcal{R}
factors based on Appendix
B
,

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{R}_{\alpha^{t}\alpha}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{t}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{R}_{\alpha^{t}\alpha}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\ .

(101)

The
\mathcal{M}
factors of (
101
) contribute to the perturbative part of the partition function and can be written as the following PE:

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})=\text{PE}\left[\frac{1}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\frac{1}{1-Q_{\tau}}(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t}\,Q_{\tau})\right]\ ,

(102)

where we have included an extra factor
\mathcal{M}(1;\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})
when getting the right side of the
\simeq
. From here on, we use the symbol
\simeq
to denote equality up to extra factors. We can also get another expression by including an extra factor
\mathcal{M}(\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})
instead:

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})=\text{PE}\left[\frac{1}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\frac{1}{1-Q_{\tau}}(\mathfrak{q}\,Q_{\tau}+\mathfrak{t})\right]\ .

(103)

So from (
4.2
) and (
4.2
), the product of
\mathcal{M}
factors of (
101
) is equal to the following PE up to extra factors:

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\mathcal{M}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}};\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\simeq\text{PE}\left[\frac{\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t}}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\frac{1+Q_{\tau}}{1-Q_{\tau}}\frac{1}{2}\right]\ .

(104)

Combined with two
\tilde{Z}
factors coming from the two vertices at the ends of
A
, the
\mathcal{N}
factors from (
101
) can be transformed into
\varTheta
functions by (
31
) which contribute to the instanton part of the partition function:

\left(\frac{\mathfrak{t}^{\frac{||\alpha^{t}||^{2}}{2}}\mathfrak{q}^{\frac{||\alpha||^{2}}{2}}}{W^{2|\alpha|}}\varTheta_{\alpha\alpha}(1|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\alpha\alpha}(\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})\right)^{-1}\ .

(105)

Apart from the contributions from
eqs.

92
,
100
and
105
, the following factors which come from the leftover factors of the two vertices of
A
and the edge factor of
A
also contribute to the instanton partition function of the single node:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{t}^{\frac{||\alpha^{t}||^{2}}{2}}\mathfrak{q}^{\frac{||\alpha||^{2}}{2}}{f_{\alpha}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})}^{h}(-Q_{A})^{|\alpha|}=\mathfrak{t}^{\frac{||\alpha^{t}||^{2}}{2}}\mathfrak{q}^{\frac{||\alpha||^{2}}{2}}(-1)^{(h+1)|\alpha|}{\hat{f}_{\alpha}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})}^{h}Q_{A}^{|\alpha|}\ ,

(106)

with

h=x_{1}+x_{2}+r_{1}-r_{3}-l_{1}+l_{2}\ .

(107)

Computation of the perturbative part.

We discussed the bifundamental or vector contributions arising from the arbitrary brane
A
inside the gauge node of figure
20
and the arbitrary brane
B
, among these contributions there are three types of sources,
(
88
), (
97
) and (
104
), that contribute to the perturbative partition function. Hold
A
fixed and sum over these three types of sources from all the color branes in figure
20
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\text{PE}\left[\frac{1}{2(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\frac{1+Q_{\tau}}{1-Q_{\tau}}\bigg(-\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}\,\mathfrak{q}}\sum_{i=n_{1}+1}^{n_{1}+n_{2}}Q_{B_{i}A}+(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}Q_{B_{i}A}\bigg)\right]\ ,

(108)

where for simplicity we have relabelled the Young diagrams in figure
20
as

\displaystyle\{{\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{n_{1}}}\}\equiv\{\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{r_{1}},\alpha,\nu_{l_{1}},\cdots,\nu_{1}\},

(109)

\displaystyle\{\beta_{n_{1}+1},\cdots,\beta_{n_{1}+n_{2}}\}\equiv\{\rho_{1},\cdots,\rho_{r_{2}},\gamma_{l_{2}},\cdots,\gamma_{1},\lambda_{1},\cdots,\lambda_{r_{3}},\xi_{l_{3}},\cdots,\xi_{1}\}\ ,

(110)

so the brane
B_{i}
is the brane labelled by the Young diagram
\beta_{i}
.

The contributions to the perturbative partition function coming from the other color branes inside the gauge node can also be obtained from (
108
) by just replacing
A
with the corresponding color brane. Multiplying all the contributions from all the color branes inside the gauge node together, we get the perturbative partition function of the single gauge node

\displaystyle Z^{\text{node,pert}}=\text{PE}\Bigg[

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\frac{1+Q_{\tau}}{1-Q_{\tau}}

(111)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{j=1}^{n_{1}}\bigg(-\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}\,\mathfrak{q}}\sum_{i=n_{1}+1}^{n_{1}+n_{2}}Q_{B_{i}B_{j}}+(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}Q_{B_{i}B_{j}}\bigg)\Bigg]\ .

(112)

Computation of the instanton part.

Among the bifundamental or vector contributions between brane
A
and
B
, there are four types of sources from (
92
), (
100
), (
105
) and (
106
) that contribute to the instanton partition function. Hold
A
fixed and sum over these sources from all the color branes in figure
20
, we can get the contribution of color brane
A
to the instanton partition function of the single gauge node in which the remaining
W
,
\hat{f}
and
-1
factors are

\displaystyle W^{-(r_{2}+r_{3}+l_{2}+l_{3}-2(r_{1}+l_{1}+1))}

\displaystyle=1,

(113)

\displaystyle\left(\hat{f}_{\alpha}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\right)^{h+\frac{1}{2}(r_{2}+r_{3}-2r_{1}+2l_{1}-l_{2}-l_{3})}

\displaystyle=1,

(114)

\displaystyle(-1)^{(h+1+r_{2}+r_{3}-2r_{1})|\alpha|}=(-1)^{2|\alpha|}

\displaystyle=1,

(115)

where we used (
72
), (
73
) and (

). On the other hand, by using
sections

4.1
,
4.1
and
77
, the net
Q
factors in the result become

Q_{b}^{|\alpha|}.

(116)

So the instanton contribution from brane
A
to the single node partition function can be written as

\displaystyle Q_{b}^{|\alpha|}\frac{\prod_{k=n_{1}+1}^{n_{1}+n_{2}}\varTheta_{\alpha\beta_{k}}(\tfrac{y_{A}}{y_{B_{k}}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})}{\prod_{k=1}^{n_{1}}\varTheta_{\alpha\beta_{k}}(\tfrac{y_{A}}{y_{B_{k}}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\alpha\beta_{k}}(\tfrac{y_{A}}{y_{B_{k}}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})}\ .

(117)

The contributions of the other color branes inside the single node can also be obtained from (
117
) by replacing
\alpha
with the corresponding Young diagram of the color brane. Multiply all the contributions from all the color branes inside the single gauge node, we get the instanton partition function of the single node

\displaystyle Z_{\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{n_{1}+n_{2}}}^{\text{node,inst}}=Q_{b}^{\sum_{\ell=1}^{n_{1}}|\beta_{\ell}|}\prod_{\ell=1}^{n_{1}}\frac{\prod_{k=n_{1}+1}^{n_{1}+n_{2}}\varTheta_{\beta_{\ell}\beta_{k}}(\tfrac{y_{B_{\ell}}}{y_{B_{k}}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})}{\prod_{k=1}^{n_{1}}\varTheta_{\beta_{\ell}\beta_{k}}(\tfrac{y_{B_{\ell}}}{y_{B_{k}}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\beta_{\ell}\beta_{k}}(\tfrac{y_{B_{\ell}}}{y_{B_{k}}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})}\ ,

(118)

which is the basic unit that appears in the instanton partition functions of rank-1
D_{4}
,
D_{5}
and
E_{6}
-type LSTs in (
3.1
),(
3.2
) and (
3.3.1
).

4.3
Full partition function

We have obtained the partition function of a general single gauge node in the previous subsection.
Now we can easily obtain the full partition function of a general
D,E
-type LST by multiplying the partition functions of each gauge node together.
But before that, there is still one more kind of factor that is missing in the perturbative part.
We further need to include the factor
8
8

8

The same kind of factor also appears in (
89
) where we denote it by
W^{-1}
, but the
W
’s there are generated when transforming
\mathcal{N}
functions to
\varTheta
functions by (
30
) and (
