), and we have included them in the instanton part by (
113
).

\displaystyle\prod_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-Q_{\tau}^{l}}

(119)

which is generated from a single periodic vertical strip by (
B
) into the perturbative part.
This factor can be rewritten
9
9

9

The two formulas are used:
\log(1-Q)=-\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{Q^{n}}{n}
,
\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}Q^{l}=\frac{Q}{1-Q}
.
in terms of PE:

\displaystyle\prod_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-Q_{\tau}^{l}}=\exp\left[-\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\log(1-Q_{\tau}^{l})\right]=\exp\left[\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\frac{Q_{\tau}^{n}}{1-Q_{\tau}^{n}}\right]=\text{PE}\left[\frac{Q_{\tau}}{1-Q_{\tau}}\right].

(120)

For
D_{N}
- or
E_{N}
-type LST which has
N+1
gauge nodes in the affine quiver, in its trivalent gluing brane webs, a single vertical strip might be shared by more than one gauge node, but we should not double count the strip.
One can show that the total number of independent vertical strips is
N+2
, so we should include
N+2
copies of (
119
) into the perturbative part. This is consistent with the corresponding factors in (
3.1
), (
3.2
) and (
3.3.1
). Including this factor, now we can finally obtain the full partition function.

Before providing the explicit form of the partition function, we summarize the notation and convention for the expression of the full partition function:
we use the capital indices
I,J
to label the nodes in the affine
D_{N}
- or
E_{N}
-type Dynkin diagram. The total number of gauge nodes is
N+1
, so
I,J\in\{1,2,\cdots,N+1\}
. The Cartan matrix is denoted as
C_{IJ}
, where
C_{IJ}=+2
for
I=J
,
C_{IJ}=-1
if
I
-th node and
J
-th node are connected with each other by a single line, and
C_{IJ}=0
if
I
-th node and
J
-th node are not connected. Also, we denote the instanton factor of each gauge node as
Q_{I}
.
Moreover, in order to label the color branes, we use the combination of the capital indices
I,J
and the small indices
k,\ell
. For example, the combination of the indices
I
and
k
indicates the
k
-th color brane inside the
I
-th gauge node. And we use
n_{(I)}
to denote the total number of color branes in the
I
-th gauge node, so
k\in\{1,2,\cdots,n_{(I)}\}
. We denote the exponentiated vertical position of the
k
-th color brane inside the
I
-th gauge node as
y_{I,k}
and the Young diagram assigned to it as
\mu_{I,k}
.

Partition function of
D_{N}
- or
E_{N}
-type LST.

It then follows from (
111
) and (
120
) that the perturbative part of the full partition function of a
D_{N}
- or
E_{N}
-type LST with arbitrary rank is given up a flop by

\displaystyle Z_{\text{pert}}=\text{PE}\left[\frac{\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}\mathfrak{q}}}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\frac{1+Q_{\tau}}{1-Q_{\tau}}\sum_{I,J}\sum_{k,\ell}\sum_{s=\pm 1}\frac{1}{4}C_{IJ}\frac{y_{J,\ell}}{y_{I,k}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}s\,\delta_{IJ}}+\frac{(N+2)Q_{\tau}}{1-Q_{\tau}}\right]\ .

(121)

And from
(
118
), one finds that the instanton part of the full partition function of a
D_{N}
- or
E_{N}
-type LST with arbitrary rank is given by

\displaystyle Z_{\text{inst}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\left(\prod_{I}Q_{I}^{\sum_{k}|\mu_{I,k}|}\right)\prod_{I,J}\prod_{k,\ell}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\varTheta_{\mu_{I,k}\mu_{J,\ell}}\left(\tfrac{y_{I,k}}{y_{J,\ell}}\left(\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}s\,\delta_{IJ}}\Big|Q_{\tau}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}C_{IJ}}.

(122)

Note that the indices
I
and
J
both run all the gauge nodes, and the indices
k
and
\ell
run all the color branes in the
I
-th and
J
-th gauge nodes respectively. However, due to the Cartan matrix
C_{IJ}
, there is no contribution unless
I=J
or
I
-th node and
J
-th node are connected with each other.
Also, note that we need to assume

|Q_{\tau}|<1
and
|\mathfrak{t}|,|\mathfrak{q}|\ll 1

in order for convergence of (
121
).

Also remember that the centers of the Coulomb branch of each gauge node have the same vertical position that is set to be zero point which is concluded at the end of section
4.1
, so the
y
parameters have to satisfy the constraints

\displaystyle\prod_{k=1}^{n_{(I)}}y_{I,k}=1,\quad\text{for }I\in\{1,2,\cdots,N+1\}.

(123)

If we define the distance between
y_{I,j}
and
y_{I,j+1}
as

\displaystyle Q_{I,j}\equiv\frac{y_{I,j}}{y_{I,j+1}},

(124)

then
Q_{I,j}
’s are independent Kähler parameters that solve the constraints (
123
) by

\displaystyle y_{I,k}=\frac{\prod_{i=k}^{n_{(I)}-1}Q_{I,i}}{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n_{(I)}-1}Q_{I,i}^{i}\right)^{\frac{1}{n_{(I)}}}}\ .

(125)

We have confirmed that our formulas (
121
) and (
122
) reproduces all the results discussed in section
3
.

Partition function of
A_{N}
-type LST.

We can also use the formulas (
121
) and (
122
) but with slight modifications to express the partition function of a general
A
-type LST which has affine
A
-type quiver. For
A_{N}
-type LST, we draw the affine
A_{N}
Dynkin diagram and number the nodes as the following:
affine
A_{N}

1
2
3
N-1
N
N+1
.

The schematic picture of the corresponding brane web is in figure
21
, in which we have labelled the vertical positions of all the color branes and the red dashed lines are the centers of Coulomb branch in each gauge node and the leftmost D5-branes are identified with the corresponding rightmost D5-branes to form the
(N+1)
-th gauge node. In order to be vertically periodic, for each gauge node the condition (
73
) has to be satisfied, and due to the fact that the number of leftmost color branes is equal to the number of rightmost color branes by the identification, we can derive that the numbers of color branes in each gauge node are the same, that is

\displaystyle n_{(1)}=n_{(2)}=\cdots=n_{(N)}=n_{(N+1)}\ .

(126)

Then for each gauge node, (
78
), which also needs to be satisfied for the vertical periodicity, reduces to

\displaystyle Q_{\textbf{m}}^{2}=Q_{\textbf{r}}Q_{\textbf{l}}\ ,

(127)

which means that the center of the Coulomb branch of a gauge node is in the middle of the two centers of the Coulomb branch of the two gauge nodes on its left and right. So the distances between the centers of the Coulomb branch of two adjacent gauge nodes are always the same which means the bifundamental masses are all the same, and we denote this distance with
Q_{d}
in figure
21
.

Figure 21:
Schematic picture of brane web realization of
A_{N}
-type LST.

Due to the distance
Q_{d}
between adjacent gauge nodes,
the expressions of vertical positions
y_{I,k}
in terms of independent parameters become

y_{I,k}=Q_{d}^{-I}\frac{\prod_{i=k}^{n_{(I)}-1}Q_{I,i}}{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n_{(I)}-1}Q_{I,i}^{i}\right)^{\frac{1}{n_{(I)}}}},\quad\text{for }I\in\{1,2,\cdots,N+1\}\ ,

(128)

where we have set the leftmost center of Coulomb branch in figure
21
to be the zero point.
The vertical positions of the leftmost color branes are shifted by
Q_{d}^{N+1}
to the rightmost color branes:

\displaystyle\tilde{y}_{N+1,k}=Q_{d}^{N+1}y_{N+1,k}\ ,\quad\text{for }k\in\{1,2,\cdots,n_{(N+1)}\},

(129)

so there are ambiguities in the vertical positions of color branes in the
(N+1)
-th gauge node when we apply the formulas (
121
) and (
122
).
And also for the
A_{N}
-type LST, the number of independent vertical strips is
N+1
rather than
N+2
in the
D_{N},E_{N}
-type case. So the following modifications to formulas (
121
) and (
122
) are needed in order to work for
A_{N}
-type LST:

(1)

We need to use
y_{N+1,k}
for the vertical positions in the
(N+1)
-th gauge node when
I=N,J=N+1
or
I=N+1,J=N
, and we need to use
\tilde{y}_{N+1,k}
for the vertical positions in the
(N+1)
-th gauge node when
I=1,J=N+1
or
I=N+1,J=1
. When
I=J=N+1
, we can use either
y_{N+1,k}
or
\tilde{y}_{N+1,k}
.

(2)

The factor
(N+2)
in (
121
) should be replaced by
(N+1)
.

Substitute the Cartan matrix
C_{IJ}
of affine
A
-type quiver into the modified formulas, we can obtain the partition function of
A
-type LST, and we checked that it agrees with the partition function of
A
-type LST computed in
Hohenegger:2013ala
, we have presented their result in Appendix
C
.

We can interpret our proposal as the Nekrasov partition function for the 6d
ADE
-type quiver gauge theories discussed in
Nekrasov:2012xe
;
Nekrasov:2013xda
. In our proposal, the contribution from the vector fields and from the bifundamental fields are given in a unified manner.
For example, the contribution from the vector multiplets of the
I_{0}
-th gauge node,

\displaystyle\prod_{k,\ell}\left[\varTheta_{\mu_{I_{0},k}\mu_{I_{0},\ell}}\left(\tfrac{y_{I_{0},k}}{y_{I_{0},\ell}}\Big|Q_{\tau}\right)\varTheta_{\mu_{I_{0},k}\mu_{I_{0},\ell}}\left(\tfrac{y_{I_{0},k}}{y_{I_{0},\ell}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}\Big|Q_{\tau}\right)\right]^{-1},

(130)

is obtained from
I=J=I_{0}
in (
122
), where the first factor comes from
s=+1
while the second factor comes from
s=-1
. Also, the contribution from the bifundamental multiplets on the
I_{0}
-th gauge node and the
J_{0}
-th gauge node,

\displaystyle\prod_{k,\ell}\left[\varTheta_{\mu_{I_{0},k}\mu_{J_{0},\ell}}\left(\tfrac{y_{I_{0},k}}{y_{J_{0},\ell}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}\Big|Q_{\tau}\right)\varTheta_{\mu_{J_{0},\ell}\mu_{I_{0},k}}\left(\tfrac{y_{J_{0},\ell}}{y_{I_{0},k}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}\Big|Q_{\tau}\right)\right],

(131)

is obtained from
I=I_{0},J=J_{0}
, which gives the first factor, and from
I=J_{0},J=I_{0}
, which gives the second factor.
The product for
s
is performed trivially and the dummy indices
k
and
\ell
are exchanged for the second factor.

Finally, we comment on some 6d, 5d and 4d SCFTs limits. By taking the limit
Q_{\tau}\to 0
, we obtain the partition functions for 5d affine quiver gauge theories which are also dual to 6d conformal matters on a circle. The perturbative part of the partition function is given up to flop by

\displaystyle Z_{\text{pert}}^{\text{5d}}=\text{PE}\left[\frac{\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}\mathfrak{q}}}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\sum_{I,J}\sum_{k,\ell}\sum_{s=\pm 1}\frac{1}{4}C_{IJ}\frac{y_{J,\ell}}{y_{I,k}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}s\,\delta_{IJ}}\right]\ .

(132)

And the instanton part is given by

\displaystyle Z_{\text{inst}}^{\text{5d}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\left(\prod_{I}Q_{I}^{\sum_{k}|\mu_{I,k}|}\right)\prod_{I,J}\prod_{k,\ell}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{(i,j)\in\mu_{I,k}}

(133)

\displaystyle\qquad\left[\left(\frac{y_{J,\ell}}{y_{I,k}}\mathfrak{t}^{-\left(\mu^{t}_{J,\ell}\right)_{j}+i-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}s\delta_{IJ}}\mathfrak{q}^{-\left(\mu_{I,k}\right)_{i}+j-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}s\delta_{IJ}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right.

(134)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.-\left(\frac{y_{J,\ell}}{y_{I,k}}\mathfrak{t}^{-\left(\mu^{t}_{J,\ell}\right)_{j}+i-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}s\delta_{IJ}}\mathfrak{q}^{-\left(\mu_{I,k}\right)_{i}+j-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}s\delta_{IJ}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]^{-\frac{1}{2}C_{IJ}}

(135)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\left(\prod_{I}Q_{I}^{\sum_{k}|\mu_{I,k}|}\right)\prod_{I,J}\prod_{k,\ell}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{(i,j)\in\mu_{I,k}}

(136)

\displaystyle\qquad\left[2\sinh\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{\beta}}{2}\Big(a_{I,k}-a_{J,\ell}+\big(-(\mu^{t}_{J,\ell})_{j}+i-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}s\delta_{IJ}\big)\epsilon_{2}\right.\right.

(137)

\displaystyle\left.\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\big((\mu_{I,k})_{i}-j+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}s\delta_{IJ}\big)\epsilon_{1}\Big)\right)\right]^{-\frac{1}{2}C_{IJ}}.

(138)

By further taking the limit
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\to 0
while keeping
Q_{I}
as they are,
we obtain the partition function for 4d affine quiver gauge theories. The perturbative part of the partition function is given by

\displaystyle Z_{\text{pert}}^{\text{4d}}=\prod_{I,J}\prod_{k,\ell}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\Gamma_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\left(a_{I,k}-a_{J,\ell}+\frac{1}{2}(1+s\delta_{IJ})(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2})\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}C_{IJ}}\ ,

(139)

up to regularization, where
\Gamma_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}
is the Barnes double gamma function
Barnes
,
which can be regarded as a regularized infinite product

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}(x)\propto\prod_{m,n\geq 1}\left(x-m\epsilon_{1}+(n-1)\epsilon_{2}\right)\ ,

(140)

in the region
\epsilon_{1}<0
,
\epsilon_{2}>0
.
The instanton part is given by

\displaystyle Z_{\text{inst}}^{\text{4d}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\left(\prod_{I}Q_{I}^{\sum_{k}|\mu_{I,k}|}\right)\prod_{I,J}\prod_{k,\ell}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{(i,j)\in\mu_{I,k}}

(141)

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[a_{I,k}-a_{J,\ell}+\Big(-(\mu^{t}_{J,\ell})_{j}+i-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}s\delta_{IJ}\Big)\epsilon_{2}\right.

(142)

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\Big((\mu_{I,k})_{i}-j+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}s\delta_{IJ}\Big)\epsilon_{1}\right]^{-\frac{1}{2}C_{IJ}},

(143)

in which we have used
\sinh({\boldsymbol{\beta}}x)\rightarrow{\boldsymbol{\beta}}x
in the limit
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\to 0
and the
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
factors are extracted out and all cancelled in a similar way that leads to the cancellation of
W
factors in (
113
).

From the
(1,0)

ADE
-type LSTs, we can also obtain 6d
(1,0)

ADE
-type SCFTs
Gadde:2015tra
;
Haghighat:2017vch
by taking the limit
Q_{I_{0}}\rightarrow 0
where
I_{0}
denotes the affine node of the affine
ADE
quiver, which means taking the length of the instanton factor of the affine gauge node to be infinite.
Then the Young diagrams
\mu_{I_{0},k}
of the affine node are restricted to be empty sets in order to have nonzero contributions to the partition function. We compared the instanton partition function in (
122
) at this limit with the computation of elliptic genus of the 6d
(1,0)

ADE
-type SCFTs in
Haghighat:2017vch
where the unrefined limit is taken in the result and the tensor branch parameters of all the gauge nodes are turned off, our result agrees with theirs when we also take unrefined limit and turn off tensor branch parameters.

5
Partition function and symmetry

In this section, we will see the symmetries of
D
-type and
E
-type LSTs in the form of characters by expanding their partition functions which are obtained in the previous section. Although we have constructed brane webs for general
D
-type and
E
-type LSTs, we still need to determine the appropriate parameters to expand the partition function correctly and to represent the fugacities of the symmetry group. Surprisingly, we find that the brane web has all the information of the Weyl group symmetry that the LSTs should have. Then, from the Weyl group symmetry in the brane web, we can determine the appropriate parameters for the fugacities of the symmetry group as well as the appropriate expansion parameters. Moreover, the Weyl group symmetry is also helpful in determining the coefficients of the expansion of the partition function.
In the following, we first give a brief introduction about Weyl group symmetry hidden in brane webs, and then we derive the expansion forms of the partition functions of
D_{4}
,
D_{5}
and
E_{6}
-type LSTs by utilizing the Weyl group symmetry.

5.1
Weyl group symmetry in brane web

One of the ways to see the (global) symmetry of a gauge theory explicitly is to expand its partition function with the expansion parameters being the Coulomb branch parameters, and then the coefficients of the Coulomb branch parameters will turn out to be expressed by the characters of the symmetry group.
In this process, we have to make sure that the Coulomb branch parameters are not affected by the Weyl reflections of the symmetry group, so we have to properly choose the so called invariant Coulomb branch parameters
Mitev:2014jza
;
Hayashi:2019jvx
as the expansion parameters which are invariant under the Weyl reflection. Thus the partition function has Weyl group symmetry as we expect. We will see such Weyl group symmetry after we obtain the expansion of partition function.
However, the Weyl group symmetry can also be seen just from the brane web without computing the partition functions.

For example, a part of the Weyl reflections can be identified as the fiber-base duality
Katz:1997eq
;
Aharony:1997bh
;
Bao:2011rc

in the case of 5d rank-1 gauge theories
Mitev:2014jza
. Another simpler example is exchanging parallel flavor branes on the same side, which corresponds to the Weyl group symmetry of exchanging mass parameters. These examples illustrate that the Weyl reflections of the symmetry group can be seen from the brane web. In this paper, we are dealing with the affine
D
-type and
E
-type quiver gauge theories, and we will find that all the independent Weyl reflections of the symmetry groups of these quiver theories can be identified with certain symmetries of the corresponding brane webs. In the following subsections, we will show that the partition functions have the same symmetries as the web diagrams.

5.2
Expansion of
D_{4}
-type LST

Figure 22:
(a) A quadrivalent gluing brane web of
D_{4}
-type LST. (b) An affine Dynkin diagram of affine
D_{4}
.

For the brane web of
D_{4}
-type LST, as shown in figure
22
(a), each gauge node has two parallel external NS-charged branes
10
10

10

Note that we have identified the top and bottom NS-charged branes, so they are just counted as a single brane.
, so the configuration of the brane web is invariant if we swap the two NS-charged branes. In this swapping, the Kähler parameters will accordingly be transformed. For the
D_{4}
-type LST, we have five gauge nodes, and we will have five Kähler parameter transformations that correspond to the swapping of external NS-charged branes for each of the five nodes.
The five transformations are as follows:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{1}^{-1},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{1};\\
Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}^{-1},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{2};\\
Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{3}^{-1},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{3};\\
Q_{4}\rightarrow Q_{4}^{-1},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{4};\\
Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}^{-1},\ Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{1}Q_{5},\ Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}Q_{5},\ Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{3}Q_{5},\ Q_{4}\rightarrow Q_{4}Q_{5};\end{array}\right.

(144)

where we have omitted the trivial transformations such as
Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}
.
Due to the structure of the brane web in figure
22
(a),
Q_{1}
is only connected to
Q_{5}
. Then, when
Q_{1}
is transformed to
Q_{1}^{-1}
, only
Q_{5}
is affected. However,
Q_{5}
is connected to
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4}
, so when
Q_{5}
is transformed to
Q_{5}^{-1}
,
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4}
are all affected.

On the other hand, we consider the Dynkin diagram of affine
D_{4}
given in figure
22
(b), where we have labeled the five affine simple roots whose indices are in accord with the
Q
’s in figure
22
(a). Via the following formula for (affine) Weyl reflection

\displaystyle r_{\alpha_{i}}\cdot\alpha_{j}=\alpha_{j}-\frac{2(\alpha_{j},\alpha_{i})}{(\alpha_{i},\alpha_{i})}\alpha_{i},

(145)

where
r_{\alpha_{i}}
is the (affine) Weyl reflection corresponding to the simple root
\alpha_{i}
and it acts on the simple root
\alpha_{j}
,
we can easily obtain the five affine Weyl reflections that correspond to the five simple roots:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}r_{\alpha_{1}}:\ \ \alpha_{1}\rightarrow-\alpha_{1},\ \alpha_{5}\rightarrow\alpha_{5}+\alpha_{1};\\
r_{\alpha_{2}}:\ \ \alpha_{2}\rightarrow-\alpha_{2},\ \alpha_{5}\rightarrow\alpha_{5}+\alpha_{2};\\
r_{\alpha_{3}}:\ \ \alpha_{3}\rightarrow-\alpha_{3},\ \alpha_{5}\rightarrow\alpha_{5}+\alpha_{3};\\
r_{\alpha_{4}}:\ \ \alpha_{4}\rightarrow-\alpha_{4},\ \alpha_{5}\rightarrow\alpha_{5}+\alpha_{4};\\
r_{\alpha_{5}}:\ \ \alpha_{5}\rightarrow-\alpha_{5},\ \alpha_{1}\rightarrow\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{5},\ \alpha_{2}\rightarrow\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{5},\ \alpha_{3}\rightarrow\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{5},\ \alpha_{4}\rightarrow\alpha_{4}+\alpha_{5};\end{array}\right.

(146)

where we have omitted the trivial transformations such as
\alpha_{2}\rightarrow\alpha_{2}
.
By comparing (
144
) with (
146
), we realize that the
Q
’s are just the fugacities of affine
D_{4}
in
\alpha
-basis and the five transformations in (
144
) are actually the five Weyl reflections in (
146
).

For usual brane web which is just a single web diagram, we know that its partition function computed by topological vertex formalism is flop invariant up to possible analytic continuation, where the “flop” includes flop transitions which change the configuration of the brane web as well as only swappings of parallel branes which do not change the brane web configuration.
Although we use trivalent and quadrivalent gluing of brane webs in this paper which are not usual brane webs, we will still assume that the partition function of trivalently and quadrivalently glued brane webs is flop invariant up to possible analytic continuation. Now if we start with the brane webs in figure
22
(a) and swap the external NS-charged branes, the partition function
Z^{D_{4}}_{\text{swapped}}
of the swapped brane webs will be the same as the partition function
Z^{D_{4}}_{\text{original}}
of the original brane webs up to possible analytic continuation:

Z^{D_{4}}_{\text{original}}(Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4},Q_{5})=Z^{D_{4}}_{\text{swapped}}\ .

(147)

On the other hand, the partition function
Z^{D_{4}}_{\text{swapped}}
of the swapped brane webs can also be obtained by just replacing the Kähler parameters in the partition function
Z^{D_{4}}_{\text{original}}
of the original brane web by the transformed Kähler parameters via (
144
) due to the invariance of the configuration of the brane webs under swapping external NS-charged branes:

Z^{D_{4}}_{\text{swapped}}=Z^{D_{4}}_{\text{original}}(Q_{1}^{\prime},Q_{2}^{\prime},Q_{3}^{\prime},Q_{4}^{\prime},Q_{5}^{\prime})\ ,

(148)

where
Q_{1}^{\prime},Q_{2}^{\prime},Q_{3}^{\prime},Q_{4}^{\prime},Q_{5}^{\prime}
are the transformed Kähler parameters, for example, if we swap the two NS-charged branes of node 1 in figure
22
, then the transformed Kähler parameters are
Q_{1}^{\prime}=Q_{1}^{-1},Q_{2}^{\prime}=Q_{2},Q_{3}^{\prime}=Q_{3},Q_{4}^{\prime}=Q_{4},Q_{5}^{\prime}=Q_{5}Q_{1}
according to the first line in (
144
) which is the Weyl reflection of
Q_{1}
. Then from (
147
) and (
148
), up to analytic continuation we get

Z^{D_{4}}_{\text{original}}(Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4},Q_{5})=Z^{D_{4}}_{\text{original}}(Q_{1}^{\prime},Q_{2}^{\prime},Q_{3}^{\prime},Q_{4}^{\prime},Q_{5}^{\prime})\ .

(149)

Thus we conclude that:
The partition function of
D_{4}
-type LST is invariant up to possible analytic continuation under the transformations in (
144
) which are all the independent affine Weyl reflections of affine
D_{4}
, so
the partition function of
D_{4}
-type LST has affine
D_{4}
symmetry.

Note that in the above analysis we focus on
Q_{1},\cdots,Q_{5}
which are the fugacities of affine
D_{4}
in
\alpha
-basis, but aside from these parameters there are also other parameters such as Coulomb branch parameters, usually such parameters are also transformed under Weyl reflections. In order to see the symmetry manifestly, we need to choose the proper expansion parameters that are invariant under Weyl reflections and expand the partition function with respect to these invariant expansion parameters, then we will see the characters of the symmetry group in the coefficients of the expansion
Hayashi:2019jvx
. In principle, if we choose the expansion parameters to be invariant under the affine
D_{4}
Weyl reflections, we will see affine
D_{4}
characters in the expansion of partition function. But the affine characters are infinite polynomials that contain infinitely high orders of fugacities, in order to see these infinitely high orders we need to sum over all the horizontal Young diagrams in the brane webs up to infinity which is demanding. So, instead we try to just see the
D_{4}
symmetry whose characters are finite polynomials. Then we should choose the expansion parameters to be invariant under
D_{4}
Weyl reflections. There are two ways to reduce affine
D_{4}
to
D_{4}
in the
D_{4}
-type LST which correspond to two different choices of selecting four Weyl reflections out of the five affine
D_{4}
Weyl reflections.

Choice I.

One choice is to select the Weyl reflections corresponding to
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4}
.
The remaining parameter
Q_{5}
can not be used as a proper expansion parameter since it transforms under these four Weyl reflections non-trivially, so we replace
Q_{5}
by the horizontal period
u
in (
17
)
which is Weyl reflection invariant. Now the four chosen affine Weyl reflections reduce to the following transformations:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{1}^{-1};\\
Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}^{-1};\\
Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{3}^{-1};\\
Q_{4}\rightarrow Q_{4}^{-1}.\end{array}\right.

(150)

They are the Weyl reflections of SU(2) in each SU(1) gauge node, but they do not form the Weyl reflections of
D_{4}
. By looking at the brane web in figure
22
(a), we can see that there is a permutation symmetry among
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4}
,

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{2},\ Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{1};\\
Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{3},\ Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{2};\\
Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{4},\ Q_{4}\rightarrow Q_{3};\end{array}\right.

(151)

because the four SU(1) subdiagrams share the same Coulomb branch parameter with the middle SU(2) subdiagram, and they only differ by their instanton factors
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4}
. If we pick only one of the transformations in (
150
), we can use the permutation symmetry to obtain the other three transformations in (
150
) by using (
151
).
So the SU(2) Weyl reflection and the permutation symmetry form the following symmetry of the theory

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{1}^{-1};\\
Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{2},\ Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{1};\\
Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{3},\ Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{2};\\
Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{4},\ Q_{4}\rightarrow Q_{3}.\end{array}\right.

(152)

From this symmetry, we can obtain the following transformations

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{2},\ Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{1};\\
Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{3},\ Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{2};\\
Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{4},\ Q_{4}\rightarrow Q_{3};\\
Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{4}^{-1},\ Q_{4}\rightarrow Q_{3}^{-1}.\end{array}\right.

(153)

They are the Weyl reflections of
D_{4}
in orthonormal basis fugacities
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4}
.

Note that this method of obtaining the
D_{4}
symmetry is applicable only for the rank-1
D_{4}
-type LST. If we consider higher rank
D_{4}
-type LST, there is no permutation symmetry that only involves
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4}
because each one of the four gauge nodes in the corners will have different Coulomb branch parameters in the higher rank case, and the permutation symmetry will involve both instanton factors and Coulomb branch parameters of the four gauge nodes in the corners.

For convenience in computations, we use the following new Coulomb branch parameter

\displaystyle w_{5}\equiv Q_{5,1}^{1/2}\ .

(154)

w_{5}
is invariant under the Weyl reflections in (
153
), so it is an invariant Coulomb branch parameter and can be used as an expansion parameter. Note that
w_{5}
is not affected by the four SU(2) Weyl reflections in (
150
) either, so the coefficients of
w_{5}
in the expansion of the partition function will also have SU(2) Weyl group symmetries.
Q_{\tau}
is Weyl reflection invariant, so it is also an expansion parameter.

Choice II.

The other choice is to select the Weyl reflections
11
11

11

Of course we can also choose
Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4},Q_{5}
and so on. They are equivalent choices.
correspond to
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5}
, and we replace
Q_{4}
by the horizontal period
u
in (
17
) as the expansion parameter.
The four chosen affine Weyl reflections reduce to the following:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{3}^{-1},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{3};\\
Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}^{-1},\ Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{1}Q_{5},\ Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}Q_{5},\ Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{3}Q_{5};\\
Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{1}^{-1},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{1};\\
Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}^{-1},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{2}.\end{array}\right.

(155)

They are the
D_{4}
Weyl reflections in the basis
\{\alpha_{3},\alpha_{5},\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}
where the
\alpha
’s as well as the corresponding
Q
’s are ordered in a conventional way for
D_{4}
simple roots.

Figure 23:
Transformation of Coulomb branch parameter in gauge node 5.

The Coulomb branch parameter
w_{5}
does not change under the Weyl reflections of
Q_{3},Q_{1},Q_{2}
, but it changes under the Weyl reflection of
Q_{5}
due to the invariance of
\tilde{Q}
, as shown in figure
23
. Including Coulomb branch parameter
w_{5}
, the Weyl reflections become as follows:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{3}^{-1},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{3};\\
Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}^{-1},\ Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{1}Q_{5},\ Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}Q_{5},\ Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{3}Q_{5},\ w_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}w_{5};\\
Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{1}^{-1},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{1};\\
Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}^{-1},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{2}.\end{array}\right.

(156)

The invariant Coulomb branch parameter will take the general form of

Q_{1}^{a}Q_{2}^{b}Q_{3}^{c}Q_{5}^{d}w_{5}\ .

(157)

Then, by substituting the four Weyl reflections in (
156
) into the general form respectively and requiring the invariance under these transformations, we can get four linear equations of
a,b,c,d
, and obtain one solution. According to this solution, we get the invariant Coulomb branch parameter
A

\displaystyle A=Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}^{2}w_{5}\ .

(158)

If we switch from the
\alpha
-basis fugacities
Q
’s to the orthonormal basis fugacities
B
’s, we have

\displaystyle Q_{3}=\frac{B_{1}}{B_{2}},\quad Q_{5}=\frac{B_{2}}{B_{3}},\quad Q_{1}=\frac{B_{3}}{B_{4}},\quad Q_{2}=B_{3}B_{4}\ .

(159)

Then in the orthonormal basis, the
D_{4}
Weyl reflections in (
155
) become as follows:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}B_{1}\rightarrow B_{2},\ B_{2}\rightarrow B_{1};\\
B_{2}\rightarrow B_{3},\ B_{3}\rightarrow B_{2};\\
B_{3}\rightarrow B_{4},\ B_{4}\rightarrow B_{3};\\
B_{3}\rightarrow B_{4}^{-1},\ B_{4}\rightarrow B_{3}^{-1}.\end{array}\right.

(160)

In the following part of this subsection, we will compute the expansion of the partition function of the
D_{4}
-type LST. There are two different ways of doing the expansion due to the two different choices of Weyl reflections.

From (
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle Z^{D_{4}}_{\text{inst}}=\sum_{\mu_{5,1},\mu_{5,2}}\bar{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}^{\text{SU(2)}}(w_{5},Q_{5})

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu_{1,1}}\bar{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{1,1}}(w_{5},Q_{1})\sum_{\mu_{2,1}}\bar{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{2,1}}(w_{5},Q_{2})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu_{3,1}}\bar{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{3,1}}(w_{6},Q_{3})\sum_{\mu_{4,1}}\bar{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{4,1}}(w_{6},Q_{4}),

(161)

where

\displaystyle\bar{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}^{\text{SU(2)}}(w_{5},Q_{5})\equiv\frac{Q_{5}^{|\mu_{5,1}|+|\mu_{5,2}|}}{\prod_{p=1}^{2}\prod_{r=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{5,p}\mu_{5,r}}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{5,r}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\mu_{5,p}\mu_{5,r}}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{5,r}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})},

(162)

\displaystyle\bar{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{1,1}}(w_{5},Q_{1})\equiv Q_{1}^{|\mu_{1,1}|}\frac{\prod_{l=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{1,1}\mu_{5,l}}(\frac{y_{1,1}}{y_{5,l}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})\prod_{p=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{5,p}\mu_{1,1}}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{1,1}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})}{\varTheta_{\mu_{1,1}\mu_{1,1}}(1|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\mu_{1,1}\mu_{1,1}}(\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})}.

(163)

We can observe from (
162
) and (
163
) that

\displaystyle\bar{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}^{\text{SU(2)}}(w_{5},Q_{5})=

\displaystyle\bar{Z}_{\mu_{5,2}\mu_{5,1}}^{\text{SU(2)}}(w_{5}^{-1},Q_{5})\ ,

(164)

\displaystyle\bar{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{1,1}}(w_{5},Q_{1})=

\displaystyle\bar{Z}_{\mu_{5,2}\mu_{5,1}\mu_{1,1}}(w_{5}^{-1},Q_{1})\ ,

(165)

which reveals the Weyl reflection
w_{5}\rightarrow w_{5}^{-1}
of the SU(2) gauge group and the invariance of the brane web configuration under this Weyl reflection.

We extract the zeroth order term of
Q_{5}
from (

\displaystyle Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}^{\bf 0}}\equiv Z^{D_{4}}_{\text{pert}}\cdot

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu_{1,1}}\bar{Z}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{1,1}}(w_{5},Q_{1})\sum_{\mu_{2,1}}\bar{Z}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{2,1}}(w_{5},Q_{2})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu_{3,1}}\bar{Z}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{3,1}}(w_{5},Q_{3})\sum_{\mu_{4,1}}\bar{Z}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{4,1}}(w_{5},Q_{4})\ ,

(167)

\displaystyle Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}}\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}\bar{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}^{\text{SU(2)}}(w_{5},Q_{5})\frac{\sum_{\mu_{1,1}}\bar{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{1,1}}(w_{5},Q_{1})}{\sum_{\mu_{1,1}}\bar{Z}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{1,1}}(w_{5},Q_{1})}\frac{\sum_{\mu_{2,1}}\bar{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{2,1}}(w_{5},Q_{2})}{\sum_{\mu_{2,1}}\bar{Z}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{2,1}}(w_{5},Q_{2})}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\sum_{\mu_{3,1}}\bar{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{3,1}}(w_{5},Q_{3})}{\sum_{\mu_{3,1}}\bar{Z}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{3,1}}(w_{5},Q_{3})}\frac{\sum_{\mu_{4,1}}\bar{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{4,1}}(w_{5},Q_{4})}{\sum_{\mu_{4,1}}\bar{Z}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{4,1}}(w_{5},Q_{4})}\quad.

(168)

Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}}
is the part of partition function that depends on
Q_{5}
.

5.2.1
D_{4}
symmetry from expansion I

\displaystyle Z^{D_{4}}_{u^{\bf 0}}=Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}^{\bf 0}},\qquad Z^{D_{4}}_{u}=Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}}\ ,

(169)

\displaystyle Z^{D_{4}}=Z^{D_{4}}_{u^{\bf 0}}\cdot Z^{D_{4}}_{u}\ .

(170)

Z^{D_{4}}_{u^{\bf 0}}

From the general formula in (

\displaystyle Z^{D_{4}}_{\text{pert}}

\displaystyle=\text{PE}

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{1}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\frac{1+Q_{\tau}}{1-Q_{\tau}}\Big(3(\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q})-4\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}\mathfrak{q}}(Q_{5,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}+Q_{5,1}^{-\frac{1}{2}})+\frac{1}{2}(\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q})(Q_{5,1}+Q_{5,1}^{-1})\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{6Q_{\tau}}{1-Q_{\tau}}\bigg].

(171)

We expand (

) up to first order of
Q_{\tau}
and get

\displaystyle Z^{D_{4}}_{\text{pert}}=~\text{PE}\bigg[

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\bigg(-8\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}\mathfrak{q}}w_{5}+(\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q})(3+w_{5}^{2})+Q_{\tau}\Big(6(1+\mathfrak{t}\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle-8\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}\mathfrak{q}}(w_{5}+w_{5}^{-1})+(\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q})(w_{5}^{2}+w_{5}^{-2})\Big)+\mathcal{O}(Q_{\tau}^{2})\bigg)\bigg],

(172)

The function

\displaystyle~\text{PE}\bigg[\frac{Q_{1}\big(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t}-\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}(w_{5}+w_{5}^{-1})\big)}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}+\frac{Q_{\tau}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}\bigg(\frac{Q_{1}}{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})P_{0}(w_{5})

(174)

\displaystyle+\frac{Q_{1}^{2}}{\mathfrak{q}^{2}\mathfrak{t}^{2}}(\mathfrak{q}^{3}+\mathfrak{q}^{2}\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}^{2}+\mathfrak{t}^{3})P_{0}(w_{5})+\frac{Q_{1}^{3}}{\mathfrak{q}^{3}\mathfrak{t}^{3}}(\mathfrak{q}^{5}+\mathfrak{q}^{4}\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q}^{3}\mathfrak{t}^{2}+\mathfrak{q}^{2}\mathfrak{t}^{3}+\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}^{4}+\mathfrak{t}^{5})P_{0}(w_{5})

where

P_{0}(w_{5})=w_{5}^{-2}(\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}}w_{5}-\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}})(\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}}w_{5}-\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}})(w_{5}-\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}})(\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}w_{5}-1).

(176)

We find that the coefficient of
Q_{\tau}
is an infinite polynomial that has a pattern of a geometric series and can be analytically summed:

\displaystyle\frac{Q_{1}}{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})P_{0}(w_{5})+\frac{Q_{1}^{2}}{\mathfrak{q}^{2}\mathfrak{t}^{2}}(\mathfrak{q}^{3}+\mathfrak{q}^{2}\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}^{2}+\mathfrak{t}^{3})P_{0}(w_{5})

\displaystyle+\frac{Q_{1}^{3}}{\mathfrak{q}^{3}\mathfrak{t}^{3}}(\mathfrak{q}^{5}+\mathfrak{q}^{4}\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q}^{3}\mathfrak{t}^{2}+\mathfrak{q}^{2}\mathfrak{t}^{3}+\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}^{4}+\mathfrak{t}^{5})P_{0}(w_{5})

\displaystyle+\frac{Q_{1}^{4}}{\mathfrak{q}^{4}\mathfrak{t}^{4}}(\mathfrak{q}^{7}+\mathfrak{q}^{6}\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q}^{5}\mathfrak{t}^{2}+\mathfrak{q}^{4}\mathfrak{t}^{3}+\mathfrak{q}^{3}\mathfrak{t}^{4}+\mathfrak{q}^{2}\mathfrak{t}^{5}+\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}^{6}+\mathfrak{t}^{7})P_{0}(w_{5})+\mathcal{O}(Q_{1}^{5})

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{Q_{1}^{n}}{\mathfrak{q}^{n}\mathfrak{t}^{n}}\sum_{i=0}^{2n-1}\mathfrak{q}^{2n-1-i}\mathfrak{t}^{i}\right)P_{0}(w_{5})=\frac{Q_{1}(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})}{(Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}P_{0}(w_{5}),

(177)

so

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu_{1,1}}\bar{Z}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{1,1}}(w_{5},Q_{1})=\text{PE}

\displaystyle+\frac{Q_{\tau}P_{0}(w_{5})}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}\frac{Q_{1}(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})}{(Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}+\mathcal{O}(Q_{\tau}^{2})\Bigg]\ ,

where we have used (

\displaystyle Z^{D_{4}}_{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\resizebox{21479355}{}{$\sum_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}\bar{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}^{\text{SU(2)}}(w_{5},u^{\frac{1}{2}})\frac{\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}(w_{5},Q_{1})}{Q_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}(|\mu_{5,1}|+|\mu_{5,2}|)}}\frac{\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}(w_{5},Q_{2})}{Q_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}(|\mu_{5,1}|+|\mu_{5,2}|)}}\frac{\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}(w_{5},Q_{3})}{Q_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}(|\mu_{5,1}|+|\mu_{5,2}|)}}\frac{\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}(w_{5},Q_{4})}{Q_{4}^{\frac{1}{2}(|\mu_{5,1}|+|\mu_{5,2}|)}}$}.

(186)

By the definition (
185
), we need to sum over the Young diagrams
\mu_{1,1}
to infinity.
Because both the functions
\bar{Z}
in the numerator and denominator are proportional to
Q_{1}^{|\mu_{1,1}|}
, the numerator and denominator will both be infinite polynomials of
Q_{1}
with the leading order being zeroth order. As an example, we compute
\mathcal{Z}_{\text{\o},\{1\}}
expanded up to
Q_{\tau}Q_{1}^{4}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\text{\o},\{1\}}(w_{5},Q_{1})=

\displaystyle\frac{(\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}}w_{5}-\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}})(1+Q_{1})}{(\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{1/4}\sqrt{w_{5}}}+Q_{\tau}\bigg[

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathfrak{t}^{3/2}w_{5}^{3}-\mathfrak{q}^{3/2}}{(\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{3/4}w_{5}^{3/2}}(1+Q_{1})+\frac{Q_{1}}{(\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{5/4}}P_{1}(w_{5})+\frac{Q_{1}^{2}}{(\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{9/4}}(\mathfrak{q}^{2}+\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{t}^{2})P_{1}(w_{5})

\displaystyle+\frac{Q_{1}^{3}}{(\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{13/4}}(\mathfrak{q}^{4}+\mathfrak{q}^{3}\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q}^{2}\mathfrak{t}^{2}+\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}^{3}+\mathfrak{t}^{4})P_{1}(w_{5})+\frac{Q_{1}^{4}}{(\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{17/4}}(\mathfrak{q}^{6}+\mathfrak{q}^{5}\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q}^{4}\mathfrak{t}^{2}

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{q}^{3}\mathfrak{t}^{3}+\mathfrak{q}^{2}\mathfrak{t}^{4}+\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}^{5}+\mathfrak{t}^{6})P_{1}(w_{5})+\mathcal{O}(Q_{1}^{5})\bigg]+\mathcal{O}(Q_{\tau}^{2})\,,

(187)

where

\displaystyle P_{1}(w_{5})=w_{5}^{-5/2}(\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}}w_{5}-\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}})^{2}(\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}}w_{5}-\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}})(w_{5}-\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}})(\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}w_{5}-1)\,.

(188)

From (
5.2.1
), we see that the zeroth order coefficient of
Q_{\tau}
is a finite polynomial of
Q_{1}
while the first order coefficient of
Q_{\tau}
is not finite. By looking at the polynomial of
Q_{1}
in the coefficient of
Q_{\tau}
, we find that it also has a pattern of a geometric series starting from the second term in the square bracket which can be analytically summed:

\displaystyle\frac{Q_{1}}{(\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{5/4}}P_{1}(w_{5})+\frac{Q_{1}^{2}}{(\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{9/4}}(\mathfrak{q}^{2}+\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{t}^{2})P_{1}(w_{5})+\frac{Q_{1}^{3}}{(\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{13/4}}(\mathfrak{q}^{4}+\mathfrak{q}^{3}\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q}^{2}\mathfrak{t}^{2}+\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}^{3}+\mathfrak{t}^{4})

\displaystyle\times P_{1}(w_{5})+\frac{Q_{1}^{4}}{(\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{17/4}}(\mathfrak{q}^{6}+\mathfrak{q}^{5}\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q}^{4}\mathfrak{t}^{2}+\mathfrak{q}^{3}\mathfrak{t}^{3}+\mathfrak{q}^{2}\mathfrak{t}^{4}+\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}^{5}+\mathfrak{t}^{6})P_{1}(w_{5})+\mathcal{O}(Q_{1}^{5})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{Q_{1}^{n}}{(\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{n+\frac{1}{4}}}\sum_{i=0}^{2n-2}\mathfrak{q}^{2n-2-i}\mathfrak{t}^{i}\right)P_{1}(w_{5})=\frac{Q_{1}(1+Q_{1})}{(\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{1/4}(Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}P_{1}(w_{5}),

(189)

so

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\text{\o},\{1\}}(w_{5},Q_{1})=

\displaystyle\frac{(\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}}w_{5}-\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}})(1+Q_{1})}{(\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{1/4}\sqrt{w_{5}}}+Q_{\tau}\bigg[

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathfrak{t}^{3/2}w_{5}^{3}-\mathfrak{q}^{3/2}}{(\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{3/4}w_{5}^{3/2}}(1+Q_{1})+\frac{Q_{1}(1+Q_{1})}{(\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{1/4}(Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}P_{1}(w_{5})\bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(Q_{\tau}^{2}).

(190)

We can easily check that
Q_{1}^{-1/2}\mathcal{Z}_{\text{\o},\{1\}}(w_{5},Q_{1})
is invariant under
Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{1}^{-1}
at the zeroth order and the first order of
Q_{\tau}
, which is also due to the SU(2) Weyl group symmetry in (
150
).

In this way, we can also compute other
\mathcal{Z}
functions.
As an example, we list
\mathcal{Z}_{\{1\},\text{\o}}
below,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\{1\},\text{\o}}(w_{5},Q_{1})=

\displaystyle-\frac{(\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}}w_{5}-\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}})(1+Q_{1})}{(\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{1/4}\sqrt{w_{5}}}+Q_{\tau}\bigg[

\displaystyle\frac{(\mathfrak{q}^{3/2}w_{5}^{3}-\mathfrak{t}^{3/2})(1+Q_{1})}{(\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{3/4}w_{5}^{3/2}}-\frac{Q_{1}(1+Q_{1})}{(\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{1/4}(Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}P_{2}(w_{5})\bigg]+\mathcal{O}(Q_{\tau}^{2}),

(191)

where

\displaystyle P_{2}(w_{5})=

\displaystyle w_{5}^{-5/2}(\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}}w_{5}-\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}})^{2}(\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}}w_{5}-\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}})(w_{5}-\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}})(\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}w_{5}-1).

(192)

\displaystyle-(3\chi_{\bf c}+3\chi_{\bf s}+\chi_{\bf 1}\chi_{\bf c}+\chi_{\bf 1}\chi_{\bf s})\left[\frac{1}{2},4\right]+(\chi_{\bf c}+\chi_{\bf s})\left[\frac{1}{2},5\right]\bigg)\bigg/D,

(198)

\displaystyle d_{1,1,1}=

\displaystyle\bigg((153\chi_{\bf c}^{2}\chi_{\bf s}+153\chi_{\bf c}\chi_{\bf s}^{2}+72\chi_{\bf c}^{3}-57\chi_{\bf 1}\chi_{\bf c}-144\chi_{\bf 2}\chi_{\bf c}-112\chi_{\bf c}+72\chi_{\bf s}^{3}-57\chi_{\bf 1}\chi_{\bf s}

\displaystyle+163\chi_{\bf 1}\chi_{\bf c}+38\chi_{\bf 2}\chi_{\bf c}+16\chi_{\bf c}+163\chi_{\bf 1}\chi_{\bf s}+38\chi_{\bf 2}\chi_{\bf s}+16\chi_{\bf s})[0,2]+(-21\chi_{\bf 1}\chi_{\bf c}

\displaystyle+16\chi_{\bf 2}\chi_{\bf c}-172\chi_{\bf c}-21\chi_{\bf 1}\chi_{\bf s}+16\chi_{\bf 2}\chi_{\bf s}-172\chi_{\bf s})[0,3]+(-16\chi_{\bf 1}\chi_{\bf c}+4\chi_{\bf c}

\displaystyle-16\chi_{\bf 1}\chi_{\bf s}+4\chi_{\bf s})[0,4]+(16\chi_{\bf c}+16\chi_{\bf s})[0,5]+(25\chi_{\bf c}^{2}\chi_{\bf s}+25\chi_{\bf c}\chi_{\bf s}^{2}+8\chi_{\bf c}^{3}

\displaystyle-17\chi_{\bf 1}\chi_{\bf c}-24\chi_{\bf 2}\chi_{\bf c}-16\chi_{\bf c}+8\chi_{\bf s}^{3}-17\chi_{\bf 1}\chi_{\bf s}-24\chi_{\bf 2}\chi_{\bf s}-16\chi_{\bf s})\left[\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\right]

\displaystyle+(-8\chi_{\bf c}^{2}\chi_{\bf s}-8\chi_{\bf c}\chi_{\bf s}^{2}+16\chi_{\bf 1}\chi_{\bf c}-34\chi_{\bf 2}\chi_{\bf c}+16\chi_{\bf 1}\chi_{\bf s}-34\chi_{\bf 2}\chi_{\bf s})\left[\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2}\right]

\displaystyle+(51\chi_{\bf 1}\chi_{\bf c}+8\chi_{\bf 2}\chi_{\bf c}-8\chi_{\bf c}+51\chi_{\bf 1}\chi_{\bf s}+8\chi_{\bf 2}\chi_{\bf s}-8\chi_{\bf s})\left[\frac{1}{2},\frac{5}{2}\right]+(-8\chi_{\bf 1}\chi_{\bf c}

\displaystyle-68\chi_{\bf c}-8\chi_{\bf 1}\chi_{\bf s}-68\chi_{\bf s})\left[\frac{1}{2},\frac{7}{2}\right]+(8\chi_{\bf c}+8\chi_{\bf s})\left[\frac{1}{2},\frac{9}{2}\right]\bigg)\bigg/D,

(199)

where
\chi_{\bf 1},\chi_{\bf 2},\chi_{\bf s}
and
\chi_{\bf c}
are the characters of fundamental weights of
D_{4}
whose expressions are in (
D
) but with the orthonormal basis being
\{Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4}\}
, and
D
is the one defined in (
183
).

From the above listed expressions for
d_{\ell,n,r}
, we see that the form of
d_{\ell,n,1}
is quite different from
d_{\ell,n,0}
, there is a common denominator
D
that appears in
d_{\ell,n,1}
which are the coefficients of
Q_{\tau}^{1}
order terms. We have also encountered similar situation in (
5.2.1
) for
Z^{D_{4}}_{u^{\bf 0}}
. The coefficients of the
Q_{\tau}^{0}
order terms in
Z^{D_{4}}_{u^{\bf 0}}
and
Z^{D_{4}}_{u}
can be understood as Gopakumar-Vafa invariants
Gopakumar:1998ii
;
Gopakumar:1998jq
, but the
Q_{\tau}^{1}
order terms do not look like Gopakumar-Vafa invariants at first sight because of the denominator
D
. This is because we are dealing with
D_{4}
-type LST whose tensor branch parameters
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4}
are not parameters for global symmetry but parameters for local symmetry. In the limit
Q_{\tau}\rightarrow 0
, the vertical direction of the
D_{4}
-type LST is decompactified, and the theory reduces to E-string theory, so the
Q_{\tau}^{0}
terms also coincide with the corresponding terms in the partition function of E-string theory
Kim:2022dbr
. But the
Q_{\tau}^{1}
order terms contain the information of LST at nonzero
Q_{\tau}
, so
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4}
appear as parameters for local symmetry in the
Q_{\tau}^{1}
terms. In order to see the Gopakumar-Vafa invariants at the
Q_{\tau}^{1}
order, we have to further expand the
Q_{\tau}^{1}
terms with respect to
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4}
. We expand
c_{0},c_{1},c_{2},d_{-2,1,1},d_{-1,1,1},d_{0,1,1},d_{1,1,1}
which are all the
Q_{\tau}^{1}
order coefficients in
Z^{D_{4}}_{u^{\bf 0}}
and
Z^{D_{4}}_{u}
we have obtained with respect to
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4}
and arrange
\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q}
in the form of spin content in (
184
), and find that the coefficients of the expansion terms are all positive integers,
so it is consistent with the fact that the Gopakumar-Vafa invariants should be positive integers.

The partition function of rank-1
D_{4}
-type LST was computed in
Kim:2017xan
, where
the authors expanded the partition function in terms of
Q_{1},

Q_{2},

Q_{3},

Q_{4},

Q_{5},

w_{5},
Q_{\tau}
up to
Q_{1}^{2}Q_{2}^{2}Q_{3}^{2}Q_{4}^{2}Q_{5}^{2}w_{5}^{2}Q_{\tau}^{1}
order.
In order to compare our result with
Kim:2017xan
, we re-expanded
Z^{D_{4}}
and we checked our result is consistent with the result of
Kim:2017xan
, up to the order that they computed.

5.2.2
D_{4}
symmetry from expansion II

In choice II, we use
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5}
as the fugacities and
A,u,Q_{\tau}
as the expansion parameters. We call these sets of parameters as new parameters compared with the old parameters
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4},Q_{5},w_{5},Q_{\tau}
.
However, in actual computation of the partition function, it is much more convenient to use the old parameters to do expansions. So we will keep using these old parameters in the intermediate process of computation, but in the final step we will switch to the new parameters in order to see the
D_{4}
symmetry manifestly.

By (
166
), we compute
Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}^{\bf 0}}
and
Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}}
separately.

Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}^{\bf 0}}
part.

We rewrite (
5.2.1
) by (
86
) as

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu_{1,1}}\bar{Z}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{1,1}}(w_{5},Q_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{PE}\left[\frac{Q_{1}(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})-\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}(Q_{1}+Q_{1}^{-1})w_{5}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}+Q_{\tau}P_{0}(w_{5})\frac{Q_{1}(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})}{(Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}+\mathcal{O}(Q_{\tau}^{2})\right].

(200)

By substituting (
5.2.2
) and (
5.2.1
) back to (
5.2
), and expanding up to
Q_{4}Q_{\tau}
order,
we get

\displaystyle Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}^{\bf 0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{PE}\bigg[\frac{1}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}\bigg((\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})(3+Q_{1}+Q_{2}+Q_{3})-\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}w_{5}\big(8+Q_{1}+\frac{1}{Q_{1}}+Q_{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{Q_{2}}+Q_{3}+\frac{1}{Q_{3}}\big)+(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})w_{5}^{2}+Q_{4}\big(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t}-\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}(w_{5}+\frac{1}{w_{5}})\big)+\mathcal{O}(Q_{4}^{2})\bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{Q_{\tau}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}\bigg(6(1+\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})+\big(\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q}^{2}\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q}(1+\mathfrak{t})^{2}\big)(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})\Big(\frac{Q_{1}}{(Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}

\displaystyle+\frac{Q_{2}}{(Q_{2}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{2}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}+\frac{Q_{3}}{(Q_{3}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{3}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}\Big)-\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}\big(w_{5}+\frac{1}{w_{5}}\big)\Big(8

\displaystyle+\frac{Q_{1}(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})(1+\mathfrak{q})(1+\mathfrak{t})}{(Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}+\frac{Q_{2}(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})(1+\mathfrak{q})(1+\mathfrak{t})}{(Q_{2}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{2}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}+\frac{Q_{3}(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})(1+\mathfrak{q})(1+\mathfrak{t})}{(Q_{3}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{3}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}\Big)

\displaystyle+(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})\big(w_{5}^{2}+\frac{1}{w_{5}^{2}}\big)\Big(1+\frac{Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}{(Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}+\frac{Q_{2}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}{(Q_{2}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{2}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}

\displaystyle+\frac{Q_{3}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}{(Q_{3}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{3}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}\Big)+Q_{4}\Big(\frac{(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})\big(\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q}^{2}\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q}(1+\mathfrak{t})^{2}\big)}{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}-\frac{(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})(1+\mathfrak{q})(1+\mathfrak{t})}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}}

\displaystyle\times\big(w_{5}+\frac{1}{w_{5}}\big)+(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})\big(w_{5}^{2}+\frac{1}{w_{5}^{2}}\big)\Big)+\mathcal{O}(Q_{4}^{2})\bigg)+\mathcal{O}(Q_{\tau}^{2})\bigg]\ .

(201)

Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}}
part and rescaling.

From (
168
) and (
185
), we have

\displaystyle Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}}=\sum_{\mu_{5,1},\mu_{5,2}}

\displaystyle\bar{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}^{\text{SU(2)}}(w_{5},Q_{5})\,\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}(w_{5},Q_{1})\,\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}(w_{5},Q_{2})

(202)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}(w_{5},Q_{3})\,\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}(w_{5},Q_{4})\ .

(203)

The summation over
\mu_{5,1},\mu_{5,2}
up to infinity is difficult as the computation will be more and more time consuming when the upper bound of the total Young diagram box number increases, so we expand
Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}}
with respect to
Q_{5}
whose order is restricted by
|\mu_{5,1}|+|\mu_{5,2}|
.
As
Q_{4}
is proportional to
u
, we also expand
Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}}
with respect to
Q_{4}
to obtain the expansion of the partition function with respect to
u
.
Even with the two expansion parameters
Q_{5},Q_{4}
, the computation is still time-consuming, and we need to further expand with respect to
w_{5}
in order to reduce the heavy computation. We first need to expand each of the five factors of the summand in (
202
) with respect to
w_{5}
and then multiply the expansions together to obtain the expansion of
Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}}
with respect to
w_{5}
.
However, unlike
Q_{4},Q_{5}
and
Q_{\tau}
with respect to which the expansions of each of the five factors only have non-negative power terms, there are negative powers appearing in the expansions with respect to
w_{5}
for each of these five factors which will make the total expansion with respect to
w_{5}
for
Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}}
difficult to truncate.
We need to rescale parameters and the five factors in order to have non-negative power expansions with
w_{5}
. We find that the following rescalings,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}

\displaystyle~\rightarrow~~w_{5}^{\frac{1}{2}(|\mu_{5,1}|+|\mu_{5,2}|)}\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}},

\displaystyle\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)}}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}

\displaystyle~\rightarrow~~w_{5}^{-2(|\mu_{5,1}|+|\mu_{5,2}|)}\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)}}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}},

\displaystyle Q_{\tau}

\displaystyle~\rightarrow~~w_{5}^{2}Q_{\tau},

(204)

make
\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)}}
and
\mathcal{Z}
functions up to
Q_{\tau}^{1}
order have non-negative power expansions with respect to
w_{5}
. Then through multiplication and truncation, we can obtain non-negative power expansion of
Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}}
with respect to
w_{5}
and transform it into the corresponding expansion inside PE. Finally, we switch back to the original parameter
Q_{\tau}
inside the PE.

Evaluation of the partition function by Weyl group symmetry.

Before we dive into detailed computation, we would like to discuss the general problems that we would encounter when doing the computation.

If we finally obtain the expansion of
Z^{D_{4}}
inside PE with the expansion parameters being
A,u
and
Q_{\tau}
, the term
A^{\ell}u^{n}Q_{\tau}^{r}
will be of the following form,

\displaystyle b_{\ell,n,r}(Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5})A^{\ell}u^{n}Q_{\tau}^{r},

(205)

where
b_{\ell,n,r}
is function of
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5}
.
From the results in (
5.2.1
) and (
193
) in the last subsection, we know that the
Q_{\tau}^{0}
order coefficients of
Z^{D_{4}}_{u^{\bf 0}}
and
Z^{D_{4}}_{u}
are composed of finite polynomials of
D_{4}
characters, whereas the
Q_{\tau}^{1}
order coefficients of
Z^{D_{4}}_{u^{\bf 0}}
and
Z^{D_{4}}_{u}
are in fractional forms with both numerators and denominators being composed of finite polynomials of
D_{4}
characters. We explained that this is due to the fact that the
D_{4}
fugacities appear as parameters for local symmetry in the
Q_{\tau}^{1}
order terms. We expect that similar things also happen to
b_{\ell,n,r}
.

(1)

For
r=0
which is at
Q_{\tau}^{0}
order, we expect
b_{\ell,n,0}
to be also finite polynomials of
D_{4}
characters with
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5}
as the fugacities. If we focus on
Q_{5}
which is the intermediate expansion parameter,
b_{\ell,n,0}
can be regarded as Laurent polynomials of
Q_{5}
with both negative and non-negative powers. Since

\displaystyle\quad A=Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}^{2}w_{5},\quad u=Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{4}Q_{5}^{2},

(206)

the
A^{\ell}u^{n}
term will be of the following form,

\displaystyle b_{\ell,n,0}(Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5})A^{\ell}u^{n}=b_{\ell,n,0}(Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3})^{\ell+n}Q_{5}^{2\ell+2n}w_{5}^{\ell}Q_{4}^{n}.

(207)

We know that the contribution to
w_{5}^{\ell}Q_{4}^{n}
term inside PE from the
Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}}
part with
Q_{5}
expanded up to
Q_{5}^{k}
order takes the following form,

\displaystyle\Big(``Q_{5}+Q_{5}^{2}+\cdots+Q_{5}^{k}+\cdots"\Big)\,w_{5}^{\ell}Q_{4}^{n}\ .

(208)

Here we introduced a notation “   ” which means that we ignore the coefficients of the polynomial of
Q_{5}
which should be functions of
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3}
, and if some of the coefficients are accidentally zero, the corresponding terms should disappear from (
208
).
In the remaining part of this section, we will use this notation to ignore such coefficients in order to avoid complicated expressions.

In (
208
),
n
is always a non-negative integer whereas
\ell
could be any integer. For
n=0
, we need to flop
w_{5}^{\ell}
terms with
\ell\leq 0
by (
86
) and combine them with
\ell\geq 0
terms, in particular,
\ell=0
term will have
\frac{1}{2}
factor due to the flop and combination. These will bring negative power terms of
Q_{5}
to the
w_{5}^{\ell}
terms with
\ell\geq 0
. There are also possible
Q_{5}^{0}
order contributions to the
w_{5}^{\ell}Q_{4}^{n}
term from the
Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}^{\bf 0}}
part. In total, the
w_{5}^{\ell}Q_{4}^{n}
term inside the PE of
Z^{D_{4}}
will be of the following form,

\displaystyle\Big(``\cdots+1+Q_{5}+Q_{5}^{2}+\cdots+Q_{5}^{k}+\cdots"\Big)\,w_{5}^{\ell}Q_{4}^{n}\ ,

(209)

where the first
\cdots
means possible negative orders of
Q_{5}
because of flop.

Because the right hand side of (
207
) is the general form of
w_{5}^{\ell}Q_{4}^{n}
term that we expect, it should equal to (
209
), thus we have

\displaystyle b_{\ell,n,0}(Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3})^{\ell+n}Q_{5}^{2\ell+2n}=``\cdots+1+Q_{5}+Q_{5}^{2}+\cdots+Q_{5}^{k}+\cdots"\ .

(210)

As
b_{\ell,n,0}
is Laurent polynomial of
Q_{5}
which is finite, the right hand side of (
210
) should also be finite.
So the coefficient of
w_{5}^{\ell}Q_{4}^{n}
does not go up to infinite power of
Q_{5}
but stops at some finite order of
Q_{5}
.
If the highest order of
Q_{5}
in
b_{\ell,n,0}
is
Q_{5}^{j}
, then we just need to expand
Q_{5}
up to
Q_{5}^{j+2\ell+2n}
on the right hand side and the result will be exact, and from the right hand side result, we can then determine
b_{\ell,n,0}
on the left hand side.
Then the issue is how can we know the highest order
j
of
Q_{5}
in
b_{\ell,n,0}
. Instead of considering the highest order
j
, we consider how to determine
b_{\ell,n,0}
without expanding
Q_{5}
up to the highest order on the right hand side.

As we said,
b_{\ell,n,0}
is a finite polynomial of
D_{4}
characters, so we can divide the finite polynomial
b_{\ell,n,0}
into a summation of Weyl group orbits
12
12

12

A Weyl group orbit of
D_{4}
can be constructed as the following: First we start with a monomial
Q_{1}^{i_{0}}Q_{2}^{j_{0}}Q_{3}^{k_{0}}Q_{5}^{l_{0}}
, then we act all possible Weyl reflections on the monomial and it will generate new monomials. All the different monomials(including the original one) form a Weyl group obit of
D_{4}
, we also call the corresponding polynomial a Weyl group orbit.
of
D_{4}
where each Weyl group orbit can be regarded as a Laurent polynomial of
Q_{5}
. If we know one monomial from a Weyl group orbit, we can generate the full Weyl group orbit by Weyl group symmetry. So if we know at least one monomial for each Weyl group orbit of
b_{\ell,n,0}
, we can then determine the full polynomial
b_{\ell,n,0}
. As each Weyl group orbit of
b_{\ell,n,0}
is a Laurent polynomial of
Q_{5}
that contains
Q_{5}^{0}
order term, if we can obtain the complete
Q_{5}^{0}
order term of
b_{\ell,n,0}
, we can completely determine the full polynomial
b_{\ell,n,0}
by Weyl group symmetry. Because of the factor
Q_{5}^{2\ell+2n}
on the left hand side of (
210
), the
Q_{5}^{0}
order term of
b_{\ell,n,0}
is shifted to
Q_{5}^{2\ell+2n}
order on the right hand side.
So we can just compute up to
Q_{5}^{2\ell+2n}
order on the right hand side and use the
Q_{5}^{2\ell+2n}
order term to determine the
Q_{5}^{0}
order term of
b_{\ell,n,0}
. Then we can use Weyl group symmetry to generate all the terms of
b_{\ell,n,0}
.
After that, we will know what is the highest order
j
of
Q_{5}
in
b_{\ell,n,0}
, and we can then compute
Q_{5}
up to
Q_{5}^{j+2\ell+2n}
order on the right hand side to confirm the correctness of
b_{\ell,n,0}
that we have obtained by Weyl group symmetry.

(2)

For
r=1
which is at
Q_{\tau}^{1}
order, we expect that
b_{\ell,n,1}
to be of the following fractional form

\displaystyle b_{\ell,n,1}(Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5})=\frac{\tilde{b}_{\ell,n,1}(Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5})}{\tilde{D}},

(211)

where both
\tilde{b}_{\ell,n,1}
and
\tilde{D}
are finite polynomials of
D_{4}
characters which is similar to the form of
d_{\ell,n,1}
in (
193
). The form of
D
in
d_{\ell,n,1}
is determined and given in (
183
), and it actually comes from the exact summations of the geometric series of
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4}
that we encountered in the last subsection
5.2.1
. Here, we also need to compute the geometric series that involve
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5}
in order to know the explicit form of
\tilde{D}
, but this is difficult for the current case because we have to compute the expansion of
Q_{5}
up to very high order in order to see the pattern of geometric series. There is a more convenient method to determine the explicit form of
\tilde{D}
, but before that let us illustrate how this method works for determining the form of
D
.

Let us first write down the denominator
D
that we found in the choice I case,

\displaystyle D=

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{4}\frac{(Q_{i}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{i}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}{Q_{i}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}\ .

(212)

It is not difficult to explain why it should be in the above form.
First, it has to respect the symmetry in (
152
) which includes both SU(2) Weyl group symmetry and
D_{4}
Weyl group symmetry.
Second, the
\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q}
in the expression after the expansion should have appropriate powers so that the expression can be written as the numerators of the spin contents in (
184
).
As we said,
D
comes from exact summations of the geometric series of
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4}
, in particular, the geometric series of
Q_{1}
contributes the following factor

\displaystyle\frac{(Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}{Q_{1}}\ ,

(213)

which respects the SU(2) Weyl group symmetry
Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{1}^{-1}
. Now, by requiring the highest orders and lowest orders of
\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q}
to have the same absolute values, we need to divide (
213
) by
\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}
, so we get

\displaystyle\frac{(Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}{Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}.

(214)

By further requiring the permutation symmetry among
Q_{1},\cdots,Q_{4}
, we can finally reproduce (
212
). The lesson that we learnt is that the SU(2) Weyl group symmetry of
Q_{1}
together with the permutation symmetry among
Q_{1},\cdots,Q_{4}
generate the group orbit of
Q_{1}
,

\displaystyle\text{orbit}(Q_{1})=\big\{Q_{1},Q_{1}^{-1},Q_{2},Q_{2}^{-1},Q_{3},Q_{3}^{-1},Q_{4},Q_{4}^{-1}\big\}.

(215)

For each pair of elements
\{x,x^{-1}\}\subseteq\text{orbit}(Q_{1})
, there is a corresponding factor whose form is like (
214
) with
Q_{1}
replaced by
x
, and the denominator
D
is just the product of all such factors.

Using this method, now we can also determine the form of
\tilde{D}
. First, we have the same factor (
213
) that comes from the exact summation of the geometric series of
Q_{1}
. Then, by the Weyl group symmetry of the current case from (
155
), the Weyl group orbit of
Q_{1}
is

\displaystyle\text{orbit}(Q_{1})=

\displaystyle\{Q_{1},Q_{1}^{-1},Q_{2},Q_{2}^{-1},Q_{3},Q_{3}^{-1},Q_{5},Q_{5}^{-1},Q_{1}Q_{5},(Q_{1}Q_{5})^{-1},Q_{2}Q_{5},(Q_{2}Q_{5})^{-1},

\displaystyle Q_{3}Q_{5},(Q_{3}Q_{5})^{-1},Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{5},(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{5})^{-1},Q_{1}Q_{3}Q_{5},(Q_{1}Q_{3}Q_{5})^{-1},Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5},

\displaystyle(Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5})^{-1},Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5},(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5})^{-1},Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}^{2},(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}^{2})^{-1}\},

(216)

so we have

\displaystyle\tilde{D}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathfrak{q}^{12}\mathfrak{t}^{12}Q_{1}^{6}Q_{2}^{6}Q_{3}^{6}Q_{5}^{10}}(Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{2}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{2}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{3}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{3}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle\times(Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{1}Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{2}Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{2}Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle\times(Q_{3}Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{3}Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{1}Q_{3}Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})

\displaystyle\times(Q_{1}Q_{3}Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})

\displaystyle\times(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}^{2}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}^{2}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q}).

(217)

Transforming to orthonormal basis by (
159
),
\tilde{D}
takes the following form

\displaystyle\tilde{D}=\prod_{i=1}^{4}\prod_{j>i}^{4}\frac{(B_{i}B_{j}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(B_{i}B_{j}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(B_{i}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(B_{i}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}{(B_{i}B_{j}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{2}}.

(218)

Now for
A^{\ell}u^{n}Q_{\tau}^{1}
term, we have

\displaystyle b_{\ell,n,1}(Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5})A^{\ell}u^{n}Q_{\tau}^{1}=\frac{\tilde{b}_{\ell,n,1}(Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5})}{\tilde{D}}(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3})^{\ell+n}Q_{5}^{2\ell+2n}w_{5}^{\ell}Q_{4}^{n}Q_{\tau}^{1},

(219)

and also like (
209
), the
w_{5}^{\ell}Q_{4}^{n}Q_{\tau}^{1}
term should be

\displaystyle\big(``1+Q_{5}+Q_{5}^{2}+\cdots+Q_{5}^{k}+\cdots"\big)\,w_{5}^{\ell}Q_{4}^{n}Q_{\tau}^{1}.

(220)

Note that there is no negative order of
Q_{5}
in the above polynomial of
Q_{5}
because the power of
Q_{\tau}
is now positive and so we do not do any flop.

By equating the right hand side of (
219
) with (
220
), we have

\displaystyle\tilde{b}_{\ell,n,1}(Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5})\mathfrak{q}^{12}\mathfrak{t}^{12}(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3})^{6+\ell+n}Q_{5}^{10+2\ell+2n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(``1+Q_{5}+Q_{5}^{2}+\cdots+Q_{5}^{k}+\cdots"\big)\,(Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{2}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{2}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{3}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})

\displaystyle\times(Q_{3}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{1}Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{2}Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{2}Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle\times(Q_{3}Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{3}Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{1}Q_{3}Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})

\displaystyle\times(Q_{1}Q_{3}Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})

\displaystyle\times(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}^{2}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}^{2}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q}).

(221)

From (
5.2.2
), in order to obtain the
Q_{5}^{0}
order terms of
\tilde{b}_{\ell,n,1}
, the expansion of
Q_{5}
has to be taken up to
k=10+2\ell+2n
.
After that, we can determine the complete form of
\tilde{b}_{\ell,n,1}
by computing the Weyl group orbits of the
Q_{5}^{0}
order terms of
\tilde{b}_{\ell,n,1}
. But due to the difficulty of computation up to higher order of
Q_{5}
, obtaining exact
\tilde{b}_{\ell,n,1}
is difficult, and we will only compute
Q_{\tau}^{0}
order for the partition function.

Final result of
Z^{D_{4}}
.

Combining
Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}^{\bf 0}}
with
Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}}
,
we express
Z^{D_{4}}
as PE form with the expansion parameters being
A,u,Q_{\tau}
,

\displaystyle Z^{D_{4}}=Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}^{\bf 0}}Z^{D_{4}}_{Q_{5}}=\text{PE}\bigg[\sum_{\ell,n,r}b_{\ell,n,r}A^{\ell}u^{n}Q_{\tau}^{r}\bigg]\ ,

(222)

and compute up to
A^{1}u^{1}Q_{\tau}^{0}
order with the expansion of
Q_{5}
up to
Q_{5}^{4}
order. The corresponding coefficients are listed in the following,

\displaystyle b_{0,0,0}=\frac{1}{2}(2+\chi_{\bf 2})\left[0,\frac{1}{2}\right],

(223)

\displaystyle b_{1,0,0}=(\chi_{\bf 1}^{2}+2\chi_{\bf 2}+\chi_{\bf s}^{2}+\chi_{\bf c}^{2})[0,0]+\bigg[\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\bigg],

(224)

\displaystyle b_{-1,1,0}=[0,0],

(225)

\displaystyle b_{0,1,0}=\chi_{\bf 2}\bigg[0,\frac{1}{2}\bigg]+2\bigg[\frac{1}{2},0\bigg],

(226)

\displaystyle b_{1,1,0}=(-3+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}2\chi_{\bf 1}^{2}-4\chi_{\bf 2}+\chi_{\bf 2}^{2}+6\chi_{\bf 1}\chi_{\bf s}\chi_{\bf c}+2\chi_{\bf s}^{2}+2\chi_{\bf c}^{2}})[0,0]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+(1+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\chi_{\bf 1}^{2}+2\chi_{\bf 2}+\chi_{\bf s}^{2}+\chi_{\bf c}^{2}})\bigg[\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\bigg]+[1,1],

(227)

where
\chi_{\bf 1},\chi_{\bf 2},\chi_{\bf s},\chi_{\bf c}
are the characters of fundamental weights of
D_{4}
which will be in the form of (
D
) if we switch to the orthonormal basis by (
159
).
We remark here that in (
227
), we wrote some terms in red to denote that those are terms obtained by the Weyl group symmetry rather than the output of a direct computation.
Due to demanding computations, we restrict ourselves to the expansion of
Q_{5}
up to order
Q_{5}^{4}
which is just enough to determine the
Q_{5}^{0}
order terms in

. The highest order among these characters in red is
Q_{5}^{4}
, so by (

) we need to compute the
Q_{5}
expansion up to
