to confirm these characters in red, which is computationally demanding.

5.3
Expansion of
D_{5}
-type LST

Just like
D_{4}
-type LST, swapping the external NS-charged branes of each gauge node of the brane web of
D_{5}
-type LST corresponds to the Weyl reflection of the corresponding simple root in the affine
D_{5}
Dynkin diagram. The Weyl reflections from the brane web of
D_{5}
-type LST as shown in figure
24
(a) are as follows:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{1}^{-1},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{1};\\
Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}^{-1},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{2};\\
Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{3}^{-1},\ Q_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}Q_{3};\\
Q_{4}\rightarrow Q_{4}^{-1},\ Q_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}Q_{4};\\
Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}^{-1},\ Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{1}Q_{5},\ Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}Q_{5},\ Q_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}Q_{5};\\
Q_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}^{-1},\ Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{3}Q_{6},\ Q_{4}\rightarrow Q_{4}Q_{6},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{6}.\end{array}\right.

(228)

Figure 24:
(a) Trivalent gluing brane web of
D_{5}
-type LST. (b) Affine Dynkin diagram of affine
D_{5}
.

By similar argument like the one that leads to (
149
) in the
D_{4}
-type LST case, we conclude that
the partition function of
D_{5}
-type LST has affine
D_{5}
symmetry
. Again, in order to see the affine
D_{5}
characters manifestly in the expansion of the partition function, we have to sum over all the Young diagrams of all the color branes up to infinity, which is very demanding. So instead, we try to see the
D_{5}
characters by using five of the six independent affine
D_{5}
Weyl reflections. But unlike the rank-1
D_{4}
-type LST case, here there is only one choice to reduce affine
D_{5}
Weyl reflections to
D_{5}
Weyl reflections. We pick the affine Weyl reflections
13
13

13

Of course we can also pick
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{4},Q_{5},Q_{6}
and so on which are equivalent choices.
of
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5},Q_{6}
, and then
Q_{4}
will be replaced by the horizontal period
u
in (
45
)
as Weyl reflection invariant parameter. The five chosen affine
D_{5}
Weyl reflections reduce to the following forms:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{3}^{-1},\ Q_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}Q_{3};\\
Q_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}^{-1},\ Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{3}Q_{6},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{6};\\
Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}^{-1},\ Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{1}Q_{5},\ Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}Q_{5},\ Q_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}Q_{5};\\
Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{1}^{-1},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{1};\\
Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}^{-1},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{2}.\end{array}\right.

(229)

They are the
D_{5}
Weyl reflections in the basis
\{\alpha_{3},\alpha_{6},\alpha_{5},\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}
where the
\alpha
’s as well as the corresponding
Q
’s are ordered in a conventional way for
D_{5}
simple roots.

For
D_{5}
-type LST, we have two Coulomb branch parameters
Q_{5,1},Q_{6,1}
as shown in figure
13
. For convenience in computation, we use the following new Coulomb branch parameters
w_{5}
and
w_{6}
,

\displaystyle w_{5}\equiv Q_{5,1}^{1/2},\qquad w_{6}\equiv Q_{6,1}^{1/2}.

(230)

w_{5}
does not change under the Weyl reflections of
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{6}
but it changes under the Weyl reflection of
Q_{5}
due to the invariance of
\tilde{Q}
and
Q_{6,1}
as shown in figure
25
, whereas
w_{6}
does not change under the Weyl reflections of
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5}
but it changes under the Weyl reflection of
Q_{6}
due to the invariance of
\bar{Q}
and
Q_{5,1}
as shown in figure
26
.

Figure 25:
Transformation of gauge node 5.

Figure 26:
Transformation of gauge node 6.

Including the Coulomb branch parameters
w_{5}
and
w_{6}
, the Weyl reflections become as follows:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{3}^{-1},\ Q_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}Q_{3};\\
Q_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}^{-1},\ Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{3}Q_{6},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{6},\ w_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}w_{6};\\
Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}^{-1},\ Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{1}Q_{5},\ Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}Q_{5},\ Q_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}Q_{5},\ w_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}w_{5};\\
Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{1}^{-1},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{1};\\
Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}^{-1},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{2}.\end{array}\right.

(231)

The above transformations involve
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5},Q_{6},w_{5},w_{6}
, so the invariant Coulomb branch parameters should be a combination of these parameters which take the general form of

Q_{1}^{a}Q_{2}^{b}Q_{3}^{c}Q_{5}^{d}Q_{6}^{e}w_{5}^{f}w_{6}^{g}.

(232)

By requiring this general form to be invariant under the above five transformations, we obtain five linear equations about
a,b,c,d,e,f,g
. There are two independent invariant Coulomb branch parameters that are proportional to
w_{5}
(i.e.,
f=1,g=0
) and
w_{6}
(i.e.,
f=0,g=1
) respectively. For each case we can solve the five linear equations and obtain one solution, so we get the following solutions which are the invariant Coulomb branch parameters

\displaystyle A_{1}=Q_{1}^{\frac{3}{2}}Q_{2}^{\frac{3}{2}}Q_{3}Q_{5}^{3}Q_{6}^{2}w_{5},\quad A_{2}=Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}^{2}Q_{6}^{2}w_{6}\ .

(233)

If we switch from the
\alpha
-basis fugacities
Q
’s to the orthonormal basis fugacities
B
’s, we have

\displaystyle Q_{3}=\frac{B_{1}}{B_{2}},\ Q_{6}=\frac{B_{2}}{B_{3}},\ Q_{5}=\frac{B_{3}}{B_{4}},\ Q_{1}=\frac{B_{4}}{B_{5}},\ Q_{2}=B_{4}B_{5}.

(234)

Then in the orthonormal basis, the
D_{5}
Weyl reflections in (
229
) become as follows:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}B_{1}\rightarrow B_{2},\ B_{2}\rightarrow B_{1};\\
B_{2}\rightarrow B_{3},\ B_{3}\rightarrow B_{2};\\
B_{3}\rightarrow B_{4},\ B_{4}\rightarrow B_{3};\\
B_{4}\rightarrow B_{5},\ B_{5}\rightarrow B_{4};\\
B_{4}\rightarrow B_{5}^{-1},\ B_{5}\rightarrow B_{4}^{-1}.\end{array}\right.

(235)

Like in section
5.2.2
, it is most convenient to keep using the old parameters
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4},Q_{5},Q_{6},w_{5},w_{6},Q_{\tau}
when computing the partition function of
D_{5}
-type LST in the intermediate process and in particular pick
Q_{5},Q_{6}
as the main expansion parameters, and switch to the new set of parameters
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5},Q_{6},A_{1},A_{2},u,Q_{\tau}
in the final step in order to see the
D_{5}
characters manifestly.

We separate the partition function into
Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}^{\bf 0}Q_{6}^{\bf 0}}
part which is the
Q_{5}^{0}Q_{6}^{0}
order term in the full partition function and
Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}Q_{6}}
part which is the remaining part that depends on
Q_{5},Q_{6}
after extracting out
Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}^{\bf 0}Q_{6}^{\bf 0}}
,

\displaystyle Z^{D_{5}}=Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}^{\bf 0}Q_{6}^{\bf 0}}Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}Q_{6}}\ .

(236)

From (
51
) and (
3.2
), the
Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}^{\bf 0}Q_{6}^{\bf 0}}
part that does not depend on
Q_{5},Q_{6}
is

\displaystyle Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}^{\bf 0}Q_{6}^{\bf 0}}=Z^{D_{5}}_{\text{pert}}\cdot

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu_{1,1}}\bar{Z}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{1,1}}(w_{5},Q_{1})\sum_{\mu_{2,1}}\bar{Z}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{2,1}}(w_{5},Q_{2})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu_{3,1}}\bar{Z}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{3,1}}(w_{6},Q_{3})\sum_{\mu_{4,1}}\bar{Z}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{4,1}}(w_{6},Q_{4})\,,

(237)

the
Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}Q_{6}}
part that depend on
Q_{5},Q_{6}
is

\displaystyle Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}Q_{6}}=\sum_{\mu_{5,1},\mu_{5,2},\mu_{6,1},\mu_{6,2}}

\displaystyle\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)-SU(2)}}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}(w_{5},w_{6},Q_{5},Q_{6})\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}(w_{5},Q_{1})\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}(w_{5},Q_{2})

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}(w_{6},Q_{3})\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}(w_{6},Q_{4}),

(238)

where

\displaystyle\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)-SU(2)}}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}(w_{5},w_{6},Q_{5},Q_{6})

\displaystyle\equiv Q_{5}^{|\mu_{5,1}|+|\mu_{5,2}|}\prod_{p=1}^{2}\bigg(\frac{\prod_{r=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{5,p}\mu_{6,r}}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{6,r}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})}{\prod_{c=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{5,p}\mu_{5,c}}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{5,c}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\mu_{5,p}\mu_{5,c}}(\frac{y_{5,p}}{y_{5,c}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})}\bigg)

\displaystyle~\times Q_{6}^{|\mu_{6,1}|+|\mu_{6,2}|}\prod_{s=1}^{2}\bigg(\frac{\prod_{d=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{6,s}\mu_{5,d}}(\frac{y_{6,s}}{y_{5,d}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})}{\prod_{e=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{6,s}\mu_{6,e}}(\frac{y_{6,s}}{y_{6,e}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\mu_{6,s}\mu_{6,e}}(\frac{\mu_{6,s}}{\mu_{6,e}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})}\bigg)\ ,

(239)

and the
\mathcal{Z}
’s are the same functions that is defined in (
185
).

Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}^{\bf 0}Q_{6}^{\bf 0}}
part.

From the general formula in (
121
), we get the perturbative part of the partition function of
D_{5}
-type LST,

\displaystyle Z^{D_{5}}_{\text{pert}}=

\displaystyle~\text{PE}\bigg[\frac{1}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\frac{1+Q_{\tau}}{1-Q_{\tau}}\Big((\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})\big(4+\frac{1}{2}(w_{5}^{2}+\frac{1}{w_{5}^{2}})+\frac{1}{2}(w_{6}^{2}+\frac{1}{w_{6}^{2}})\big)

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}\mathfrak{q}}\big(2(w_{5}+\frac{1}{w_{5}})+2(w_{6}+\frac{1}{w_{6}})+w_{5}w_{6}+\frac{1}{w_{5}w_{6}}+\frac{w_{5}}{w_{6}}+\frac{w_{6}}{w_{5}}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle+7\frac{Q_{\tau}}{1-Q_{\tau}}\bigg]\ .

(240)

Substitute (
5.3
) and (
5.2.2
) into (
5.3
) and expand up to
Q_{\tau}Q_{4}
order, we get

\displaystyle Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}^{\bf 0}Q_{6}^{\bf 0}}=

\displaystyle~\text{PE}\bigg[\frac{1}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\bigg((\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}+Q_{2}+Q_{3}+4)-\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}\mathfrak{q}}\Big((Q_{1}+\frac{1}{Q_{1}}+Q_{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{Q_{2}}+4)w_{5}+(Q_{3}+\frac{1}{Q_{3}}+4)w_{6}+2w_{5}w_{6}+2\frac{w_{5}}{w_{6}}\Big)+Q_{4}\Big((\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}(w_{6}+\frac{1}{w_{6}})\Big)+\mathcal{O}(Q_{4}^{2})\bigg)+\frac{Q_{\tau}}{(1-\mathfrak{t})(1-\mathfrak{q})}\bigg(7(\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}+1)+\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t}

\displaystyle+(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})\big(\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q}^{2}\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q}(1+\mathfrak{t})^{2}\big)\Big(\frac{Q_{1}}{(Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}+\frac{Q_{2}}{(Q_{2}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{2}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}

\displaystyle+\frac{Q_{3}}{(Q_{3}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{3}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}\Big)-\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}\Big((w_{5}+\frac{1}{w_{5}})\big(4+\frac{Q_{1}(1+\mathfrak{q})(1+\mathfrak{t})(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})}{(Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}

\displaystyle+\frac{Q_{2}(1+\mathfrak{q})(1+\mathfrak{t})(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})}{(Q_{2}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{2}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}\big)+(w_{6}+\frac{1}{w_{6}})\big(4+\frac{Q_{3}(1+\mathfrak{q})(1+\mathfrak{t})(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})}{(Q_{3}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{3}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}\big)

\displaystyle+2(w_{5}w_{6}+\frac{1}{w_{5}w_{6}}+\frac{w_{5}}{w_{6}}+\frac{w_{6}}{w_{5}})\Big)+(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})\Big((w_{5}^{2}+\frac{1}{w_{5}^{2}})\big(\frac{Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}{(Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}

\displaystyle+\frac{Q_{2}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}{(Q_{2}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{2}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}+1\big)+(w_{6}^{2}+\frac{1}{w_{6}^{2}})\big(\frac{Q_{3}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}{(Q_{3}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{3}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})}+1\big)\Big)

\displaystyle+Q_{4}\Big(\frac{(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})\big(\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q}^{2}\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{q}(1+\mathfrak{t})^{2}\big)}{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}-\frac{(1+\mathfrak{q})(1+\mathfrak{t})(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}}(w_{6}+\frac{1}{w_{6}})

\displaystyle+(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})(w_{6}^{2}+\frac{1}{w_{6}^{2}})\Big)+\mathcal{O}(Q_{4}^{2})\bigg)+\mathcal{O}(Q_{\tau}^{2})\bigg].

(241)

Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}Q_{6}}
part and rescaling.

To compute
Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}Q_{6}}
in (
5.3
), we expand with respect to
Q_{5},Q_{6}
whose orders are restricted by
|\mu_{5,1}|+|\mu_{5,2}|,|\mu_{6,1}|+|\mu_{6,2}|
and with respect to
Q_{4}
which is proportional to
u
.
Like in the
D_{4}
-type LST case, in order to reduce the heavy computation
we need to further expand
Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}Q_{6}}
with respect to
w_{5},w_{6}
up to finite order. To obtain such expansion of
Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}Q_{6}}
, it is easier to expand the five factors in the summand of (
5.3
) first and then multiply and truncate the expansions.
In order to make the truncation easy, we need to rescale
\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)-SU(2)}}
,
\mathcal{Z}
functions, and parameters in (
5.3
).
We find that the following rescalings,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}\rightarrow w_{5}^{\frac{1}{2}(|\mu_{5,1}|+|\mu_{5,2}|)}\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}},

(242)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}\rightarrow w_{6}^{\frac{1}{2}(|\mu_{6,1}|+|\mu_{6,2}|)}\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}},

\displaystyle\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)-SU(2)}}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}\rightarrow w_{5}^{-(|\mu_{5,1}|+|\mu_{5,2}|)}w_{6}^{-(|\mu_{6,1}|+|\mu_{6,2}|)}\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)-SU(2)}}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}},

\displaystyle Q_{5}\rightarrow w_{6}Q_{5},\qquad Q_{6}\rightarrow w_{5}Q_{6},\qquad Q_{\tau}\rightarrow w_{5}^{2}w_{6}^{2}Q_{\tau},

(243)

make
\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)-SU(2)}}
and
\mathcal{Z}
functions have non-negative power expansions with respect to
w_{5},w_{6}
. After obtaining the PE form of
Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}Q_{6}}
expanded with respect to
w_{5},w_{6}
under the rescalings (
242
), we switch back to the original parameters
Q_{5},Q_{6},Q_{\tau}
in the PE form.

Evaluation of the partition function by Weyl group symmetry.

For the
A_{1}^{\ell}A_{2}^{m}u^{n}Q_{\tau}^{r}
term in the PE of
Z^{D_{5}}
, it will be of the following form,

\displaystyle b_{\ell,m,n,r}(Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5},Q_{6})A_{1}^{\ell}A_{2}^{m}u^{n}Q_{\tau}^{r}.

(244)

(1)

For
r=0
which is at
Q_{\tau}^{0}
order, we expect
b_{\ell,m,n,0}
to be finite polynomials of
D_{5}
characters with
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5},Q_{6}
as the fugacities. If we focus on
Q_{5},Q_{6}
which are the intermediate expansion parameters,
b_{\ell,m,n,0}
can be regarded as Laurent polynomials of
Q_{5},Q_{6}
with both negative and non-negative powers. In particular, all the Weyl group orbits of the polynomial
b_{\ell,m,n,0}
are also Laurent polynomials of
Q_{5},Q_{6}
and they all contain
Q_{5}^{0}Q_{6}^{0}
order terms. So if we can get the complete
Q_{5}^{0}Q_{6}^{0}
order terms of
b_{\ell,m,n,0}
, we can completely determine the full polynomial
b_{\ell,m,n,0}
by Weyl group symmetry.

In (
244
), we switch to the parameters
w_{5},w_{6},Q_{4}
by (
233
) and (
45
), then we get

\displaystyle b_{\ell,m,n,0}(Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5},Q_{6})A_{1}^{\ell}A_{2}^{m}u^{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle b_{\ell,m,n,0}(Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5},Q_{6})(Q_{1}Q_{2})^{\frac{3}{2}\ell+m+n}Q_{3}^{\ell+m+n}Q_{5}^{3\ell+2m+2n}Q_{6}^{2\ell+2m+2n}w_{5}^{\ell}w_{6}^{m}Q_{4}^{n}\ .

(245)

On the other hand, from the actual computation, we would get

\displaystyle\big(``\cdots+1+Q_{5}+Q_{6}+\cdots+Q_{5}^{k_{1}}Q_{6}^{k_{2}}+\cdots"\big)\,w_{5}^{\ell}w_{6}^{m}Q_{4}^{n},

(246)

if we sum over the Young diagrams up to
|\mu_{5,1}|+|\mu_{5,2}|\leq k_{1},|\mu_{6,1}|+|\mu_{6,2}|\leq k_{2}
.
By equating the right hand side of (
5.3
) with (
246
), we have

\displaystyle b_{\ell,m,n,0}(Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{5},Q_{6})(Q_{1}Q_{2})^{\frac{3}{2}\ell+m+n}Q_{3}^{\ell+m+n}Q_{5}^{3\ell+2m+2n}Q_{6}^{2\ell+2m+2n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle``\cdots+1+Q_{5}+Q_{6}+\cdots+Q_{5}^{k_{1}}Q_{6}^{k_{2}}+\cdots"\,.

(247)

In order to obtain the
Q_{5}^{0}Q_{6}^{0}
order terms of
b_{\ell,m,n,0}
, we need to expand
Q_{5},Q_{6}
on the right hand side of (
5.3
) up to
k_{1}=3\ell+2m+2n,k_{2}=2\ell+2m+2n
. Then we can use Weyl group symmetry to obtain the full terms of
b_{\ell,m,n,0}
from its
Q_{5}^{0}Q_{6}^{0}
order terms. After that we will know the highest order of
Q_{5},Q_{6}
in
b_{\ell,m,n,0}
, and we can increase the expansion orders of
Q_{5},Q_{6}
to necessary orders on the right hand side of (
5.3
) to confirm the correctness of the result of
b_{\ell,m,n,0}
that is obtained by Weyl group symmetry.

(2)

For
r=1
which is at
Q_{\tau}^{1}
order, like in the
D_{4}
-type LST case, we expect that there will be a common denominator
D
appearing in the
Q_{\tau}^{1}
order, and
b_{\ell,m,n,1}
will be in the form of a finite polynomial
\tilde{b}_{\ell,m,n,1}
divided by
D
,

\displaystyle b_{\ell,m,n,1}=\frac{\tilde{b}_{\ell,m,n,1}}{D}.

(248)

The denominator
D
of the current
D_{5}
-type LST case has the same factor (
213
) which comes from the exact summation of the geometric series of
Q_{1}
, so we can start from it and use the
D_{5}
Weyl group symmetry (
229
) to generate all the factors of
D
. The Weyl group orbit of
Q_{1}
is

\displaystyle\text{orbit}(Q_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{Q_{1},Q_{1}^{-1},Q_{2},Q_{2}^{-1},Q_{3},Q_{3}^{-1},Q_{5},Q_{5}^{-1},Q_{6},Q_{6}^{-1},Q_{1}Q_{5},(Q_{1}Q_{5})^{-1},Q_{2}Q_{5},(Q_{2}Q_{5})^{-1},

\displaystyle Q_{3}Q_{6},(Q_{3}Q_{6})^{-1},Q_{5}Q_{6},(Q_{5}Q_{6})^{-1},Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{5},(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{5})^{-1},Q_{1}Q_{5}Q_{6},(Q_{1}Q_{5}Q_{6})^{-1},

\displaystyle Q_{2}Q_{5}Q_{6},(Q_{2}Q_{5}Q_{6})^{-1},Q_{3}Q_{5}Q_{6},(Q_{3}Q_{5}Q_{6})^{-1},Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{5}Q_{6},(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{5}Q_{6})^{-1},

\displaystyle Q_{1}Q_{3}Q_{5}Q_{6},(Q_{1}Q_{3}Q_{5}Q_{6})^{-1},Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}Q_{6},(Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}Q_{6})^{-1},Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{5}^{2}Q_{6},(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{5}^{2}Q_{6})^{-1},

\displaystyle Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}Q_{6},(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}Q_{6})^{-1},Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}^{2}Q_{6},(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}^{2}Q_{6})^{-1},Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}^{2}Q_{6}^{2},

\displaystyle(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}^{2}Q_{6}^{2})^{-1}\},

(249)

so we find

\displaystyle D=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathfrak{q}^{20}\mathfrak{t}^{20}Q_{1}^{10}Q_{2}^{10}Q_{3}^{8}Q_{5}^{18}Q_{6}^{14}}(Q_{1}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{2}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{2}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{3}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{3}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle\times(Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{1}Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{2}Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{2}Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle\times(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{5}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{5}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{6}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{6}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{3}Q_{6}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{3}Q_{6}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle\times(Q_{5}Q_{6}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{5}Q_{6}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{1}Q_{5}Q_{6}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}Q_{5}Q_{6}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{2}Q_{5}Q_{6}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})

\displaystyle\times(Q_{2}Q_{5}Q_{6}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{5}Q_{6}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{5}Q_{6}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{3}Q_{5}Q_{6}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})

\displaystyle\times(Q_{3}Q_{5}Q_{6}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{1}Q_{3}Q_{5}Q_{6}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}Q_{3}Q_{5}Q_{6}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}Q_{6}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})

\displaystyle\times(Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}Q_{6}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}Q_{6}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}Q_{6}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{5}^{2}Q_{6}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})

\displaystyle\times(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{5}^{2}Q_{6}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}^{2}Q_{6}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}^{2}Q_{6}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle\times(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}^{2}Q_{6}^{2}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}Q_{5}^{2}Q_{6}^{2}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q}).

(250)

Transformed into the orthonormal basis by (
234
),
D
takes the following form,

\displaystyle D=\prod_{i=1}^{5}\prod_{j>i}^{5}\frac{(B_{i}B_{j}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{t})(B_{i}B_{j}\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{q})(B_{i}\mathfrak{q}-B_{j}\mathfrak{t})(B_{i}\mathfrak{t}-B_{j}\mathfrak{q})}{(B_{i}B_{j}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t})^{2}}.

(251)

Like in the
D_{4}
-type LST case, to obtain the coefficients of
Q_{\tau}^{1}
order, the expansion of
Q_{5},Q_{6}
has to be taken up to very high order which is difficult, so we will only compute the
Q_{\tau}^{0}
order for
Z^{D_{5}}
.

Final result of
Z^{D_{5}}
.

Combining
Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}^{\bf 0}Q_{6}^{\bf 0}}
with
Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}Q_{6}}
, we express
Z^{D_{5}}
as PE form with the expansion parameters being
A_{1},A_{2},u,Q_{\tau}
,

\displaystyle Z^{D_{5}}=Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}^{\bf 0}Q_{6}^{\bf 0}}\cdot Z^{D_{5}}_{Q_{5}Q_{6}}=\text{PE}\bigg[\sum_{\ell,m,n,r}b_{\ell,m,n,r}A_{1}^{\ell}A_{2}^{m}u^{n}Q_{\tau}^{r}\bigg]\ ,

(252)

and list a few
b_{\ell,m,n,r}
that we could determine with the expansion of
Q_{5},Q_{6}
up to
Q_{5}^{3}Q_{6}^{2}
order,

\displaystyle b_{0,0,0,0}=\frac{1}{2}(3+\chi_{\bf 2})\left[0,\frac{1}{2}\right],

\displaystyle b_{1,-1,0,0}=2\chi_{\bf 1}[0,0],

\displaystyle b_{1,0,0,0}=({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\chi_{\bf s}^{2}+\chi_{\bf c}^{2}})[0,0]+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}2\chi_{\bf 1}}\bigg[0,\frac{1}{2}\bigg],

\displaystyle b_{0,1,0,0}=(1+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\chi_{\bf 1}^{2}+2\chi_{\bf 2}})[0,0]+[0,1]+\bigg[\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\bigg],

\displaystyle b_{0,0,1,0}=(1+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\chi_{\bf 2}})\bigg[0,\frac{1}{2}\bigg]+2\bigg[\frac{1}{2},0\bigg],

\displaystyle b_{1,-1,1,0}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}2\chi_{\bf 1}}[0,0]\ ,

\displaystyle b_{-1,1,1,0}=2\chi_{\bf 1}[0,0]\ ,

(253)

where

\chi_{\bf 1},\chi_{\bf 2},\chi_{\bf 3},\chi_{\bf s}
and
\chi_{\bf c}
are characters of fundamental weights of
D_{5}
whose expressions are in (
D
) when transformed to orthonormal basis by (
234
). For
b_{1,0,0,0},b_{0,1,0,0},b_{0,0,1,0}
, and
b_{1,-1,1,0}
, we denote the characters in red which means that these characters are obtained by Weyl group symmetry but not yet confirmed by direct computation, because we only compute the expansion of
Q_{5},Q_{6}
up to
Q_{5}^{3}Q_{6}^{2}
order which is just enough to determine the
Q_{5}^{0}Q_{6}^{0}
order of these characters. As the highest order among these characters is
Q_{5}^{3}Q_{6}^{2}
, by (
5.3
) one needs to compute the
Q_{5},Q_{6}
expansion up to
Q_{5}^{6}Q_{6}^{4}
to confirm these characters in red, which is computationally demanding.

The partition function of rank-1
D_{5}
-type LST computed in
Kim:2017xan
is expanded in terms of
Q_{1},

Q_{2},

Q_{3},

Q_{4},
Q_{5},
Q_{6},
w_{6},
Q_{\tau}
up to
Q_{1}^{1}Q_{2}^{1}Q_{3}^{1}Q_{4}^{1}Q_{5}^{2}Q_{6}^{2}w_{6}^{2}Q_{\tau}^{1}
order. Analogous to the
D_{4}
-case, we also re-expanded
Z^{D_{5}}
and our result is consistent with the result of
Kim:2017xan
up to the order that they computed.

5.4
Expansion of
E_{6}
-type LST

Figure 27:
(a) Trivalent gluing brane web of
E_{6}
-type LST. (b) Affine Dynkin diagram of affine
E_{6}
.

For
E_{6}
-type LST, it is also easy to show that the swapping of external NS-charged branes of each gauge node of
Q_{i}
in the trivalent gluing brane web in figure
27
(a) corresponds to the affine Weyl reflection of each affine simple root
\alpha_{i}
in the affine
E_{6}
Dynkin diagram in figure
27
(b). So by similar argument like in the
D_{4}
-type LST case, we conclude that
the partition function of
E_{6}
-type LST has affine
E_{6}
symmetry
.

The affine
E_{6}
Weyl reflections from the brane web in figure
27
(a) are as following forms:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{1}^{-1},\ Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}Q_{1};\\
Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}^{-1},\ Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{1}Q_{2},\ Q_{7}\rightarrow Q_{7}Q_{2};\\
Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{3}^{-1},\ Q_{4}\rightarrow Q_{4}Q_{3};\\
Q_{4}\rightarrow Q_{4}^{-1},\ Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{3}Q_{4},\ Q_{7}\rightarrow Q_{7}Q_{4};\\
Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}^{-1},\ Q_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}Q_{5};\\
Q_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}^{-1},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{6},\ Q_{7}\rightarrow Q_{7}Q_{6};\\
Q_{7}\rightarrow Q_{7}^{-1},\ Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}Q_{7},\ Q_{4}\rightarrow Q_{4}Q_{7},\ Q_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}Q_{7}.\end{array}\right.

(254)

Again like in the
D_{4},D_{5}
-type LST cases, seeing the affine
E_{6}
characters in the expansion of partition function is difficult, so we try to reduce them to the
E_{6}
characters by using only six of the seven affine Weyl reflections. We pick the affine Weyl reflections of
Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4},Q_{5},Q_{6},Q_{7}
, and then
Q_{1}
will be replaced by the horizontal period
u
in (
60
)
as Weyl reflection invariant parameter. The six chosen affine
E_{6}
Weyl reflections reduce to the following forms:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}^{-1},\ Q_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}Q_{5};\\
Q_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}^{-1},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{6},\ Q_{7}\rightarrow Q_{7}Q_{6};\\
Q_{7}\rightarrow Q_{7}^{-1},\ Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}Q_{7},\ Q_{4}\rightarrow Q_{4}Q_{7},\ Q_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}Q_{7};\\
Q_{4}\rightarrow Q_{4}^{-1},\ Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{3}Q_{4},\ Q_{7}\rightarrow Q_{7}Q_{4};\\
Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{3}^{-1},\ Q_{4}\rightarrow Q_{4}Q_{3};\\
Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}^{-1},\ Q_{7}\rightarrow Q_{7}Q_{2}.\end{array}\right.

(255)

They are the
E_{6}
Weyl reflections in the basis
\{\alpha_{5},\alpha_{6},\alpha_{7},\alpha_{4},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{2}\}
where the
\alpha
’s as well as the corresponding
Q
’s are ordered in a conventional way for
E_{6}
simple roots.

Figure 28:

Transformation of node 6. The nodes 2 and 4 also transform similarly.

Figure 29:
Transformation of node 7.

For
E_{6}
-type LST, we have five Coulomb branch parameters
Q_{7,1},Q_{7,2},Q_{2,1},Q_{4,1}
and
Q_{6,1}
as shown in figure
17
, and these Coulomb branch parameters also change under the
E_{6}
Weyl reflections as shown in figure
28
and figure
29
. For convenience in the later computation, we transform them into the following new Coulomb branch parameters
v_{1},v_{2},w_{2},w_{4},w_{6},

\displaystyle Q_{7,1}=v_{1}^{3},\quad Q_{7,2}=v_{2}^{3},\quad Q_{2,1}=w_{2}^{2},\quad Q_{4,1}=w_{4}^{2},\quad Q_{6,1}=w_{6}^{2}.

(256)

The
E_{6}
Weyl reflections including the Coulomb branch parameters now take the following forms:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}^{-1},\ Q_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}Q_{5};\\
Q_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}^{-1},\ Q_{5}\rightarrow Q_{5}Q_{6},\ Q_{7}\rightarrow Q_{7}Q_{6},\ w_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}w_{6};\\
Q_{7}\rightarrow Q_{7}^{-1},\ Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}Q_{7},\ Q_{4}\rightarrow Q_{4}Q_{7},\ Q_{6}\rightarrow Q_{6}Q_{7},\ v_{1}\rightarrow Q_{7}^{\frac{1}{3}}v_{1},\ v_{2}\rightarrow Q_{7}^{\frac{1}{3}}v_{2};\\
Q_{4}\rightarrow Q_{4}^{-1},\ Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{3}Q_{4},\ Q_{7}\rightarrow Q_{7}Q_{4},\ w_{4}\rightarrow Q_{4}w_{4};\\
Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{3}^{-1},\ Q_{4}\rightarrow Q_{4}Q_{3};\\
Q_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}^{-1},\ Q_{7}\rightarrow Q_{7}Q_{2},\ w_{2}\rightarrow Q_{2}w_{2}.\end{array}\right.

(257)

The invariant Coulomb branch parameters should take the general form of

\displaystyle Q_{2}^{a}Q_{3}^{b}Q_{4}^{c}Q_{5}^{d}Q_{6}^{e}Q_{7}^{f}v_{1}^{g}v_{2}^{h}w_{2}^{i}w_{4}^{j}w_{6}^{k}\ .

(258)

By requiring this general form to be invariant under all the Weyl reflections in (
257
), we obtain six linear equations about
a,b,c,d,e,f,g,h,i,j,k
. There are five independent invariant Coulomb branch parameters which are proportional to
v_{1}
(i.e.
g=1,h=i=j=k=0
),
v_{2}
(i.e.
h=1,g=i=j=k=0
),
w_{2}
(i.e.
i=1,g=h=j=k=0
),
w_{4}
(i.e.
j=1,g=h=i=k=0
) and
w_{6}
(i.e.
k=1,g=h=i=j=0
) respectively.
For each case, we can solve the six linear equations and obtain one solution.
Totally, we get the following five invariant Coulomb branch parameters,

\displaystyle A_{1}=Q_{2}Q_{3}^{\frac{2}{3}}Q_{4}^{\frac{4}{3}}Q_{5}^{\frac{2}{3}}Q_{6}^{\frac{4}{3}}Q_{7}^{2}v_{1},\ A_{2}=Q_{2}Q_{3}^{\frac{2}{3}}Q_{4}^{\frac{4}{3}}Q_{5}^{\frac{2}{3}}Q_{6}^{\frac{4}{3}}Q_{7}^{2}v_{2},\ A_{3}=Q_{2}^{2}Q_{3}Q_{4}^{2}Q_{5}Q_{6}^{2}Q_{7}^{3}w_{2},

\displaystyle A_{4}=Q_{2}^{2}Q_{3}^{\frac{5}{3}}Q_{4}^{\frac{10}{3}}Q_{5}^{\frac{4}{3}}Q_{6}^{\frac{8}{3}}Q_{7}^{4}w_{4},\ A_{5}=Q_{2}^{2}Q_{3}^{\frac{4}{3}}Q_{4}^{\frac{8}{3}}Q_{5}^{\frac{5}{3}}Q_{6}^{\frac{10}{3}}Q_{7}^{4}w_{6}\ .

(259)

If we switch from
E_{6}

\alpha
-basis fugacities
Q
’s to the parameters
B
’s which corresponds to the embedding
E_{6}\supset\text{ SO}(10)\times\text{U}(1)

\displaystyle Q_{5}=\frac{B_{1}}{B_{2}},~Q_{6}=\frac{B_{2}}{B_{3}},~Q_{7}=\frac{B_{3}}{B_{4}},~Q_{4}=B_{4}B_{5},~Q_{3}=\frac{\sqrt{B_{6}}}{\sqrt{B_{1}B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}B_{5}}},~Q_{2}=\frac{B_{4}}{B_{5}},

(260)

the
E_{6}
Weyl reflections in (
255
) become as follows:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}B_{1}\rightarrow B_{2},\ B_{2}\rightarrow B_{1};\\
B_{2}\rightarrow B_{3},\ B_{3}\rightarrow B_{2};\\
B_{3}\rightarrow B_{4},\ B_{4}\rightarrow B_{3};\\
B_{4}\rightarrow B_{5}^{-1},\ B_{5}\rightarrow B_{4}^{-1};\\
B_{i}\rightarrow\frac{B_{i}B_{6}^{\frac{1}{4}}}{(B_{1}B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}B_{5})^{\frac{1}{4}}}\ \text{for}\ i=1,2,3,4,5,\ B_{6}\rightarrow(B_{1}B_{2}B_{3}B_{4}B_{5})^{\frac{3}{4}}B_{6}^{\frac{1}{4}};\\
B_{4}\rightarrow B_{5},\ B_{5}\rightarrow B_{4}.\end{array}\right.

(261)

Like the previous subsections, it is most convenient to keep using the old parameters
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4},Q_{5},Q_{6},Q_{7},v_{1},v_{2},w_{2},w_{4},w_{6},Q_{\tau}
when computing the partition function of
E_{6}
-type LST in the intermediate process and in particular pick
Q_{7}
as a main expansion parameter, and switch to the new set of parameters
Q_{2},Q_{3},Q_{4},Q_{5},Q_{6},

Q_{7},A_{1},A_{2},A_{3},A_{4},A_{5},u,Q_{\tau}
in the final step in order to see the
E_{6}
characters manifestly.

We separate the full partition function
Z^{E_{6}}
into
Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}^{\bf 0}}
part which is the
Q_{7}^{0}
order term in the full partition function and
Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}}
part which is the remaining part that depend on
Q_{7}
after extracting out
Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}^{\bf 0}}
,

\displaystyle Z^{E_{6}}=Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}^{\bf 0}}\cdot Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}},

(262)

where by (
69
) and (
3.3.1
)

\displaystyle Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}^{\bf 0}}=Z^{E_{6}}_{\text{pert}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu_{1,1}}\bar{Z}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{1,1}}(w_{2},Q_{1})\sum_{\mu_{2,1},\mu_{2,2}}\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)}}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,2}}(v_{1},v_{2},w_{2},Q_{2})\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,2}}(w_{2},Q_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu_{3,1}}\bar{Z}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{3,1}}(w_{4},Q_{3})\sum_{\mu_{4,1},\mu_{4,2}}\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)}}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{4,1}\mu_{4,2}}(v_{1},v_{2},w_{4},Q_{4})\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{4,1}\mu_{4,2}}(w_{4},Q_{3})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu_{5,1}}\bar{Z}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{5,1}}(w_{6},Q_{5})\sum_{\mu_{6,1},\mu_{6,2}}\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)}}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}(v_{1},v_{2},w_{6},Q_{6})\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}(w_{6},Q_{5}),

(263)

\displaystyle Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu_{7,1},\mu_{7,2},\mu_{7,3}}\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(3)}}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}(v_{1},v_{2},Q_{7})

\displaystyle\times\frac{\sum_{\mu_{2,1},\mu_{2,2}}\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)}}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,2}}(v_{1},v_{2},w_{2},Q_{2})\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,2}}(w_{2},Q_{1})}{\sum_{\mu_{2,1},\mu_{2,2}}\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)}}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,2}}(v_{1},v_{2},w_{2},Q_{2})\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,2}}(w_{2},Q_{1})}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\sum_{\mu_{4,1},\mu_{4,2}}\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)}}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}\mu_{4,1}\mu_{4,2}}(v_{1},v_{2},w_{4},Q_{4})\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{4,1}\mu_{4,2}}(w_{4},Q_{3})}{\sum_{\mu_{4,1},\mu_{4,2}}\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)}}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{4,1}\mu_{4,2}}(v_{1},v_{2},w_{4},Q_{4})\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{4,1}\mu_{4,2}}(w_{4},Q_{3})}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\sum_{\mu_{6,1},\mu_{6,2}}\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)}}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}(v_{1},v_{2},w_{6},Q_{6})\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}(w_{6},Q_{5})}{\sum_{\mu_{6,1},\mu_{6,2}}\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)}}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}(v_{1},v_{2},w_{6},Q_{6})\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}(w_{6},Q_{5})}.

(264)

with

\displaystyle\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(3)}}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}(v_{1},v_{2},Q_{7})\equiv\frac{Q_{7}^{|\mu_{7,1}|+|\mu_{7,2}|+|\mu_{7,3}|}}{\prod_{c=1}^{3}\prod_{e=1}^{3}\varTheta_{\mu_{7,c}\mu_{7,e}}(\frac{y_{7,c}}{y_{7,e}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\mu_{7,c}\mu_{7,e}}(\frac{y_{7,c}}{y_{7,e}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})},

(265)

\displaystyle\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)}}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,2}}(v_{1},v_{2},w_{2},Q_{2})

\displaystyle~~\equiv Q_{2}^{|\mu_{2,1}|+|\mu_{2,2}|}\prod_{p=1}^{2}\bigg(\frac{\prod_{r=1}^{3}\varTheta_{\mu_{2,p}\mu_{7,r}}(\frac{y_{2,p}}{y_{7,r}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\mu_{7,r}\mu_{2,p}}(\frac{y_{7,r}}{y_{2,p}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}|Q_{\tau})}{\prod_{s=1}^{2}\varTheta_{\mu_{2,p}\mu_{2,s}}(\frac{y_{2,p}}{y_{2,s}}|Q_{\tau})\varTheta_{\mu_{2,p}\mu_{2,s}}(\frac{y_{2,p}}{y_{2,s}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}|Q_{\tau})}\bigg).

(266)

By defining

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}(v_{1},v_{2},w_{2},Q_{2},Q_{1})

\displaystyle\,\equiv\frac{\sum_{\mu_{2,1},\mu_{2,2}}\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)}}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,2}}(v_{1},v_{2},w_{2},Q_{2})\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,2}}(w_{2},Q_{1})}{\sum_{\mu_{2,1},\mu_{2,2}}\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)}}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,2}}(v_{1},v_{2},w_{2},Q_{2})\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,2}}(w_{2},Q_{1})},

(267)

we can rewrite (
5.4
) as

\displaystyle Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu_{7,1},\mu_{7,2},\mu_{7,3}}\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(3)}}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}(v_{1},v_{2},Q_{7})\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}(v_{1},v_{2},w_{2},Q_{2},Q_{1})

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}(v_{1},v_{2},w_{4},Q_{4},Q_{3})\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}(v_{1},v_{2},w_{6},Q_{6},Q_{5}).

(268)

Like the
\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{5,1}\mu_{5,2}}
defined in (
185
), we can also compute
\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}
by expansion with respect to
Q_{2}
. Then we can substitute the expansions of
\mathcal{Z}
’s into (
5.4
) to obtain
Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}}
.

Evaluation of the partition function by Weyl group symmetry.

Among all the terms of a character of
E_{6}
in an arbitrary representation in the
\alpha
-basis, the terms
Q_{7}^{0}Q_{2}^{0}Q_{3}^{i}Q_{4}^{j}Q_{5}^{k}Q_{6}^{\ell}
with
i,j,k,\ell\leq 0
include at least one element of each Weyl orbit in the character, so these terms are enough to determine all the terms of the character by Weyl group symmetry. We can utilize this to determine the coefficients of the expansion of the partition function.
In the computation of
Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}^{\bf 0}}
and
Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}}
, we need to sum over enough Young diagrams in order to go up to higher orders of
Q_{2},Q_{4},Q_{6},Q_{7}
. As we go up to higher orders, it becomes more time consuming. By combining a lower order computation with the Weyl group symmetry analysis, we find that up to
Q_{2}^{2}Q_{4}^{2}Q_{6}^{2}Q_{7}^{3}
order, the coefficients of the following terms inside PE of
Z^{E_{6}}
can be determined by Weyl group symmetry

\displaystyle 1,\ \frac{A_{3}^{2}}{A_{1}A_{2}^{2}},\ \frac{A_{3}^{2}}{A_{1}^{2}A_{2}},\ \frac{A_{4}^{3}}{A_{1}^{3}A_{2}^{3}},\ \frac{A_{5}^{3}}{A_{1}^{3}A_{2}^{3}},\ A_{3},\ \frac{A_{1}A_{3}}{A_{2}},\ \frac{A_{2}A_{3}}{A_{1}},\ u,

(269)

where
1
means the zeroth order of all the expansion parameters. Note that there might be more terms whose coefficients can also be determined by Weyl group symmetry when the expansion is taken up to
Q_{2}^{2}Q_{4}^{2}Q_{6}^{2}Q_{7}^{3}
order, we did not try to exhaust all such possible terms. In the following computation of
Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}^{\bf 0}}
and
Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}}
, we will only consider these terms in (
269
) which are all at
Q_{\tau}^{0}
order, and all the other terms in the PE will be ignored and just put into “
\cdots
”. Switching to
v_{1},v_{2},w_{2},w_{4},w_{6},Q_{1}
, these terms are

\displaystyle 1,\ \frac{w_{2}^{2}}{v_{1}v_{2}^{2}},\ \frac{w_{2}^{2}}{v_{1}^{2}v_{2}},\ \frac{w_{4}^{3}}{v_{1}^{3}v_{2}^{3}},\ \frac{w_{6}^{3}}{v_{1}^{3}v_{2}^{3}},\ w_{2},\ \frac{v_{1}w_{2}}{v_{2}},\ \frac{v_{2}w_{2}}{v_{1}},\ Q_{1}.

(270)

Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}^{\bf 0}}
part.

We first compute the summation over
\mu_{2,1},\mu_{2,2}
in (
5.4
) by the definition in (
266
) and (
185
). In order to obtain the exact form, we have to sum over
\mu_{2,1},\mu_{2,2}
up to infinity, but if we further expand with respect to
v_{1},v_{2},w_{2}
up to low orders, its expansion coefficients turn out to be finite polynomials of
Q_{2}
which only depend on a few Young diagrams, and the result is given as follows:

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu_{2,1},\mu_{2,2}}\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)}}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,2}}(v_{1},v_{2},w_{2},Q_{2})\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{2,1}\mu_{2,2}}(w_{2},Q_{1})

\displaystyle=~\text{PE}\bigg[\frac{(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})Q_{2}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}+\frac{(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})Q_{2}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}\frac{w_{2}^{2}}{v_{1}v_{2}^{2}}+\frac{(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})Q_{2}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}\frac{w_{2}^{2}}{v_{1}^{2}v_{2}}

\displaystyle~~-\frac{2\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}Q_{2}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}w_{2}-\frac{\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}Q_{2}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}\frac{v_{1}w_{2}}{v_{2}}-\frac{\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}Q_{2}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}\frac{v_{2}w_{2}}{v_{1}}

\displaystyle~~+\frac{(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})Q_{2}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}Q_{1}+\cdots\bigg].

(271)

In this way, we also compute the summation over
\mu_{4,1},\mu_{4,2}
and
\mu_{6,1},\mu_{6,2}
in (
5.4
) and obtain,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu_{4,1},\mu_{4,2}}\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)}}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{4,1}\mu_{4,2}}(v_{1},v_{2},w_{4},Q_{4})\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{4,1}\mu_{4,2}}(w_{4},Q_{3})

\displaystyle=~\text{PE}\bigg[\frac{(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})(1+Q_{3})Q_{4}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}-\frac{(\mathfrak{q}^{2}+\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{t}^{2})Q_{3}Q_{4}^{2}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}}\frac{w_{4}^{3}}{v_{1}^{3}v_{2}^{3}}+\cdots\bigg],

(272)

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu_{6,1},\mu_{6,2}}\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(2)}}_{\text{\o}\text{\o}\text{\o}\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}(v_{1},v_{2},w_{6},Q_{6})\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{6,1}\mu_{6,2}}(w_{6},Q_{5})

\displaystyle=~\text{PE}\bigg[\frac{(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})(1+Q_{5})Q_{6}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}-\frac{(\mathfrak{q}^{2}+\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{t}^{2})Q_{5}Q_{6}^{2}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}}\frac{w_{6}^{3}}{v_{1}^{3}v_{2}^{3}}+\cdots\bigg].

(273)

Substituting (
5.4
), (
5.4
), (
5.4
), (
5.2.2
), and (
121
) into (
5.4
), we get

\displaystyle Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}^{\bf 0}}=

\displaystyle~\text{PE}\bigg[\frac{\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}\Big(6+Q_{2}+Q_{3}+Q_{4}+Q_{5}+Q_{6}+Q_{3}Q_{4}+Q_{5}Q_{6}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}Q_{2}\frac{w_{2}^{2}}{v_{1}v_{2}^{2}}+\frac{\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}Q_{2}\frac{w_{2}^{2}}{v_{1}^{2}v_{2}}-\frac{(\mathfrak{q}^{2}+\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{t}^{2})Q_{3}Q_{4}^{2}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}}\frac{w_{4}^{3}}{v_{1}^{3}v_{2}^{3}}

\displaystyle-\frac{(\mathfrak{q}^{2}+\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}+\mathfrak{t}^{2})Q_{5}Q_{6}^{2}}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}}\frac{w_{6}^{3}}{v_{1}^{3}v_{2}^{3}}-\frac{2\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}(1+Q_{2})}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}w_{2}-\frac{\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}(1+Q_{2})}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}\frac{v_{1}w_{2}}{v_{2}}

\displaystyle-\frac{\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{t}}(1+Q_{2})}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}\frac{v_{2}w_{2}}{v_{1}}+\frac{(\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{t})(1+Q_{2})}{(1-\mathfrak{q})(1-\mathfrak{t})}Q_{1}+\cdots\bigg],

(274)

where terms containing
w_{2}^{-1}
are flopped.

Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}}
part and rescaling.

To compute
Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}}
in (
5.4
), we expand with respect to
Q_{7},Q_{2},Q_{4},Q_{6}
whose orders are restricted by Young diagram box numbers and with respect to
Q_{1}
which is proportional to
u
.
Like in the previous cases, in order to reduce the heavy computation we need to further expand
Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}}
with respect to

v_{1},v_{2},w_{2},w_{4},w_{6}
up to finite order. And we need to rescale
\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(3)}}
,
\mathcal{Z}
functions and parameters in (
5.4
) to make the expansions of
\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(3)}}
,
\mathcal{Z}
with respect to
v_{1},v_{2},w_{2},w_{4},w_{6}
have all non-negative orders which can be fulfilled by the following rescalings up to
Q_{\tau}^{0}
order:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}(w_{2})\rightarrow(v_{1}^{2}v_{2}^{2}w_{2})^{|\mu_{7,1}|+|\mu_{7,2}|+|\mu_{7,3}|}\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}(w_{2}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}(w_{4})\rightarrow(v_{1}^{2}v_{2}^{2}w_{4})^{|\mu_{7,1}|+|\mu_{7,2}|+|\mu_{7,3}|}\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}(w_{4}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}(w_{6})\rightarrow(v_{1}^{2}v_{2}^{2}w_{6})^{|\mu_{7,1}|+|\mu_{7,2}|+|\mu_{7,3}|}\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}(w_{6}),

\displaystyle\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(3)}}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}}\rightarrow(v_{1}^{6}v_{2}^{6}w_{2}w_{4}w_{6})^{-(|\mu_{7,1}|+|\mu_{7,2}|+|\mu_{7,3}|)}\bar{Z}^{\text{SU(3)}}_{\mu_{7,1}\mu_{7,2}\mu_{7,3}},

\displaystyle Q_{2}\rightarrow v_{1}v_{2}Q_{2},\quad Q_{4}\rightarrow v_{1}v_{2}Q_{4},\quad Q_{6}\rightarrow v_{1}v_{2}Q_{6},\quad Q_{7}\rightarrow v_{1}^{6}v_{2}^{6}w_{2}w_{4}w_{6}Q_{7}\ .

(275)

After obtaining the PE form of
Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}}
expanded with
v_{1},v_{2},w_{2},w_{4},w_{6}
under the rescalings, we switch back to the original parameters in the PE form.

Final result of
Z^{E_{6}}
.

We compute the terms (
270
) in the PE of
Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}}
and then combine it with (
5.4
) to obtain the full partition function
Z^{E_{6}}
.
We express
Z^{E_{6}}
as PE form with the expansion parameters being
A_{1},A_{2},A_{3},A_{4},A_{5},u,Q_{\tau},

\displaystyle Z^{E_{6}}=Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}^{\bf 0}}\cdot Z^{E_{6}}_{Q_{7}}=\text{PE}\left[\sum_{i,j,k,\ell,m,n,r}b_{i,j,k,\ell,m,n,r}A_{1}^{i}A_{2}^{j}A_{3}^{k}A_{4}^{\ell}A_{5}^{m}u^{n}Q_{\tau}^{r}\right],

(276)

and list the coefficients of the terms (
269
) which we could determine from the expansion of
Q_{2},Q_{4},Q_{6},Q_{7}
up to
Q_{2}^{2}Q_{4}^{2}Q_{6}^{2}Q_{7}^{3}
order,

\displaystyle b_{0,0,0,0,0,0,0}=\frac{1}{2}(6+\chi_{\bf 6})\bigg[0,\frac{1}{2}\bigg],

\displaystyle b_{-1,-2,2,0,0,0,0}=b_{-2,-1,2,0,0,0,0}=\bigg[0,\frac{1}{2}\bigg],

\displaystyle b_{-3,-3,0,3,0,0,0}=b_{-3,-3,0,0,3,0,0}=[0,1],

\displaystyle b_{0,0,1,0,0,0,0}=(6+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}2\chi_{\bf 6}})[0,0]+2[0,1]+3\bigg[\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\bigg],

\displaystyle b_{1,-1,1,0,0,0,0}=b_{-1,1,1,0,0,0,0}=(2+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\chi_{\bf 6}})[0,0]+\bigg[\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\bigg],

\displaystyle b_{0,0,0,0,0,1,0}=(2+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\chi_{\bf 6}})\bigg[0,\frac{1}{2}\bigg]+4\bigg[\frac{1}{2},0\bigg],

(277)

where
\chi_{\bf 6}
is for short the character
\chi_{\bf 6}^{E_{6}}
of the adjoint representation of
E_{6}
in (
D
). Note that for
b_{0,0,1,0,0,0,0}
,
b_{1,-1,1,0,0,0,0}
,
b_{-1,1,1,0,0,0,0}
and
b_{0,0,0,0,0,1,0}
we denote the characters in red which means the characters are obtained by Weyl group symmetry but not yet confirmed by direct computation of higher orders.

5.5
E_{7}
and
E_{8}
-type LSTs

Due to the quiver structures of the brane webs of
E_{7}
and
E_{8}
LSTs, it is also easy to figure out the affine Weyl group symmetry in the brane webs which can confirm that the partition functions of these theories also have affine
E_{7}
and affine
E_{8}
symmetries.
We can also follow the similar procedure of the
E_{6}
-type LST to reduce affine
E_{7}
and affine
E_{8}
symmetries to
E_{7}
and
E_{8}
symmetries, and determine the corresponding invariant Coulomb branch parameters,
then use the general formulas (
121
) and (
122
) to compute the partition functions and do expansions to see the
E_{7}
and
E_{8}
characters.
But as we have experienced time-consuming in the computation of
E_{6}
-type LST, we expect that it will be more time-consuming in the computation of
E_{7}
and
E_{8}
LSTs, so we do not compute
E_{7}
and
E_{8}
cases, but the computation is essentially straightforward.

6
Conclusion

In this paper, we proposed a novel way of computing the supersymmetric partition functions for 6d
\mathcal{N}=(1,0)
little string theories (LSTs) engineered from type IIB NS5-branes probing
D_{N}
or
E_{N}
singularity. The effective gauge theory description of these LSTs on tensor branch is affine
D_{N}
or
E_{N}
quiver gauge theories and we propose 5-brane configurations realizing these quiver gauge theories based on tri-/quadri-valent gluings of 5-brane webs
Hayashi:2017jze
;
Hayashi:2021pcj
, by identifying external 5-branes which gives rise to a compact direction. This can be summarized as follows. We first utilize 5-brane configurations of the tri-/quadri-valent gluings so that the resulting configuration describes 6d theories on a circle, and then we further introduce the bivalent self-gluing of the external 5-branes in such a way that all external 5-branes are glued which makes another compact direction.

5-brane configurations are known for little string theories of
A
-type or those engineered from type IIA NS5-brane probing
D
-type singularities. These 5-brane webs consist of a circular quiver. Our brane construction is, however, different from such conventional configurations, as we enclose the external 5-branes of Type IIB configuration that arise from trivalent (or quadrivalent) gluings for 6d
(D_{N},D_{N})
and
(E_{N},E_{N})
conformal matters. Our construction is therefore a natural generalization making those little string theories whose conformal theory limit gives rise to a
D
- and
E
-type conformal matter. With these glued 5-brane webs, we computed the supersymmetric partition functions of these LSTs, using topological vertex, and we obtained compact formulas for the partition function of a general
D
- or
E
-type LST with arbitrary rank. And the general formulas also work for
A
-type LST upon slight modifications.

For
D
-type LSTs, it is well-known that one can realize the LSTs using type IIA brane configurations with two ON
0
-planes as they give rise to affine
D_{N}
-type quiver theories
Hanany:1999sj
. Upon T-duality of this IIA configuration, one can find a type IIB description for this
D_{N}
-type LSTs with 5-brane webs with two ON-planes where NS5-branes are compactified along the T-dual circle. We showed that such 5-brane description with two ON-planes can be consistently mapped to a trivalent or quadrivalent gluing of 5-brane webs and hence they are equivalent. Unlike
D
-type LSTs, there are no known brane descriptions for
E
-type LSTs. Our proposal gives 5-brane configurations that can describe
E
-type LSTs.

We then tried to expand the partition functions of the LSTs to see their symmetries. We discovered that the symmetries of the affine quiver structures of these LSTs are hidden in various flops of the brane webs. These flops are just exchanges of external NS-charged branes which keep the brane configurations invariant, and each such flop correspond to a Weyl reflection of the symmetry. Thus we conclude that the
D
- and
E
-type LSTs have corresponding affine
D
and
E
symmetries. These affine symmetries are demanding to see in the expansion because their characters are infinite polynomials that contain infinitely high orders which means the Young diagram sums need to be taken up to infinity, so we reduce the affine
D
and
E
symmetries to
D
and
E
symmetries and choose appropriate parameters under these reduced symmetries. We successfully obtained the expansions of the partition functions of
D_{4},D_{5},E_{6}
-type LSTs up to low orders and saw the
D_{4},D_{5},E_{6}
characters in the coefficients.

Our computation for the expansions of partition functions is for rank-1 LSTs engineered from one NS5-brane probing
D,E
-type singularities. The partition functions for rank-
k
LSTs engineered from
k
NS5-branes probing
D,E
-type singularities can be generalized in a straightforward manner in our setup, though the computation itself would be demanding.

There are various directions to pursue. One of them would be the T-duality of
E
-type LSTs. The T-duality of LSTs can be realized as an S-duality in the corresponding type IIB 5-brane configurations. As discussed,
D
-type LSTs, engineered from IIB NS5-brane probing
D
-type singularity, have a 5-brane description with two ON-planes and then taking the S-duality leads another 5-brane configuration with two O5-planes which give rise to a circular quiver. This corresponds to IIA NS5-brane probing the same
D
-type singularity. For
E
-type LSTs, in particular, no 5-brane webs are known. Checking T-duality for
E
-type LSTs would be very challenging. As we proposed a new 5-brane configuration for
D,E
-type LSTs as a tri-/quadri-valent gluing, it would be interesting to study the T-duality within the framework of glued branes. For instance, the corresponding brane configurations for this S-duality would be interesting. Also, once we have them, we can take the decompactification limit which yields a 6d SCFT, which would give a new 5-brane realization of the corresponding SCFT.

Appendix A
Gluings over nonparallel external 5-branes

In this appendix, we discuss the validity of non-parallel identification of 5-branes which appeared in the main text. Type IIB description of 6d SU(1) gauge theory with 2 fundamental hypermultiplets and with a tensor multiplet is depicted in figure
30
. In the first figure (a), one hyper is located on the left of the first NS5-brane and the other hyper is located on the right of the second NS5-brane, which we denote by (
[1]-SU(1)-[1]
). These two NS5 branes are identified (or glued) to yield a 5-brane configuration for a 6d theory on a circle, which is, in fact, a well-known (S-dual configuration of) 5-brane setup for rank-1 M-string theory on a circle
Haghighat:2013gba
.

In figure
30
(a), the 5-brane configuration is equivalent to a 5-brane where two external 5-branes are attached to two different D7-branes whose 7-brane monodromy cuts are pointing the opposite direction such that the 7-brane on the left points
(-1,0)
-direction (to the left) where the 7-brane on the right points
(1,0)
-direction (to the right). One then can take a Hanany-Witten move with one of these D7-branes, say we take the 7-brane on the right to the left which gives rise to the 5-brane web given in figure
30
(b), where the monodromy cuts for both D7-branes are pointing the
(-1,0)
-direction. As a result, the vertically identified NS5-branes in figure
30
(a) are now identified in a non-parallel way. This resultant configuration represents 6d
[2]-SU(1)
on a circle. One can think of this 5-brane configuration as a 5-brane web for 5d KK theory where non-parallel 5-branes of the same NS5-brane charges are identified. In other words, one can start with a 5-brane web for
[2]-SU(1)
where two 5-branes of non-zero NS5-brane charges are bivalently self-glued. As the Hanany-Witten moves do not affect gauge theories, we claim that the partition function based on the 5-brane web in figure
30
(a) is equivalent to that based on figure
30
(b).

(a)
Q_{f}
Q
Q_{b}
(b)
Q_{f}Q^{-1}
Q
Q_{b}
Q
Q_{f}Q

Figure 30:
(a) A 5-brane web for 6d
[1]-SU(1)-[1]
. (b) A 5-brane web for 6d
[2]-SU(1)
.

To check our claim, we compute the partition function for each web diagram and compare two partition functions. The partition function for the left diagram, call it
Z^{(a)}
, is already derived in
Haghighat:2013gba
and given by

\displaystyle Z^{(a)}=

\displaystyle\prod_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1-Q_{\tau}^{k})^{2}}\prod_{k=1}^{\infty}\prod_{i,j=1}^{\infty}\frac{(1-QQ_{\tau}^{k-1}\mathfrak{t}^{i-\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}^{j-\frac{1}{2}})^{2}(1-Q^{-1}Q_{\tau}^{k}\mathfrak{t}^{i-\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}^{j-\frac{1}{2}})^{2}}{(1-Q_{\tau}^{k}\mathfrak{t}^{i-1}\mathfrak{q}^{j})^{2}(1-Q_{\tau}^{k}\mathfrak{t}^{i}\mathfrak{q}^{j-1})^{2}}

(278)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\mu_{b}}(-QQ_{b})^{|\mu_{b}|}\prod_{(i,j)\in\mu_{b}}\frac{\theta_{1}(Q^{-1}\mathfrak{t}^{-\mu_{b,i}+j-\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}^{i-\frac{1}{2}})\theta_{1}(Q^{-1}\mathfrak{t}^{\mu_{b,i}-j+\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}^{-i+\frac{1}{2}})}{\theta_{1}(\mathfrak{t}^{-\mu_{b,i}+j-1}\mathfrak{q}^{-\mu_{b,j}^{t}+i})\theta_{1}(\mathfrak{t}^{-\mu_{b,i}+j}\mathfrak{q}^{-\mu_{b,j}^{t}+i-1})},

where
Q_{\tau}=Q_{f}Q
.

.

We label the topological vertex computation of the left and the right building blocks respectively as

, and the partition function is given as a sum of this

\displaystyle Z^{(b)}

where each piece is given by

\displaystyle Z^{\rm L}(\mu_{b})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\nu_{1,2,3}}(-Q)^{|\nu_{1}|+|\nu_{2}|}(-Q_{f}Q^{-1})^{|\nu_{3}|}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.98857pt\times\tilde{f}^{-1}_{\nu_{3}}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{t})C_{\nu_{3}\nu_{1}^{t}\text{\o}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})C_{\nu_{2}^{t}\nu_{1}\mu_{b}^{t}}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{t})C_{\nu_{2}\nu_{3}^{t}\text{\o}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\ ,

(281a)

\displaystyle Z^{\rm R}(\mu_{b})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\nu}(-Q_{\tau})^{|\nu|}\tilde{f}_{\nu}^{-1}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{t})C_{\nu\nu^{t}\mu_{b}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\ .

(281b)

A little calculation
14
14

14

See Appendix
B
for how to compute the partition function of a vertical periodic strip.
leads that two partition functions are equivalent up to an (unphysical) extra factor which does not depend on the Coulomb branch parameters,

\displaystyle\frac{Z^{(b)}}{Z^{{\rm extra}}}=Z^{(a)}\ ,

(282)

where

\displaystyle Z^{{\rm extra}}=\prod_{k=1}^{\infty}\prod_{i,j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1-Q^{2}Q_{\tau}^{k-1}\mathfrak{t}^{i}\mathfrak{q}^{j-1})(1-Q^{-2}Q_{\tau}^{k}\mathfrak{t}^{i}\mathfrak{q}^{j-1})}\ .

(283)

The extra factor
Z^{{\rm extra}}
typically arises when there are external parallel 5-branes in a given 5-brane configuration. It follows from (
282
) that the physical part of two partition functions
Z^{(a)}
and
Z^{(b)}
are the same. Hence, non-parallel identification leads to a physically equivalent result compared with the partition function with parallel identification. We note that such non-parallel identification of external edges in 5-brane webs of two O5-planes are also discussed in
Kim:2021cua
where the partition functions for KK theories of 6d
SO(8)
and
SU(3)
theories on a circle with a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
outer automorphism twist were computed.

Appendix B
Sum over Young diagrams along a periodic strip

In computing the partition functions for LSTs, we need to sum over Young diagrams along periodic strips. This involves a successive use of the Cauchy identities (
10
)–(
13
) and leads to Jacobi theta functions, as discussed in
Haghighat:2013gba
;
Kim:2021cua
.

We encounter products of the Schur functions whose Young diagram indices form a closed loop. As an example, we consider the following which comes from (
21
),

\displaystyle\sum_{\boldsymbol{\nu,\eta}}

\displaystyle\left(Q_{\tau}Q_{f}^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}\right)^{|\nu_{1}|}\left(Q_{f}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}\right)^{|\nu_{2}|}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{t}}\right)^{\frac{|\eta_{1}|}{2}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{t}}\right)^{\frac{|\eta_{2}|}{2}}

\displaystyle\times s_{\nu_{1}^{t}/\eta_{1}}(\mathfrak{t}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{q}^{-\lambda_{1}})s_{\nu_{2}^{t}/\eta_{1}}(\mathfrak{q}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{t}^{-\lambda_{1}^{t}})s_{\nu_{2}^{t}/\eta_{2}}(\mathfrak{t}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{q}^{-\lambda_{2}})s_{\nu_{1}^{t}/\eta_{2}}(\mathfrak{q}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{t}^{-\lambda_{2}^{t}})\ ,

(284)

which can be decomposed into three parts: the first part is the product of the factors with the power
|\nu_{i}|
, the second part is the product of the factors with the power
|\eta_{i}|
, the third part is the product of Schur functions. Because the
\nu
’s are before the
\eta
’s in the indices of Schur functions, we first sum over
\nu
’s by the extended Cauchy identities (
12
) and (
13
). We can see that the factor
Q^{|\lambda|}
does not change before and after the equal sign, so the first part in the above formula also remains the same after summation over the
\nu
’s. Then we get some
\mathcal{R}
factors generated from the extended Cauchy identities and the indices of Schur functions now change to the form in which
\eta
’s are before
\nu
’s. Then we sum over the
\eta
’s by the extended Cauchy identities. By the same reasoning as above,
the second part of the above formula does not change and the indices of Schur functions become the form in which
\nu
’s are before
\eta
’s again. After repeating another cycle of this process, one finds that (
284
) is expressed as

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1,2}\frac{1}{\mathcal{R}_{\lambda_{2}^{t}\lambda_{1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{f}^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{R}_{\lambda_{1}^{t}\lambda_{2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{f}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{R}_{\lambda_{1}^{t}\lambda_{1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{R}_{\lambda_{2}^{t}\lambda_{2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\boldsymbol{\nu,\eta}}\left(Q_{\tau}Q_{f}^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}\right)^{|\nu_{1}|}\left(Q_{f}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}\right)^{|\nu_{2}|}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{t}}\right)^{\frac{|\eta_{1}|}{2}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{t}}\right)^{\frac{|\eta_{2}|}{2}}

\displaystyle\times s_{\nu_{1}^{t}/\eta_{1}}(Q_{\tau}\mathfrak{t}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{q}^{-\lambda_{1}})s_{\nu_{2}^{t}/\eta_{1}}(Q_{\tau}\mathfrak{q}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{t}^{-\lambda_{1}^{t}})s_{\nu_{2}^{t}/\eta_{2}}(Q_{\tau}\mathfrak{t}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{q}^{-\lambda_{2}})s_{\nu_{1}^{t}/\eta_{2}}(Q_{\tau}\mathfrak{q}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{t}^{-\lambda_{2}^{t}})\ .

(285)

Note that the arguments of Schur functions are increased by
Q_{\tau}
with additional
\mathcal{R}
functions in the front. If we call this process one full cycle, then after
m
full cycles, we have

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{2m}\frac{1}{\mathcal{R}_{\lambda_{2}^{t}\lambda_{1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{f}^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{R}_{\lambda_{1}^{t}\lambda_{2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{f}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{R}_{\lambda_{1}^{t}\lambda_{1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{R}_{\lambda_{2}^{t}\lambda_{2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\boldsymbol{\nu,\eta}}\left(Q_{\tau}Q_{f}^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}\right)^{|\nu_{1}|}\left(Q_{f}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}\right)^{|\nu_{2}|}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{t}}\right)^{\frac{|\eta_{1}|}{2}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{t}}\right)^{\frac{|\eta_{2}|}{2}}

\displaystyle\times s_{\nu_{1}^{t}/\eta_{1}}(Q_{\tau}^{m}\mathfrak{t}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{q}^{-\lambda_{1}})s_{\nu_{2}^{t}/\eta_{1}}(Q_{\tau}^{m}\mathfrak{q}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{t}^{-\lambda_{1}^{t}})s_{\nu_{2}^{t}/\eta_{2}}(Q_{\tau}^{m}\mathfrak{t}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{q}^{-\lambda_{2}})s_{\nu_{1}^{t}/\eta_{2}}(Q_{\tau}^{m}\mathfrak{q}^{-\rho}\mathfrak{t}^{-\lambda_{2}^{t}})\ .

(286)

Because
0<Q_{\tau}<1
, in the limit
m\rightarrow\infty
, only when
\nu_{1}^{t}=\nu_{2}^{t}=\eta_{1}=\eta_{2}
the contribution is nonzero.
So the above expression becomes

\displaystyle\bigg(\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\mathcal{R}_{\lambda_{2}^{t}\lambda_{1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{f}^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{R}_{\lambda_{1}^{t}\lambda_{2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{f}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{R}_{\lambda_{1}^{t}\lambda_{1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{R}_{\lambda_{2}^{t}\lambda_{2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})}\bigg)\sum_{\nu_{1}}Q_{\tau}^{|\nu_{1}|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg(\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\mathcal{R}_{\lambda_{2}^{t}\lambda_{1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}Q_{f}^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{R}_{\lambda_{1}^{t}\lambda_{2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}Q_{f}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{R}_{\lambda_{1}^{t}\lambda_{1}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{R}_{\lambda_{2}^{t}\lambda_{2}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})}\bigg)\prod_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-Q_{\tau}^{l}}\ .

(287)

Figure 31:
A general periodic strip,
y_{i}
and
z_{i}
are the exponentiated vertical positions of the corresponding branes.

More general cases of products of Schur functions whose Young diagram indices form a closed loop can also be computed via this method. Now we write only the result of the topological string partition function that corresponds to a general periodic vertical strip shown in figure
31
,

\displaystyle Z^{\text{strip}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{i}\mathfrak{q}^{\frac{||\mu_{i}||^{2}}{2}}\tilde{Z}_{\mu_{i}}(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{q})\prod_{j}\mathfrak{t}^{\frac{||\nu_{j}^{t}||^{2}}{2}}\tilde{Z}_{\nu_{j}^{t}}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{t})\bigg[\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\prod_{i<j}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\mu_{i}^{t}\mu_{j}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}\frac{y_{i}}{y_{j}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\mathcal{R}_{\mu_{j}^{t}\mu_{i}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\frac{y_{j}}{y_{i}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}})\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\mu_{i}^{t}\mu_{i}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}\right)^{-1}\prod_{i<j}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\nu_{i}^{t}\nu_{j}}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}\frac{z_{i}}{z_{j}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{t}}})\mathcal{R}_{\nu_{j}^{t}\nu_{i}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\frac{z_{j}}{z_{i}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{t}}})\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i}\left(\mathcal{R}_{\nu_{i}^{t}\nu_{i}}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{t}}})\right)^{-1}\prod_{i,j}X_{\mu_{i}^{t}\nu_{j}}(y_{i},z_{j})X_{\nu_{j}^{t}\mu_{i}}(z_{j},y_{i})\bigg]\prod_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-Q_{\tau}^{l}}\ ,

(288)

with

X_{\mu^{t}\nu}(y,z)\equiv\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{R}_{\mu^{t}\nu}(Q_{\tau}^{n-1}\frac{y}{z}),&\quad\text{if}\ y<z\\
\mathcal{R}_{\mu^{t}\nu}(Q_{\tau}^{n}\frac{y}{z}),&\quad\text{if}\ y>z\end{array}\right.\ .

(289)

Appendix C
A
-type little string theory

The
A
-type little string theory has been considered in the literature, e.g.,
Hohenegger:2013ala
.
For rank-
N

A_{M-1}
-type LST with the same set of Coulomb branch parameters in each gauge node, the web diagram description is given in figure
32
, and the partition function can be obtained by the topological vertex formalism
Hohenegger:2013ala
. Here we write their result as it is,

Figure 32:
(a) The web diagram describing
A_{M-1}
-type little string theory. (b) Its building block.

\displaystyle Z^{(M,N)}_{\text{pert}}=

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{\widehat{\eta}(Q_{U})}\prod_{i,j,k=1}^{\infty}\prod_{r,\ell=1}^{N}\Big[\frac{1-Q_{U}^{k-1}Q_{r,r+\ell}Q_{m}^{-1}\mathfrak{t}^{i-\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}^{j-\frac{1}{2}}}{1-Q_{U}^{k-1}Q_{r,r+\ell}\mathfrak{t}^{i-1}\mathfrak{q}^{j}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{1-Q_{U}^{k-1}Q_{r,r+\ell-1}Q_{m}\mathfrak{t}^{i-\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}^{j-\frac{1}{2}}}{1-Q_{U}^{k-1}Q_{r,r+\ell}\mathfrak{t}^{i}\mathfrak{q}^{j-1}}\Big]\bigg)^{M}\ ,

(290)

\displaystyle Z^{(M,N)}_{\text{inst}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha^{(i)}_{a}}\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{M}Q_{B_{i}}^{|\alpha^{(i)}|}\Big)\prod_{i=1}^{M}\prod_{a=1}^{N}\frac{\vartheta_{\alpha^{(i+1)}_{a}\alpha^{(i)}_{a}}(Q_{m})}{\vartheta_{\alpha^{(i)}_{a}\alpha^{(i)}_{a}}(\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}/\mathfrak{q}})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq N}\prod_{i=1}^{M}\frac{\vartheta_{\alpha^{(i)}_{a}\alpha^{(i+1)}_{b}}(Q_{ab}Q_{m}^{-1})\vartheta_{\alpha^{(i+1)}_{a}\alpha^{(i)}_{b}}(Q_{ab}Q_{m})}{\vartheta_{\alpha^{(i)}_{a}\alpha^{(i)}_{b}}(Q_{ab}\sqrt{\mathfrak{t}/\mathfrak{q}})\vartheta_{\alpha^{(i)}_{a}\alpha^{(i)}_{b}}(Q_{ab}\sqrt{\mathfrak{q}/\mathfrak{t}})}\ ,

(291)

where
\widehat{\eta}(Q_{U})=\prod_{k=1}^{\infty}(1-Q_{U}^{k})
and
Q_{U}=Q_{1}Q_{2}\cdots Q_{N-1}Q_{N}
,
Q_{U}
is the vertical period,

Q_{B_{i}}=Q_{b_{i}}Q_{m}
for
i=1,2,\cdots,M
,

\alpha^{(a)}
is a set of
N
Young diagrams
\{\alpha^{(a)}_{1},\cdots,\alpha^{(a)}_{N}\}
and
|\alpha^{(a)}|=\sum_{b=1}^{N}|\alpha^{(a)}_{b}|
, and
M+1\equiv 1,N+1\equiv 1
due to the horizontal and vertical periodicities.
Here,
Q_{ab}
is defined as

\displaystyle Q_{ab}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}Q_{a}Q_{a+1}\cdots Q_{b-1},&\quad\text{for }a<b\ ,\\
Q_{U}Q_{ba}^{-1},&\quad\text{for }a>b\ ,\\
1,&\quad\text{for }a=b\ .\end{array}\right.

(292)

The
\vartheta
function is defined as

\displaystyle\resizebox{20348790}{}{$\vartheta_{\mu\nu}(x|Q_{U})=\prod_{(i,j)\in\mu}\vartheta(x^{-1}\mathfrak{t}^{-\nu^{t}_{j}+i-\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}^{-\mu_{i}+j-\frac{1}{2}}|Q_{U})\prod_{(i,j)\in\nu}\vartheta(x^{-1}\mathfrak{t}^{\mu^{t}_{j}-i+\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{q}^{\nu_{i}-j+\frac{1}{2}}|Q_{U})$},

(293)

with

\vartheta(x|Q_{U})=\frac{\theta_{1}(x|Q_{U})}{(-iQ_{U}^{\frac{1}{8}})\prod_{k=1}^{\infty}(1-Q_{U}^{k})}\ ,

(294)

where
\theta_{1}
is the Jacobi theta function defined in (
33
).

The
\vartheta
function can be expressed by the
\varTheta
function defined in (
32
),

\vartheta_{\mu\nu}(x|Q_{U})=\frac{(-1)^{|\nu|}}{\big(\prod_{k=1}^{\infty}(1-Q_{U}^{k})\big)^{|\mu|+|\nu|}}\varTheta_{\mu\nu}\Big(x\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}\ \big|Q_{U}\Big)\varTheta_{\nu\mu}\Big(x^{-1}\sqrt{\tfrac{\mathfrak{t}}{\mathfrak{q}}}\ \big|Q_{U}\Big),

(295)

which makes it easy to compare (
C
) with the formula for instanton part of the partition function of
A
-type LST in section
4.3
.

Appendix D
Characters

Here, we list the characters of symmetry groups that we used in the main text.
The characters of the fundamental weights of
D_{4}
are given as follows in an orthonormal basis
\{B_{1},B_{2},B_{3},B_{4}\}
:

\displaystyle\chi_{\bf 1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{8}X_{i}^{2},

\displaystyle\chi_{\bf 2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{8}\sum_{j>i}^{8}X_{i}^{2}X_{j}^{2},

\displaystyle\chi_{\text{\bf s}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{4}\left(X_{i}+X_{i+4}\right)+\prod_{i=1}^{4}\left(X_{i}-X_{i+4}\right)\right),

\displaystyle\chi_{\text{\bf c}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{4}\left(X_{i}+X_{i+4}\right)-\prod_{i=1}^{4}\left(X_{i}-X_{i+4}\right)\right),

(296)

in which
X_{i}\in\big\{B_{1}^{1/2},\dots,B_{4}^{1/2},B_{1}^{-1/2},\dots,B_{4}^{-1/2}\big\}
.

The characters of the fundamental weights of
D_{5}
in orthonormal basis
\{B_{1},B_{2},B_{3}
,
B_{4},B_{5}\}
are given as follows:

\displaystyle\chi_{\bf 1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{10}X_{i}^{2},

\displaystyle\chi_{\bf 2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{10}\sum_{j>i}^{10}X_{i}^{2}X_{j}^{2},

\displaystyle\chi_{\bf 3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{10}\sum_{j>i}^{10}\sum_{k>j}^{10}X_{i}^{2}X_{j}^{2}X_{k}^{2},

\displaystyle\chi_{\text{\bf s}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{5}\left(X_{i}+X_{i+5}\right)+\prod_{i=1}^{5}\left(X_{i}-X_{i+5}\right)\right),

\displaystyle\chi_{\text{\bf c}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{5}\left(X_{i}+X_{i+5}\right)-\prod_{i=1}^{5}\left(X_{i}-X_{i+5}\right)\right),

(297)

in which
X_{i}\in\big\{B_{1}^{1/2},\dots,B_{5}^{1/2},B_{1}^{-1/2},\dots,B_{5}^{-1/2}\big\}
.

The characters of the fundamental weights of
E_{6}

can be obtained from the embedding
E_{6}\supset\text{SO}(10)\times\text{U}(1)
in (
260
). The Dynkin diagram and the assignment of the fundamental weights are given as
E_{6}

1
2
3
4
5
6
.

The characters
\chi_{i}^{E_{6}}
of the fundamental weights associated with the node
i
of the Dynkin diagram are given as follows,

\displaystyle\chi_{\bf 1}^{E_{6}}=B_{6}^{-\frac{2}{3}}+\chi_{\bf s}^{D_{5}}B_{6}^{-\frac{1}{6}}+\chi_{\bf 1}^{D_{5}}B_{6}^{\frac{1}{3}},

\displaystyle\chi_{\bf 2}^{E_{6}}=\chi_{\bf s}^{D_{5}}B_{6}^{-\frac{5}{6}}+(\chi_{\bf 1}^{D_{5}}+\chi_{\bf 3}^{D_{5}})B_{6}^{-\frac{1}{3}}+\chi_{\bf 1}^{D_{5}}\chi_{\bf s}^{D_{5}}B_{6}^{\frac{1}{6}}+\chi_{\bf 2}^{D_{5}}B_{6}^{\frac{2}{3}},

\displaystyle\chi_{\bf 3}^{E_{6}}=\chi_{\bf 3}^{D_{5}}B_{6}^{-1}+\chi_{\bf 2}^{D_{5}}\chi_{\bf c}^{D_{5}}B_{6}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\chi_{\bf 2}^{D_{5}}+\chi_{\bf 1}^{D_{5}}\chi_{\bf 3}^{D_{5}}+\chi_{\bf 2}^{D_{5}}\chi_{\bf s}^{D_{5}}B_{6}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\chi_{\bf 3}^{D_{5}}B_{6},

\displaystyle\chi_{\bf 4}^{E_{6}}=\chi_{\bf 2}^{D_{5}}B_{6}^{-\frac{2}{3}}+\chi_{\bf 1}^{D_{5}}\chi_{\bf c}^{D_{5}}B_{6}^{-\frac{1}{6}}+(\chi_{\bf 1}^{D_{5}}+\chi_{\bf 3}^{D_{5}})B_{6}^{\frac{1}{3}}+\chi_{\bf c}^{D_{5}}B_{6}^{\frac{5}{6}},

\displaystyle\chi_{\bf 5}^{E_{6}}=\chi_{\bf 1}^{D_{5}}B_{6}^{-\frac{1}{3}}+\chi_{\bf c}^{D_{5}}B_{6}^{\frac{1}{6}}+B_{6}^{\frac{2}{3}},

\displaystyle\chi_{\bf{6}}^{E_{6}}=\chi_{\bf c}^{D_{5}}B_{6}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+1+\chi_{\bf 2}^{D_{5}}+\chi_{\bf s}^{D_{5}}B_{6}^{\frac{1}{2}},

(298)

where the
\chi^{D_{5}}
’s are the characters of the fundamental weights of
D_{5}
given in (
D
).
In a similar fashion, one can obtain the characters for the fundamental weights of
E_{7}
and
E_{8}
groups.
